# Multi-workspace (design)

**Status: design, built in phases; phase 2's data path is complete since 7.14.00.** This document describes how one deployment of the platform will serve
several separate teams or customers, each in its own **workspace**. Only what the table below lists exists in the code;
everything else is planned, and each release's CHANGELOG entry says what landed and updates the table. Routes, tables
and files named here are the planned ones unless the table lists them.

| Since | Built |
|---|---|
| 6.05.00 | The Cognito group `platform-admins`, with the bootstrapped `admin` in it (through a CloudFormation attachment, replaced in 6.06.02). Nothing reads it yet |
| 6.06.00 | The `voc-workspaces` table in `VocCoreStack` (keys below, TTL `ttl` for invites). Nothing reads or writes it yet |
| 6.06.02 | The bootstrap adds the first `admin` to `platform-admins` when it creates the user (no CloudFormation attachment); an older deployment adds one by hand (CHANGELOG 6.06.02, Upgrade notes) |
| 6.07.00 | The workspace middleware `lambda/shared/workspace.py`: the `X-Workspace-Id` header (missing 400; malformed, unknown, `deleting` or not a member: one 404), the role from the `MEMBER#` row, a platform admin who is not a member admin entering as admin (decision 2) with an audit row `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` / `{iso}#{random}` (once per fill of the 30 s warm-container cache), `ws_key` and "my workspaces". The workspaces API `voc-workspaces-api` (`lambda/api/workspaces_handler.py`, `GET /me/{proxy+}`): `GET /me/workspaces` and `GET /me/workspaces/current`, with Query and GetItem on `voc-workspaces` and PutItem only where the partition key matches `AUDIT#WS#*`. The audit-row guard (`voc-datalake/lib/utils/audit-row-guard.ts`) covers `voc-workspaces`, with this role as its audit writer: no role can change or remove an entry row. Nothing creates workspaces or members yet, so every list is empty; the web app does not call these routes yet, and `X-Workspace-Id` is not yet in the CORS preflight (both since 6.12.00). This is the first reader of `platform-admins`: the platform-admin safeguards below (Control plane) land before anything can create a workspace |
| 6.08.00 | Platform-admin membership in the users API (`lambda/api/users_handler.py`, rules in `lambda/shared/user_admin_guard.py`). `PUT /users/{username}/platform-admin` with `{"platform_admin": true\|false}`: platform admins only (403 for everyone else, plain `admins` included; in the route-gate inventory as admin-only and platform-admin-only), a grant only to a member of `admins` (400 otherwise), a revoke refused (409) when it would leave no enabled platform admin, the caller's own included; asking for what already holds changes and records nothing. Demoting a user to `users` also removes them from `platform-admins`. Demoting, disabling, enabling, deleting or resetting the password of a platform admin answers 403 unless the caller is one too (the caller's membership is checked live in Cognito, not only in the token, with an enabled account, so a revoke or a disable takes effect in the users API at once; entering a workspace still reads the token, and the change that creates workspaces adds the live check there); demoting, disabling or deleting the last enabled one answers 409 (the last-`admins` guard is unchanged). Every grant and revoke (the route, a demotion, a delete) writes an `AUDIT#platform-admins` / `{iso}#{random}` row in `voc-aggregates` (kind, via, the target's and the actor's sub, the time) before the change, so it records the attempt (a change that then fails leaves the row; the group's live state is Cognito's): a row that cannot be written refuses the change; the audit-row guard names the users API's role that partition's only writer. `GET /users` reports `platform_admin` per user. The web app calls the new route since 6.13.00 (Administration → Privacy & access → Users, for platform admins; since 7.54.00 a person's editor on Administration → People). Like the last-admin check, these checks read before they write |
| 6.09.00 | Creating and listing workspaces, for platform admins only, in the workspaces API under `/platform/{proxy+}` (GET and POST, Cognito; logic in `lambda/shared/platform_workspaces.py`). Each route calls `require_admin` and `require_platform_admin`, then the live check, now shared by the users API and the workspaces API (`lambda/shared/platform_admin.py`: the token's group, the live Cognito membership and an enabled account; 403 otherwise). `POST /platform/workspaces` with `{name, slug}` (name 1 to 80 characters, trimmed; slug 3 to 40 of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`, not starting or ending with `-`, not `admin`, `api`, `app`, `platform` or `www`; 400 otherwise) writes, in ONE transaction of conditional puts, `SLUG#{slug}` / `META` (409 when the slug is taken, nothing written), `WS#{ws}` / `META`, the creator's `MEMBER#` row (role `admin`), their `USER#` row, a `WORKSPACES` listing row and an `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` row (kind `workspace_created`); the id is `ws_` plus a new ULID. It answers 201 `{workspace: {id, name, slug, role: 'admin', secret_status}}`. The transaction's idempotency token covers the SDK's own retries; a client that retries after a lost answer gets 409 for the slug its first attempt took, and `GET /platform/workspaces` shows whether that workspace was made. Then it creates the workspace's credentials secret (Credentials, below); `secret_status` is `ready`, or `pending` when the secret could not be made (logged; the workspace stays). `GET /platform/workspaces` lists every workspace `{workspaces: [{id, name, slug, status, created_at, secret_status}], next_cursor}` by name (`?limit=` 1 to 100, default 25, `?cursor=`), one Query of the `WORKSPACES` partition. Entering a workspace as a platform admin (`GET /me/workspaces/current`, `lambda/shared/workspace.py`) now needs the live check too, on each fill of the 30 s cache that would elevate: a failed one enters with the member's own role, or answers the 404 for a non-member. The role's PutItem reaches `AUDIT#WS#*`, `SLUG#*`, `USER#*`, `WORKSPACES` and `WS#*`, its UpdateItem `WS#*` and `WORKSPACES` only (`secret_status` on `META` and the listing row, in one transaction); it reads the caller's groups and account in Cognito, and creates, describes and tags secrets under `voc/ws/*` only. The web app calls the `/platform` routes since 6.13.00 (the platform console); members are added since 6.10.00 |
| 6.10.00 | A workspace's members, in the workspaces API under `/me/workspaces/current/members` (GET, POST, PUT and DELETE on the existing `/me/{proxy+}`; logic in `lambda/shared/workspace_members.py`), each acting in the workspace `X-Workspace-Id` names as `resolve_workspace` decides (400 without the header, the one 404 for a non-member, the deployment's `admins` group included; a platform admin enters as admin, live-checked and audited). `GET` lists the members `{members: [{sub, role, email, name, invited_by, joined_at}], next_cursor}` for any member, by sub (`?limit=` 1 to 100, default 25, `?cursor=`; one Query of the `MEMBER#` rows; email and name are the row's, and for up to 5 rows a page that hold none, such as the creator's, Cognito's by sub). `POST {email, role}` (admin, editor or viewer): workspace admins only (`require_workspace_admin`: 403 for an editor or a viewer); an email with a Cognito account is a member at once, a new email first gets an account (`AdminCreateUser`, the temporary password emailed, as the users API does) when the caller is in the deployment's `admins` group (platform admins are; 403 for any other workspace admin: until the data path is keyed by workspace, phase 2, every account reads the whole deployment, so creating one stays with the users API's administrators), then 201 `{member: {sub, role, email, name, invited_by, joined_at, account: 'existing'\|'created'}}`; 409 for a member already there, for two accounts sharing the email, or for an email Cognito holds for another account. An existing account gets no notification email: the deployment has no mail path for account notices, so the add is logged. No `INVITE#` row is written: membership is immediate either way, so there is nothing to accept. `PUT /{sub}` `{role}`: workspace admins only (asking for the role a member has changes and records nothing). `DELETE /{sub}`: workspace admins, or the member themselves (leave). Demoting or removing the last member admin, the caller included, is refused (409). Each add, role change and removal writes an `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` / `{iso}#{random}` row (kind `member_added`, `member_role_changed`, `member_removed` or `member_left`; actor, target sub, role before and after, `platform_admin`, and on an add `account`) before the change, so it records the attempt (a row that cannot be written refuses the change); then ONE transaction changes the `MEMBER#` row and the `USER#` row together (an add: two conditional puts and a check that `META` is still `active`; a role change: two updates, each only of `role` and only on existing rows; a removal: two deletes), and a demotion or removal of an admin also checks that another member is still an admin, so two admins demoting each other at once cannot both succeed. Each write resolves the caller's role from the table, not the 30 s cache, so an admin demoted or removed a moment ago cannot undo it; the change drops the member's cached membership in the container that made it, and other warm containers serve the old role to reads for up to 30 s. The role's new grants: UpdateItem on `WS#*` and `USER#*` with the attribute `role` only; DeleteItem on `WS#*` and `USER#*` (`dynamodb:LeadingKeys`; IAM cannot pin a sort key, so `META` in `WS#*` is in reach of IAM and kept out by the code, while `SLUG#`, `WORKSPACES` and `AUDIT#` are not); ConditionCheckItem on `WS#*`; Cognito `ListUsers` and `AdminCreateUser` on the user pool (IAM cannot limit ListUsers to an email filter; the code sends only an email or a sub). The web app calls these routes since 6.13.00 (the members page) |
| 6.11.00 | A user's delete and an erasure by email reach the memberships. `DELETE /users/{username}` (the users API) removes every membership of the user BEFORE `AdminDeleteUser` (`plan_user_removal` and `remove_user_memberships` in `lambda/shared/workspace_members.py`): it reads their `USER#{sub}` rows, and per workspace the `MEMBER#` role and, for an admin, another admin; the last member admin of a workspace is refused (409, naming the workspace: make another member an admin there first) unless the caller is a platform admin (live-checked), in which case the workspace keeps no member admin until a platform admin enters it and names one (Invites). Every refusal comes before any write; then, per workspace, an `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` row (kind `member_removed`, `via: user_deleted`, actor, target, role before, `platform_admin`, `left_without_admin`) and ONE transaction deleting the `MEMBER#` and `USER#` rows (the `MEMBER#` delete conditional on the role read; for an admin a check that the other admin still is one: 409 when either changed meanwhile); only then the Cognito account. The users API's role gains, on `voc-workspaces`: Query on `USER#*` and `WS#*`, GetItem on `WS#*` and `AUDIT#WS#*` (an audit row read back after a retried put), ConditionCheckItem on `WS#*`, PutItem on `AUDIT#WS#*` and DeleteItem on `USER#*` and `WS#*` (`dynamodb:LeadingKeys`; the code deletes `MEMBER#` and `USER#` keys only); the audit-row guard names it a writer of `AUDIT#WS#*` there and of no other audit partition. An erasure by email without a source (`voc-retention`, docs/source-policies.md) removes the `email` and `name` of every `MEMBER#` row that holds the email, in any case, and keeps the row; each row first gets a tombstone `AUDIT#tombstone#workspace_members` / `{ws}#{sub}#{job}` (the workspace, the run, the legal basis; never the email), counted as `member_rows_deidentified`. It finds the rows with a paged Scan of `voc-workspaces` (no email index; Invites says why and how it is bounded); `voc-retention` gains Scan and UpdateItem pinned to `WS#*` and the attributes `email` and `name` there |
| 6.12.00 | The web app's side of Request flow steps 1 and 2. `X-Workspace-Id` is in the API's CORS: the gateway's preflight and error responses (`voc-datalake/lib/stacks/api-gateway.ts`) and the Lambdas' own CORS headers. After sign-in, the workspace gate (`frontend/src/workspace/WorkspaceGate.tsx`, inside `ProtectedRoute`, around the layout) reads `GET /me/workspaces`: an address `/workspace/{slug}/…` whose slug is one of the caller's enters it (the current workspace, `frontend/src/workspace/currentWorkspace.ts`, is set before any page renders, then `GET /me/workspaces/current` gives the role the server applies; its 404 shows the not-found page); a slug that is not theirs, or one the platform could not have made, shows "Workspace not found" with the caller's own workspaces as links (the same page whether it exists); no slug and one workspace moves into it with a full page load, keeping the path; no slug and two or more shows a picker (name, role; the slug last entered in the browser and confirmed by `/current`, `localStorage` `voc-last-workspace`, is highlighted, never chosen automatically). On an address without a slug, no workspace, or a list that cannot be read (logged as a warning), opens the app as before, with no header and no picker; on a `/workspace/…` address a list that cannot be read shows "Could not load your workspaces" with Try again and Sign out, never the app outside the workspace (a list read once keeps counting when a later refresh fails). Sign-out clears the current workspace and loads the plain `/login` (a full page load, out of the workspace's address), the gate clears it too when it unmounts, and the expired-session redirect keeps the `/workspace/{slug}` base path. After a 404 from `/current` (a workspace being deleted stays listed, as can a membership removed a moment ago) the gate leaves that workspace out of the list for the rest of the tab (`sessionStorage` `voc-refused-workspaces`, and the cached list): the not-found page does not offer it and says it is not available, and neither the one-workspace redirect nor a full page load of `/` moves back into it. The router takes `/workspace/{slug}` as its `basename` at load (`frontend/src/workspace/workspacePath.ts`, the slug rule of `lambda/shared/platform_workspaces.py`), so every route and link works unchanged inside a workspace; the SPA distribution's 404 fallback already serves `index.html` there. Inside one, every API call to a trusted origin carries `X-Workspace-Id` (`getAuthHeaders` in `frontend/src/api/baseUrl.ts`, which the REST client and the assistant's stream use, and the feedback boards' transport), and the header shows the workspace with, for two or more, a menu that switches with a full page load to `/workspace/{slug}/dashboard`. The data path still ignores the header (phase 2). The dev mock serves both routes, its caller in no workspace until its dev switch `PUT /__mock/workspaces` turns on two (`frontend/mock-workspaces.js`); `frontend/e2e/tests/workspaces.spec.ts` runs against it. The web app's browser-saved state was not keyed by workspace yet (since 6.15.00 it is: the row below) |
| 6.13.00 | The web app's members page and platform console, on the routes of 6.08.00 to 6.10.00 (no API change). Inside a workspace, `/workspace/{slug}/members` (`frontend/src/pages/Members/`), reached from Members in the header's workspace menu (a menu with one workspace too); outside one the address is the app's "Page not found". Every member reads the list (`GET /me/workspaces/current/members`, a page at a time: name or email, role, joined); the role the server applies there (`/current`) decides what the page offers: a workspace admin adds by email with a role, changes a role and removes a member (confirmed); any member who has a membership row leaves (confirmed, then a full page load to `/`). Each refusal is said in words by its status and the server's reason (`frontend/src/workspace/workspaceRefusals.ts`, held to the Python constants by `workspaceRefusals.lockstep.test.ts`): the last admin, a membership changed meanwhile, an admin demoted meanwhile, a person already a member, a new email from a workspace admin outside `admins`. The platform console is Administration → Privacy & access → Workspaces (since 7.54.00: Administration → Platform, `/admin/platform`; `frontend/src/pages/Settings/PlatformWorkspacesSection.tsx`), shown when the session's token holds `platform-admins` (`useIsPlatformAdmin`, `frontend/src/store/authStore.ts`; the API's live check decides): every workspace by name (`GET /platform/workspaces`, a page at a time: name, slug, status, secret pending or ready), create with name and slug checked by the server's rules first (`POST /platform/workspaces`; 409 for a taken slug), and Enter, a full page load to `/workspace/{slug}/`. A platform admin entering a workspace they are not a member of: the console records the workspace (id and slug; the name comes from the server's answer) in the tab's `sessionStorage` (`voc-platform-entries`, `frontend/src/workspace/platformEntry.ts`) before the load, and on a `/workspace/{slug}` address outside the caller's list the gate asks `/current` with that id and opens nothing until it answers that workspace with that slug (decision 2: the server's live check and audited entry); its 404 shows the same not-found page as any other slug and drops the record, so the record lets no one else in and confirms nothing. Sign-out clears the records; an expired session keeps them (signing back in returns to the workspace's address). Under Administration → Privacy & access → Users (since 7.54.00: a person's editor on Administration → People), platform admins grant and revoke platform admin per user (`PUT /users/{username}/platform-admin`), with its refusals said (the last enabled platform admin, a grant to a non-administrator). The dev mock serves all of these routes on its switch's state (`frontend/mock-workspaces.js`, `mock-server.js`); `frontend/e2e/tests/workspace-admin.spec.ts` runs against it. Still not keyed by workspace: everything the data path holds (phase 2), so the pages say that workspaces do not separate data yet |
| 6.14.00 | Phase 1's guards and the repository layer phase 2 moves the data path onto (Request flow step 4; no route or stack change, and the data path is still not keyed by workspace). The layer, `lambda/shared/ws_repository.py`: `ws_table(ws, env_var)` opens one of the tables in its registry (`TABLES`: voc-feedback, voc-aggregates, voc-projects, voc-jobs, voc-conversations, voc-memory, voc-agents, voc-radar, voc-participants and voc-feature-requests, by their environment variables, each with its partition attribute `pk` and its indexes' partition attributes, held equal to each table's definition in `lib/stacks/core-stack.ts`, both ways, by a test; any other table is refused). Before an AWS call it refuses a key whose partition attribute is not `WS#{ws}#…` with a part after the workspace (so not the control-plane partition `WS#{ws}` itself), a put whose index partition attributes are not, and an update whose `UpdateExpression` names a key or index partition attribute, as a plain word or as a `#placeholder` it uses (a condition such as `attribute_exists(#pk)` passes; index keys are set with a put); a table handle's state cannot be changed once built; each call takes only the parameters listed for it, so the legacy `AttributeUpdates`, `Expected`, `KeyConditions`, `QueryFilter` and `AttributesToGet` are refused. It has no transactions and no batch reads or writes yet: phase 2 adds scoped ones before it moves a call site that needs them. `get_item`, `put_item`, `update_item` and `delete_item` take and answer boto3's own forms (`Key=`, `Item=`), so those call sites move by swapping the handle (their key values becoming `WS#{ws}#…`, phase 2's re-keying), unless one uses a legacy parameter or its update sets a key or index attribute (which becomes a put); `query` names its partition (and an index by name) and refuses a caller's `KeyConditionExpression`, so each query call site is rewritten when it moves; there is no Scan. `ws_s3_key(ws, …)` builds `ws/{ws}/…` keys from one path segment per part (no empty part, `.`, `..`, separator, C0 or C1 control character, DEL, U+2028 or U+2029); `ws_key` is `shared/workspace.py`'s. Nothing in the data path uses it yet. The raw-access static test, the gate step `audit: ws_raw_access` (`scripts/audit/ws_raw_access.py`): in the Lambdas' Python every raw table handle (`Table(…)`, `.client`/`.resource('dynamodb')` on any receiver, `get_dynamodb_resource()`, any `.meta.client`, any reference to a `shared/tables.py` getter through any import form, the layer's private handle by attribute or by name, and any private attribute, `vars()` or `__dict__` of a layer handle) and the S3 writes, copies and deletes it can see statically (client calls such as `put_object`, `upload_file`, `copy_object`, `copy`, `delete_object(s)`, `put_object_tagging`, a presigned PUT, POST or DELETE, `Object`/`Bucket` methods, and collection deletes such as `bucket.objects.filter(…).delete()`; its docstring lists what it misses, such as an object a collection yields), and in the stream and plan-step Lambdas the DynamoDB and S3 clients built, DynamoDB commands (PartiQL included) and S3 writes, copies and deletes, its SDK names followed through import and require aliases, outside the layer, counted per file against `scripts/audit/allowlists/ws_raw_access.json`, a ratchet: a new file or a count that grows fails, and a count that drops fails until its entry is lowered. That allowlist, grouped by the phase that moves each file (phase 2's data path, phase 3's settings, projects, assistant, MCP and boards, phase 4's retention) or the reason it stays raw (the control plane, the handle plumbing, a deploy-time custom resource), is the phase 2 to 4 work list. The cross-workspace suite, `test_cross_workspace.py` in `lambda/api/test/` (replaced in 6.18.00 by `lambda/api/test/test_cross_workspace_routes.py`, which keeps these checks: the row below): every route in its registry (today `/me/workspaces/current` and the members routes) called by a member of workspace A naming workspace B, and by callers who are in neither, answers the one 404 with the same body as an id that names no workspace, with no Cognito call and nothing written; a member of A naming B's member on `PUT` or `DELETE` inside A gets the members' 404, as for a sub that is no one's; and a route anywhere in `lambda/` (on any router) that resolves the workspace, directly or through helpers in any module, or is served under `/me/workspaces/current`, but is missing from the registry fails it. The two-workspace check on the dev mock, `frontend/e2e/tests/cross-workspace.spec.ts`, with the mock switch's `member: 'acme'` (the caller a member of acme only, and no platform admin to these routes): `/workspace/juniper/members` shows "Workspace not found" |
| 6.15.00 | The browser-saved state of the web app's three persisted stores, keyed by workspace (no API change; the data path is still not keyed). Inside a workspace the assistant's UI record (`voc-assistant-ui`: the conversation in view, the panel mode and toggles, and the per-tab open state in `sessionStorage`), the manual-import draft (`voc-manual-import`) and the config store (`voc-config`: the dashboard's time range, custom days and date basis, and the browser's config) are saved as `<name>:w/{slug}` (`frontend/src/workspace/workspaceStorage.ts`, the persist `name` of each store), so what one workspace saved never opens in another and is back on return; outside a workspace, and on a `/workspace/…` address no workspace can have (the gate's not-found, which opens no page), the names are unchanged, so a deployment without workspaces sees no change. The key is the address's slug, not the id: the stores hydrate when their modules load, before the gate has read the list, and a slug is unique and fixed (decision 15); a switch is a full page load, so a page load's stores belong to one workspace. The assistant's `pendingDraft` was never saved: it lives in memory, and a switch is a full page load. Nothing is migrated: the first visit to a workspace starts from the defaults, and the deployment-wide records stay for use outside workspaces. Sign-out and an expired session remove every workspace's record of the three stores from `localStorage` and `sessionStorage` (`forgetWorkspaceStores`), and the assistant's plain record with them; the plain draft and filters are kept, as before, and Account → "Reset local settings" clears the page's config and removes every other `voc-config` record, the plain one and each workspace's (`forgetOtherRecords`). The e2e contexts drop a workspace's own UI records with the plain ones (`frontend/e2e/lib/context.ts`). The header's workspace menu leaves out the workspaces refused in this tab (`refusedWorkspaces.ts` `withoutRefused`, the gate's own filter). The members page shows the loading state until `GET /me/workspaces/current` answers (one cache entry with the gate's, `useServerWorkspace`, not retried: both act on the first answer), then offers what the role the server applies allows, so a platform admin who is also an editor or a viewer is not shown the read-only page first; a failure of that read other than its 404 keeps the membership's role, as the gate does |
| 6.16.00 | Another tab's sign-out ends this tab's hold on the per-workspace records (no API change). The signal is an explicit one, written only where a session really ends: the sign-out (`useSignOut`) and an expired session (`endExpiredSession`) set `localStorage` `voc-session-ended` to a fresh value (`announceSessionEnded`), and the browser's `storage` event tells every other tab of the origin; no channel, polling or dependency (there was no cross-tab signal before: each tab learned of it at its next 401). Not the session's own record (`voc-auth`): `authStore.logout()` also writes it signed out when a refresh fails for a moment and the retry signs the tab back in, which must not end every other tab. On the signal (from `localStorage` only), a tab removes every workspace's record of the three stores and the assistant's plain record (`forgetWorkspaceStores`), its refused list and a platform admin's console entries (`forgetPlatformEntries`, as its own sign-out does; only its own expired session keeps them), and, when it is signed in, leaves with a full page load (`location.replace`) to the plain `/login`, as a sign-out does (since 6.17.00 a public page reloads where it is: the row below) (`frontend/src/services/crossTabSignOut.ts`, whose listener is installed when the module loads: `main.tsx` imports it, so every tab has it at startup (a test reads that import)). The full load, not an in-memory reset, is what stops its persisted stores from writing the last person's state back: it is how the app already ends a session, it drops every cache and in-flight request with them, and it needs no change to the stores. A tab already signed out stays on its page. It writes nothing to `voc-auth`, and forgets the records only while the stored session is still signed out, so a late event cannot sign out, or clear the records of, a person who signed in meanwhile in another tab |
| 6.17.00 | Two follow-ups to 6.16.00 (no API change). A tab showing a public page, one of the top-level routes outside `ProtectedRoute` (`/login`, `/vote/…`, `/board…`, `/review/…`, `/unsubscribe`), forgets the records on the signal as every tab does but, when signed in, reloads where it is (`location.reload()`) instead of going to `/login`: those pages open without a session of the app, and the reload still drops the page load's copy of the session, which `/board` and `/review` use when there is one. It decides from the address as the router sees it (`appPathname`, without the page load's `/workspace/{slug}`), against one list of the routes' first segments (`frontend/src/publicRoutes.ts`, held to `routes.tsx` both ways by `routes.publicBallot.test.tsx`). The order the signal depends on is pinned: the sign-out and an expired session write `voc-auth` signed out (`authService.signOut()`) before `announceSessionEnded()`, since a tab that hears the signal while the stored session still says signed in skips the wipe; `frontend/src/services/sessionEndOrder.test.ts` checks on both paths that the callers sign out (`authService.signOut()`, stood in for by the store's `logout()`) before they announce |
| 6.18.00 | The voc-multiplayer-c track merged (no data-path change: the data path is still not keyed by workspace). The cross-workspace suite, `lambda/api/test/test_cross_workspace_routes.py` (with `cross_workspace_worlds.py` and `cross_workspace_pending.py`; Phases below), replaces 6.14.00's `test_cross_workspace.py` and keeps its checks: every route of the API is named by file and route in exactly one table, workspace-scoped (today `GET /me/workspaces/current`, the four members routes, the two plugin routes, the two resolved-problems routes and the two brand routes), outside a workspace by design, or pending (phase 2 or 3); a route anywhere in `lambda/` (on any router) that reaches `resolve_workspace` through helpers in any module must be workspace-scoped. The scope of every settings row, per workspace or for the deployment, held to the code (`lambda/shared/test/test_settings_scopes.py`, Per-workspace configuration below). The assistant's reads carry the caller's `X-Workspace-Id` to the domain Lambdas, and plan steps carry the workspace their plan was approved in: `workspace_id` on the plan's META, `voc:workspace` in each step's claims, sent as the domain calls' `X-Workspace-Id` header (Request flow below). A workspace's plugins: `GET` and `PUT /me/workspaces/current/plugins[/{plugin_id}]` in the workspaces API, the `PLUGIN#{plugin_id}` / `WS#{ws}` rows the ingestors' fan-out reads, within the deployment's ceiling (Per-workspace configuration below); enabling is refused (409) and writes nothing until phase 2's data path is in. The first settings kinds under their workspace: a team's resolved problems (`GET` and `PUT /settings/resolved-problems` with `X-Workspace-Id` read and write `WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#resolved_problems`; a viewer reads, an editor or admin marks) and its brand (`GET` and `PUT /settings/brand`, `WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#brand`: any member reads, the workspace's admins change it, `require_settings_admin`; the deployment's `admins` group grants nothing there; the engagement API's emails and boards still read the deployment's brand name until they move); without the header, the deployment's rows and rules as before. In the web app the brand form (Administration → General, behind `AdminRoute`; since 7.54.00 Administration → Brand & appearance) is reachable only by the deployment's `admins` for now, and inside a workspace it is editable only by those who are also that workspace's admins (`frontend/src/pages/Settings/useBrandEditRight.ts`); opening it to every workspace admin was the next web-app slice (since 7.02.00 they edit it on the workspace's settings page: the row below). The deployment-wide brand is edited outside a workspace, so it is unreachable from the web app for anyone who has a workspace (the gate always enters one, `frontend/src/workspace/gateDecision.ts`); the platform console gained a "deployment default" editor in the next web-app slice (7.02.00, the row below). Both go through the domain APIs' shared resolution (`lambda/shared/request_workspace.py`) and the stack's one workspace-resolution managed policy, which the audit-row guard now guards like a role. The deployment-wide settings' writes need the deployment's `admins` and a live platform admin (`require_admin`, `require_platform_admin` and `lambda/shared/platform_admin_gate.py`'s live check): `PUT /settings/appearance` and the alert writes (Per-workspace configuration below; in the route-gate inventory as admin-only and platform-admin-only) |

The guard (`lambda/shared/test/test_ws_raw_access.py`) counts, per production module and per kind, the raw table and bucket calls (`.Table(`, `.put_object(`, `.copy_object(`, `.upload_file(`, `.upload_fileobj(`, `.generate_presigned_url(`, `.generate_presigned_post(`, `.delete_object(`, `.delete_objects(`, `get_feedback_table()`, `get_aggregates_table()`, `cached_table(`, aliases included; ported from 6.14.00's audit: `.copy_from(`, `.put_object_tagging(`, `.create_multipart_upload(`, a managed `.copy(CopySource=…)`, a resource collection's batch `delete()`, `put` or `delete` on an `Object(…)` or `Bucket(…)` made in the same expression, any `.meta.client`, any reference to those getters, called or not, and the layer's private handle `_unscoped_table`) and the strings that start an unscoped data-path key (`SOURCE#`, `METRIC#`, `DATE#`, `CATEGORY#`, `URGENCY#`, `CHANNEL#`, `SOURCE_RUN#`, `raw/`, `silver/`); its allowlist names each module not moved yet with the phase that moves it (or phase 0: it stays raw by design, the control plane and the handle plumbing) and must match exactly: a new raw access fails unless its entry is raised in the same change, which review refuses. The scheduled tick (an EventBridge `Scheduled Event`) first fans out, at most once per tick (`fan_out_once`), then runs the deployment-wide run as before (fanning out first means a deployment-wide run that raises or times out never keeps the workspaces from their tick). The fan-out claims the tick in `voc-idempotency`, `ingestor#fanout#{plugin}#{EventBridge event id}` (`lambda/shared/idempotency.py` `dedupe_claim_item`, the same conditional put and two-day TTL the aggregator uses), so Lambda's asynchronous retries of a tick whose deployment-wide run raised find the claim and fan out no second time; it then reads the workspaces that enabled the plugin, `workspaces_with_plugin(table, plugin_id)` in `lambda/shared/workspace_plugins.py` (one paged, consistent Query of `voc-workspaces` partition `PLUGIN#{plugin_id}`, sort keys `WS#{ws}`, well-formed ids only), and invokes its own function asynchronously once per workspace with `{"workspace_id": ws}` only (`plugins/_shared/self_invoke.py`, shared with the app-review continuation parts). A claim that cannot be made (no table, no event id, a DynamoDB error) or a list read that fails is logged and counted (`IngestFanOutFailures`) and no workspace runs on that tick; a failed invoke is counted the same way and the other workspaces still run; the next tick tries again, each workspace catching up from its own watermarks. None of it changes the deployment-wide run's outcome or Lambda's retries of it. Nothing writes `PLUGIN#` rows yet (the per-workspace plugin settings will), so every tick fans out to no workspace. A continuation part of a run in a workspace carries its `workspace_id`. What a run in a workspace keeps is the workspace's: watermarks `WS#{ws}#{plugin}#{key}`, run rows `WS#{ws}#SOURCE_RUN#{plugin}` (and the web scraper's `WS#{ws}#SCRAPER_RUN#{id}`), the S3 import's history rows `WS#{ws}#IMPORT_RUN` and `WS#{ws}#IMPORT_RUN#{run_id}`, the circuit breaker's `WS#{ws}#FAILURES#{plugin}` and `WS#{ws}#CIRCUIT#{plugin}` (its trip stops that workspace's runs and disables no rule, which serves every workspace; a deployment-wide trip still disables the plugin's one rule, as `PUT /sources/{source}/disable` does, and so stops every workspace's scheduled runs too until the source is enabled again), raw objects `ws/{ws}/raw/{source}/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/{stem}/{sha16}.json` and the S3 import's original file there too (`lambda/shared/raw_versions.py` `item_folder` with a scope; `parse_item_key` reads both roots), every table opened through the layer (`lambda/shared/ingest_tables.py`: `ws_table` in a workspace, the table itself deployment-wide). | 7.03.00 | The voc-multiplayer-b track's retention slice merged. An erasure and a retention window reach a workspace's data (TENETS.md D5; docs/source-policies.md, "Workspace items"). The scope rule: an erasure job stays the deployment's (`POST /settings/erasure`, a deployment admin, `voc-retention`), and an erasure by `email` (with or without a source) reaches every workspace's matching items (`WS#{ws}#SOURCE#…` / `FEEDBACK#…`) in the same item Scan as the deployment's (`lambda/shared/retention.py` `reaches_workspaces`): a person's data is erased wherever it is, and Administration's erasure form and confirm dialog say so. An erasure by `author`, `source_id` or `csv_row_id` stays the deployment's: an author is a display name, not unique even within one source, and an id is unique only within one source account or upload while each workspace has its own (the deployment's ticket 12345 is not a workspace's); they reach workspaces when a job can name its workspace (until then an author erasure does not reach a workspace's copies: erase by email). A retention window reaches them too: a workspace's messages are processed under the deployment's source profiles (PII and raw-copy policy; `plugins/_shared/raw_original.py` keeps no whole-file original for a source with a window, because the window deletes item by item), so the profile's window binds their items until per-workspace profiles replace the deployment's for workspaces (phase 3). A whole-source erasure (`field: source`) does not: it is a request about the deployment's own source. Each workspace item is tombstoned before it is deleted, in the same partition as the deployment's, `AUDIT#tombstone#<source>`, with `sk` `{ws}#{feedback_id}` and `workspace_id` on the row (`lambda/jobs/retention/copies.py` `tombstone_key`): the audit-row guard (`lib/utils/audit-row-guard.ts`) pins `AUDIT#*` leading keys, so a `WS#{ws}#AUDIT#…` partition would be outside it, and the workspace in the sort key keeps two workspaces' copies of one source item (one `feedback_id`) and the deployment's apart. Then the item goes with every copy under its own workspace (`retention.delete_item`, the scope from its `pk`, `ws_repo.partition_scope`): every Silver version under `ws/{ws}/silver/{source}/{feedback_id}/` and every version of its raw objects under `ws/{ws}/raw/` (its `s3_raw_uri` is followed only under its own workspace's bronze root, `raw_versions.raw_root`, as a deployment item's only under `raw/`), then the item through `ws_table`. A row whose partition starts `WS#` but names no workspace is never handled as the deployment's: an erasure fails before that row, or anything after it on its page, is deleted, and stops there; the daily retention run skips and counts it (`unscoped_rows_skipped`, `RetentionUnscopedRowsSkipped`), so the deployment's windows keep running (the runbook is in docs/source-policies.md). The job and audit rows count them (`deleted_workspace_items`, part of `deleted_items`, written once above 0; the erasure API's job view and Administration's erasure job details show it); a run that deleted only workspace items does not run the lineage pass (no artifact cites a workspace item until projects move, phase 3; its tombstone key `{ws}#{feedback_id}` never matches the pass's lookup). The time budget and cursor are the item Scan's, unchanged: a hand-over mid-page resumes after the last erased item, a workspace's or not. An S3 import's original (`ws/{ws}/raw/s3_import/…`, as the deployment's `raw/s3_import/…`) is linked from no row, so an erasure that deletes an import's item keeps it (IAM denies voc-retention any delete under `ws/ws_??????????????????????????/raw/s3_import/*`) and lists that folder on `files_needing_review` when it holds a file (`retention.import_originals_prefix`; Administration lists it as a folder). The category reprocess stays deployment-wide (it skips `WS#` items and writes unprefixed `CATEGORY#` / `URGENCY#` partitions); a workspace's reprocess comes with the workspace's settings (phase 3). D3's replay of a workspace's bronze is ready at the unit level: `raw_versions.parse_item_key` reads `ws/{ws}/raw/` keys and the raw mode's reader follows a review's link only under its own scope's root (`_raw_key`); its role still reads `raw/*` only, and gains `ws/{ws}/raw/*` with the route that starts a workspace's reprocess. IAM: `voc-retention` deletes (DeleteObject, DeleteObjectVersion) and lists versions under `ws/ws_??????????????????????????/raw/*` and `ws/ws_??????????????????????????/silver/*` too (one workspace id per pattern), pinned by `lib/stacks/processing-stack-retention.test.ts` and, with its reason (D5, legal obligations), `lib/customer-data-deletes.test.ts` |
| 7.04.00 | The voc-multiplayer-b track's aggregator, read-API and operator-route slices merged: a workspace's feedback is counted and read back in that workspace, and the operator routes start and watch ingestion there. Phase 2's aggregator and read APIs: a workspace's feedback is counted in its own counters and read back through the metrics and feedback APIs, beside the deployment-wide path. **Aggregator** (`lambda/aggregator/handler.py`): a stream record's workspace comes from its item's `pk` (`record_scope`: `WS#{ws}#…` is that workspace's; any other key the deployment's; a `WS#` key naming no workspace is skipped and counted, `AggregatorWorkspaceRecordsSkipped`, never counted deployment-wide). A workspace's INSERT, MODIFY and REMOVE do everything a deployment record does (the arrival transaction and its claim, the conditional reversals and their claim, the aged-out-day check, the pre-deploy persona fallback, the coalesced runs) in that workspace's rows: every counter `WS#{ws}#METRIC#…` with `metric_type` `WS#{ws}#<type>` (`lambda/shared/metric_keys.py` `metric_partition` / `metric_type_partition` over the layer's `metric_pk` / `metric_type`, the one spelling the writer and the readers share; `METRIC#urgent` moved there as `URGENT_PK`), the earliest-date watermark and the data_version under `WS#{ws}#METRIC#meta` (`shared/earliest_date.py` `earliest_date_key`, `shared/data_version.py` `data_version_key`; `BatchBump` bumps once per batch and per scope). The scope is set around one record or one coalesced run (a ContextVar, read only where a request is built); a run groups by event AND scope, so it never mixes the deployment's records with a workspace's or two workspaces'. Every write of a workspace record goes through `ws_table` (an update's key and `metric_type`; each `Update` of a joint transaction passes `WorkspaceTable.check_update` first, new in the layer); the claims keep their keys (`aggregator#stream#{eventID}`: unique per record). A deployment record is aggregated exactly as before. `scripts/retention/rebuild_aggregates.py --workspace ws_…` rebuilds one workspace's counters from its items only (default: the deployment's, from the deployment's items; [Processing pipeline](processing-pipeline.md)). **Read APIs**: the metrics API (`lambda/api/metrics_handler.py`: every `GET /feedback…` and `/metrics/…` route) and the feedback edits (`lambda/api/feedback_edit_handler.py`: `PUT /feedback/{id}/category|dimensions`) resolve `X-Workspace-Id` afresh at the start of every request, before the route reads anything (`lambda/shared/workspace_request.py` over `resolve_workspace`: 400 for an empty header, the one 404 for a malformed id, an unknown or `deleting` workspace and a non-member, the deployment's `admins` included; a platform admin enters as admin, live-checked and audited; a fault a fixed 500). In a workspace every read goes through `ws_table` on its partitions (`lambda/shared/scoped_query.py`: deployment-wide the very request it always sent; in a workspace the layer builds the key condition and refuses a partition or a cursor outside the workspace): an item by id (`WS#{ws}#FEEDBACK#…`, so another workspace's id, or the deployment's, is not found), the lists by date, category, urgency and channel (`WS#{ws}#DATE#…`, `#CATEGORY#`, `#URGENCY#`, `#CHANNEL#`), the full items of a page (`BatchGetItem` keys checked by `WorkspaceTable.check_keys`, new), the counters and the metric-type breakdowns, the data_version and watermark. The response cache keys on the workspace too (`shared/response_cache.py`), and its versions read takes the workspace's data_version and watermark beside the deployment's `config_version`, so no answer crosses scopes, and every GET's `Vary` names `X-Workspace-Id` beside `Authorization` (`shared/http_cache.py`). `/feedback/urgent`'s `next_token` must name the request's urgency partition and, in a workspace, one of its items (400 otherwise). An edit in a workspace is for its editors and admins (a viewer 403), the role read from the table, never the 30 s cache (as the members routes do), and resolved before the body is checked; it updates the workspace's item through `ws_table` (`gsi2pk` to `WS#{ws}#CATEGORY#…`, `shared/feedback_category.py`), keeps its previous version under `ws/{ws}/silver/` and bumps the workspace's data_version. A request without the header reads and edits the deployment, exactly as before. The route-gate inventory (`lambda/api/test/test_route_admin_gates.py`) gains the workspace dimension for these routes (`WORKSPACE_SCOPED`: every route of a module that resolves a request's workspace is listed, and each resolves it first: the metrics API in a resolver middleware, the edits as a top-level call). The live platform-admin check is the one `lambda/shared/platform_admin_gate.py` `confirm_live_platform_admin` the settings API runs too. CORS: `X-Workspace-Id` is an allowed request header on the gateway's preflight and error responses (`lib/stacks/api-gateway.ts`) and in the Lambdas' own CORS answer (`lambda/shared/api.py`). IAM: the metrics and feedback-edit roles attach the stack's one workspace-resolution policy (`lib/utils/workspace-resolution-grants.ts` `grantRequestWorkspace`, the settings API's since 6.18.00: GetItem on `WS#*` and `AUDIT#WS#*`, PutItem on `AUDIT#WS#*` only; the audit-row guard names the policy a writer of that partition only), and may call Cognito `AdminListGroupsForUser` and `AdminGetUser` (the live check); the feedback-edit role also bumps `WS#{one id}#METRIC#meta` and puts under `ws/{one id}/silver/*`. Not yet (phase 3 unless said): `GET /logs/*`, the Data Explorer, the stream Lambda's TypeScript reads (`queryFeedbackById`), the briefing, agents and their heartbeat, projects and the assistant stay deployment-wide; settings (categories, dimensions, source profiles), each person's category access and the config version stay the deployment's; the MCP tools do not send `X-Workspace-Id` yet, so a workspace's feedback is REST-only until MCP tokens pin a workspace (phase 3); the web app sends `X-Workspace-Id` on every call inside a workspace (since 6.12.00, `frontend/src/api/baseUrl.ts`), so inside a workspace its dashboards, feedback lists and edits act in that workspace; the category reprocess does not reach a workspace's items **Operator routes.** Phase 2's operator routes: starting and watching ingestion acts in the request's workspace, beside the deployment-wide path. The integrations API (`lambda/api/integrations_handler.py`: `/integrations/*`, `/sources/*`) and the scrapers API (`lambda/api/scrapers_handler.py`: `/scrapers/*` but `/scrapers/manual/*`) resolve `X-Workspace-Id` in a resolver middleware before any route (one shared implementation, `ResolvedRoutes` in `lambda/shared/workspace_request.py`: its `middleware`, over `resolve_first` and `RequestWorkspace`, and its admin gate `require_request_admin`: 400 for an empty header, the one 404 for a malformed id, an unknown or `deleting` workspace and a non-member, the deployment's `admins` included; a platform admin enters as admin, live-checked and audited; any method but GET resolves the role from the table, never the 30 s cache). In a workspace the admin routes (credentials, app connectors, scraper saves and deletes, analyze-url, Run now, a scraper's run, reset, enable, disable) need the caller's admin role there (`require_request_admin`: a member admin or an entered platform admin; an editor or a viewer 403; the deployment's `admins` grants nothing); the reads stay open to any member. **Runs**: `POST /sources/{source}/run` and `POST /scrapers/{id}/run` invoke the ingestor with the RESOLVED workspace in `workspace_id`, set after every other field by `workspace_payload` (a `workspace_id` in the body is dropped, never copied), so the run is the ingestors' run in that workspace (its credentials, watermarks, breaker, raw objects and queued messages); their `running` rows are `WS#{ws}#SOURCE_RUN#{source}` and `WS#{ws}#SCRAPER_RUN#{id}` (`lambda/shared/run_rows.py` over the layer's `source_run_pk` / `scraper_run_pk`, through `ws_table`), the rows the run moves, and `GET /sources/status?run_status=` and `GET /scrapers/{id}/status` read them there. A workspace's run is not gated on its plugin settings (`PLUGIN#` rows, not built), but both run routes answer the credentials' 409 (below) unless the workspace's secret is `ready`, before a row is written or a run invoked; a run whose secret holds none of the plugin's credentials (a new workspace's `{}`, or only another plugin's keys) ends `error` on its run row naming `WorkspaceCredentialsNotSet` (`lambda/shared/exceptions.py`, a `SecretUnreadableError`) and does not count against the workspace's breaker, so a plugin is not stopped in a workspace that has not saved its credentials yet (deployment-wide a namespace miss is still counted, as before). Run now of `s3_import` in a workspace answers the S3 import API's 400 (below). **Radar**: a workspace's Radar takes no reviews yet (phase 3). In a workspace a scraper, an app connector or a review page that names a `radar_brand` is refused (400, `ResolvedRoutes.refuse_radar_brand`) before anything is written, rather than saved to stamp messages the processor refuses; and `PUT /integrations/{source}/credentials` takes no `configs` key at all there (400 naming `POST /scrapers` and `POST /integrations/{source}/apps`, the routes that check each entry), whatever list it holds. Without the header both are saved as before. The processor no longer lets a deployment source profile's `radar_brand` claim a workspace's message (7.00.00 refused it): such a message is the workspace's own feedback, so a workspace's Run now of a source the deployment tracks for Radar is not refused. **Breaker**: `GET /sources/status` reports the plugin's one schedule rule (shared by every workspace) and the workspace's own `WS#{ws}#CIRCUIT#{source}` row (one batch read, keys checked by `WorkspaceTable.check_keys`; `last_error` for the workspace's admins only); `POST /sources/{source}/reset` deletes that row only (through `ws_table`; the deployment's breaker and other workspaces' stay); `PUT /sources/{source}/enable` and `/disable` answer 400 in a workspace, because they switch the rule every workspace's tick fans out from: per-workspace enablement belongs to the plugin settings, and turning the schedule on or off stays deployment-wide. On both paths only the `TRIPPED` row is cleared; the `FAILURES#` rows expire on their 24 h TTL and count only inside the 15-minute window. **Credentials**: in a workspace `GET /integrations/status`, `GET`/`PUT /integrations/{source}/credentials`, `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /integrations/{source}/apps[/{app_id}]`, `GET`/`POST /scrapers`, `DELETE /scrapers/{id}` and `GET /scrapers/connectors` read and write the workspace's own secret, `{prefix}/{ws}/credentials` (`lambda/shared/workspace_plugin_store.py` `credentials_secret_id`: the name built from the resolved membership and `WORKSPACE_SECRET_PREFIX` only, never from a path, a body or a query field), with the same `<plugin>_<key>` namespacing, write-only keys and bounds; the workspace's secret is used only when its `META` says `secret_status: ready` (read when the request's workspace is resolved, `WorkspaceContext.secret_status`): otherwise, even if a secret already exists at the name, every one of these routes and both run routes answers 409, the reads included, never an empty list and never the shared secret; the answer names the secret and says an operator must create it and mark the workspace ready (the recovery steps under Credentials, below: no route creates or retries a secret). A `ready` workspace whose secret is missing gets a 409 saying the secret is missing. `GET /sources/status`, reset, enable and disable read no secret and answer as above. Scraper and app connector configs live in that secret, not in `voc-aggregates`, so a run in the workspace finds them there; the dimension and tag defaults a scraper save checks stay the deployment's settings (phase 3). **S3 import**: the import bucket is not keyed by workspace, so a reprocess in a workspace would ingest the deployment's staged file into it: `lambda/api/s3_import_handler.py` refuses every request that sends `X-Workspace-Id` with one 400 before anything is read (`deployment_only`, its first middleware), and its reprocess, its `SOURCE_RUN#s3_import` rows and the import history (`/scrapers/manual/history`) stay deployment-wide. **Guards**: the route-gate inventory (`lambda/api/test/test_route_admin_gates.py`) lists every route of both modules in `WORKSPACE_SCOPED`, pins that both modules install the one `ResolvedRoutes` middleware, that each admin route's first statement is `_request.require_request_admin()` (both branches of the gate checked), that enable and disable call `refuse_in_workspace` right after the gate, and that the S3 import API's first middleware is `deployment_only`; the raw-access guard (`lambda/shared/test/test_ws_raw_access.py`) still finds no request-facing module writing `workspace_id` into a mapping, pins `workspace_payload` (its one write is `context.ws` of a checked `WorkspaceContext`, after every other field) and that a `WorkspaceContext` is built only by `lambda/shared/workspace.py` and `workspace_members.py`; the integrations API's two `SOURCE_RUN#` literals and the ingestors' one moved to `run_rows.py` (the run rows' one spelling, which the webscraper's `SCRAPER_RUN#` and the breaker's key, `plugin_circuit.circuit_key`, share with the APIs too); `lambda/api/test/test_operator_routes_workspace.py` pins that no handler names a secret itself. IAM (`lib/stacks/api-data-lambdas.ts`): the integrations and scrapers roles attach the same workspace-resolution policy and `GetSecretValue` + `PutSecretValue` on `voc/ws/*` (`lib/utils/workspace-secrets.ts` `grantWorkspaceSecretsReadWrite`, with encrypt and decrypt on the customer-managed key the secrets are created with; every workspace's secret, so only the code keeps a request to its own: decision 1's soft isolation, TENETS.md tenet 6; no create, delete, describe or tag); the integrations role's DeleteItem reaches `CIRCUIT#*` and `WS#ws_??????????????????????????#CIRCUIT#*` (one workspace id). Not yet: the web app sends `X-Workspace-Id` on every call inside a workspace (since 6.12.00, `frontend/src/api/baseUrl.ts`), so inside a workspace Administration → Sources (credentials, Run now, scrapers, the breaker) acts in that workspace, for that workspace's admins; the MCP tools, the assistant's `get_source_run` and the plan waits read the deployment-wide run rows only (phase 3); `GET /logs/scraper/{id}` and the import history stay deployment-wide; the S3 import API, its bucket and its trigger wait for the import bucket's workspace prefix **The hold** (this release, beside the merge): while `PER_WORKSPACE_INGESTION` is off, Run now and a scraper's run in a workspace, and an S3 import's reprocess that names one, answer the 409 enabling a plugin answers (`INGESTION_NOT_YET`, one check: `lambda/shared/workspace_plugin_settings.py` `hold_workspace_ingestion`), so no workspace run starts while an erasure by author, source id or CSV row (D5) and the category reprocess (D3) do not reach workspace items; `run_source` validates the source and refuses `s3_import` before it reads the secret. The web app's Run now shows that reason (`common:workspaces.errors.ingestionRunNotYet`); after enabling a source inside a workspace a source card reads the status again instead of showing the workspace's breaker closed (enabling closes the deployment's); the members page's and platform console's notice (`workspaces.sharedData`, 9 locales) says what each workspace keeps (feedback, dashboards, brand, resolved problems) and what is still shared (projects, the assistant, Radar, the other settings). **Open** (the next web-app slice): Administration → Sources and its credentials are gated on the deployment's `admins` group, not on the caller's role in the workspace the server checks; and the deployment-wide credentials, scrapers and Run now cannot be reached from the web app by anyone who has a workspace (the gate always enters one, and these calls name it) |
| 7.05.00 | No code change: the tenet questions are [settled](#tenet-questions-settled-owner-2026-11-12) (owner, 2026-11-12). TENETS.md gains D8, "Each workspace is its own boundary": a share copies, the importing workspace owns its copy, and nothing done in the source (an edit, an erasure, deleting the source workspace) reaches it; the share step says so before anything is copied. D5 says a workspace delete is its data controller's own request (decision 17, built in phase 4) and that "every derived copy" means the copies inside the workspace. Also decided: credentials to SSM (decision 19), workspace audit rows under `AUDIT#` (decision 20), the 7.00.00 re-key for fresh deployments only (decision 21) |
| 7.06.00 | The voc-multiplayer-b track's public-routes slice merged: feedback forms and webhooks per workspace. Phase 2's public routes, first part: a feedback form and a webhook route to a workspace by a server-side record, never by what the caller sends, beside the deployment-wide path. **Lookup and guard**: `lambda/shared/workspace_routing.py` reads a public entry point's stored row in `voc-workspaces` (strongly consistent GetItem): a form's lookup row `FORM#{form id}` / `META` (`workspace_id`, `created_at`), or a workspace's own `WS#{ws}` / `META`; the workspace must be `active`, else `Unroutable`, which every route answers exactly as an unknown id. The scope is built by the layer's new `scope_from_lookup` (shared/ws_repo.py) from that stored item only, and a payload or message names the workspace only through `workspace_payload`, now the one writer for every path, in that module: it drops a `workspace_id` the payload carries and sets the server's workspace after every other field, taken from a `WorkspaceContext` (a request's resolved membership, as before) or a `RoutedWorkspace` (a stored row's, which only that module builds), anything else a `TypeError`; the raw-access guard (`lambda/shared/test/test_ws_raw_access.py`) pins all three (`scope_from_lookup` called only there, each time on an item that module read itself with `shared/aws.py` `consistent_item`; `RoutedWorkspace` built only there; the one writer's `isinstance` names exactly those two types, its one write `source.ws` after every field, and no other module defines one), and still finds no request-facing module writing `workspace_id` into a mapping. The lookup row lives in `voc-workspaces`, not `voc-aggregates`: it is control-plane routing like `SLUG#`, read in the same table and request as the workspace's `META` status it depends on, and the workspace delete (phase 4) clears the control plane's rows of a workspace there. **Feedback forms** (`lambda/api/feedback_form_handler.py`; [Feedback Forms](feedback-forms.md#workspaces-since-70600)): a new form's id is 32 hex characters (128 random bits) after a prefix: `ff_` for a workspace's form, `fd_` for a deployment form, so the public routes look up only a workspace's shape; older 8-character ids keep working. Deployment-wide, an update or delete of an id a `FORM#` lookup row holds answers the unknown id's 404, and an update is conditional on the form existing on both paths (404, never an upsert), so no deployment row can be written under a workspace form's id and served once the workspace deletes its form. The signed-in routes resolve `X-Workspace-Id` first in each route (`RequestWorkspace`, as the feedback edits do: 400 for an empty header, the one 404 for a malformed id, an unknown or `deleting` workspace and a non-member, the deployment's `admins` included; a platform admin enters as admin, live-checked and audited); in a workspace any member reads, and create, update and delete need the editor or admin role there (a viewer 403; the role read from the table), today's open-to-every-user split. A workspace's forms are `WS#{ws}#FEEDBACK_FORM` / `FORM#{id}` in `voc-aggregates`, every read and write through `ws_table`; a create puts the lookup row first (conditional: an id already routed is never taken over), then the form (conditional), and removes the lookup row again when the form cannot be written; a delete first writes an `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` / `{iso}#{random}` row (kind `form_deleted`, `actor` the caller's sub, `form_id`, `at`; a conditional put of a new row, under the audit-row guard), so it records the attempt and a row that cannot be written deletes nothing, then removes the form, then the lookup row only when it names the workspace; the stats, the submissions and the list's `?include=stats` read the workspace's own feedback partition `WS#{ws}#SOURCE#{brand}` (`shared/feedback_partitions.py` `source_partition`, the processor's key). The public `config`, `iframe` and `submit` routes never resolve the header: they look up `FORM#{id}` for an id of the `ff_` shape only (every other id is a deployment form, with no extra read); no row is a deployment form, exactly as before; a row naming a malformed, unknown or `deleting` workspace, a row without its form and an unknown id all answer the same 404; a lookup read fault a 500, never the deployment's form. A workspace's submission is queued with its `workspace_id` (so the processor stores it in the workspace) and its opt-in raw copy kept under `ws/{ws}/raw/feedback_form/…`; it uses the deployment's categories and `feedback_form` source profile, attributes no participant ask (asks stay the deployment's, phase 3) and never takes the prototype-pin path. **Webhooks** (`plugins/_shared/base_webhook.py`, `plugins/github_issues/webhook/handler.py`; [Plugin Architecture](plugin-architecture.md#a-workspaces-webhook-endpoint-since-70600)): `POST /webhooks/{plugin}/{ws}` is a new route per webhook plugin and method (no authorizer, its own stage throttle in `lib/stacks/api-gateway.ts`, as the deployment's route has, one bucket shared by every workspace). The `{ws}` value is only the key of the workspace's `META`: a well-formed id of an `active` workspace whose `secret_status` is `ready`. The webhook secret and the plugin's settings then come from that workspace's own secret `{prefix}/{ws}/credentials` (the plugin's namespace), never the shared one; a verified delivery is archived under `ws/{ws}/raw/{plugin}/…` and queued with that `workspace_id` through `workspace_payload`. A warm container keeps a workspace's secret 60 s (`plugins/_shared/base_webhook.py` `WorkspaceSecrets`, not `get_secret`'s lifelong cache), so a webhook secret the workspace rotates or saves takes effect within 60 s and a flood of forged deliveries costs no extra read; a failed read is not kept. The META read projects its routing attributes only (`pk`, `sk`, `status`, `secret_status`), and the webhook role's GetItem is limited to them in IAM (`dynamodb:Attributes`), so it cannot read a `MEMBER#` row's email or name. A delivery on `/webhooks/{plugin}/{ws}` that a plugin did not route (no `delivery_workspace`) gets the same 401 from `BaseWebhook.handle`, never deployment-wide handling. A malformed or unknown id, a `deleting` workspace, a secret not ready, unreadable or without the plugin's webhook secret, another workspace's secret and no signature all get the bad signature's 401, after the body checks (400 and 413 depend on the body alone), and a workspace that routes nowhere costs no secret read (so the answer, though not its timing, confirms nothing); a read fault is a 500. `POST /webhooks/{plugin}` stays deployment-wide, exactly as before. **Guards**: the route-gate inventory (`lambda/api/test/test_route_admin_gates.py`) lists the form API's signed-in routes in `WORKSPACE_SCOPED` (resolved first, each write's first statement `_request_scope(write=True)`, the editor gate an `if` that raises) and its public routes in a new `PUBLIC_BY_LOOKUP` table (each calls the lookup as a top-level statement and never the request resolver, and the lookup reads no header, body or event). IAM: the forms role gets the workspace resolution grants (the stack's one workspace-resolution policy, `lib/utils/workspace-resolution-grants.ts` `grantRequestWorkspace`), GetItem, PutItem and DeleteItem on `voc-workspaces` `FORM#*` only, DeleteItem on `WS#{one id}#FEEDBACK_FORM` in `voc-aggregates` and puts under `ws/{one id}/raw/feedback_form/*` (`lib/stacks/api-engagement-lambdas.ts`; the lookup row's delete is named in `lib/customer-data-deletes.test.ts` as routing configuration); the webhook role gets GetItem on `voc-workspaces` `WS#*` only, `GetSecretValue` on `voc/ws/*` (every workspace's secret: only the code, which names it from the routed workspace, keeps a delivery to its own, decision 1's soft isolation; the deployment key it already decrypts with) and puts under `ws/{one id}/raw/{plugin}/*` (`lib/stacks/api-stack.ts`). Not yet: voting sessions (`VOTE#{id}`) and the email inbox (`{slug}@<inbound domain>`) still serve the deployment only (a session scores a Prioritization row, and those are not keyed by workspace until projects move, phase 3; the inbox's dedupe state, caps and receipt rule are the deployment's, and the slugs `feedback`, `ideas`, `bugs` and `reviews` must be reserved first, since any local part reaches the deployment's inbox today); the web app shows no workspace form or webhook endpoint and sends no header **The hold** (this release): a workspace form's submission and a delivery on a workspace's webhook are ingestion into that workspace, so while `PER_WORKSPACE_INGESTION` is off both answer the same 409 as enabling a plugin and every run in a workspace (`hold_workspace_ingestion`): the submit route before anything is archived, anchored or queued, the webhook after its signature check and before the body is parsed. And `workspace_payload` (`lambda/shared/workspace_routing.py`), the one writer of `workspace_id` on a payload or message, calls the same check, so no payload carries a workspace past the hold. Creating a form in a workspace answers that 409 too (a form that would refuse every visitor is never embedded); a workspace's existing forms can be listed, edited and deleted, and a delete of an id the workspace does not hold is the 404 with no `form_deleted` row. **Open** (the next web-app slice): inside a workspace the web app's Feedback Forms page lists the workspace's forms only, so the deployment's forms cannot be reached from the web app by anyone who has a workspace; and the dev mock's form routes (`frontend/mock-forms.js`) ignore `X-Workspace-Id`, so the local web app shows the deployment's forms inside a workspace. IAM: the feedback-form role attaches the stack's one workspace-resolution policy (as the other APIs that resolve, since 7.04.00) and writes `FORM#*` from its own; the audit-row guard names it a writer of `AUDIT#WS#*` (a workspace form's `form_deleted` row) |
| 7.07.00 | The voc-multiplayer-c track's configuration-readers slice merged (no behaviour change: no caller passes a workspace yet). The categories, the dimensions and the source profiles can be read per workspace ([Per-workspace configuration](#per-workspace-configuration)): every read of the three goes through `lambda/shared/workspace_config.py` `get_config_item` (a workspace's `WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#{kind}` / `config` through `ws_table`, else the deployment's `SETTINGS#{kind}`), and each reader takes an optional workspace scope: `get_raw_categories_config` (`shared/api.py`) and `read_categories_config` (`shared/category_gate.py`), the processor's `load_categories_config` (`shared/categorization.py`; the deployment's key now in the leaf `shared/categories_key.py`), `load_dimensions_config`, `load_source_profiles`, `cached_source_profile_strict` and `source_profile_configured`, and the feedback routes' access checks `scope_for_caller` / `scope_for_event` and `read_restricted_sources`; the categories and source-profile caches keep one entry per workspace. Every existing caller, the aggregator, the metrics and feedback routes, forms, webhooks and the processor that 7.04.00 and 7.06.00 brought included, still passes none and reads the deployment's rows. The six settings routes (`GET` and `PUT /settings/categories`, `/settings/dimensions`, `/settings/sources`) stay the deployment's until the phase-2 cut-over, which moves them and the data path together (7.08.00, the row below) |
| 7.08.00 | The configuration cut-over: the categories, the dimensions and the source profiles are each workspace's, both directions in one release ([Per-workspace configuration](#per-workspace-configuration)). **Writers**: `GET` and `PUT /settings/categories`, `/settings/dimensions` and `/settings/sources` resolve `X-Workspace-Id` first (`request_workspace`); in a workspace any member reads its `WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#{kind}` row and only its admins write it (`require_settings_admin`, through `shared/workspace_config.py` `put_config_item` and `ws_table`); its profiles set no retention window and no Radar brand (400); without the header, the deployment's rows and `admins` as before. **Readers**: the processor reads a workspace message's own categories, dimensions and source profile (PII policy, defaults, tags, inline raw-copy policy), cached per workspace; the plugin ingestors' source policy and raw archive, a routed workspace webhook and a workspace form's submission read the workspace's profile, with the deployment's retention window for the source; the metrics and feedback routes' access checks (`scope_for_event`, `scope_for_caller`), their configured categories and dimensions, the feedback edits' checks, and a scraper's or form's label check read the request's workspace.
