A failed invoke ends the row `failed` (best effort) and always frees the lock; the invoke's error is the one reported (a fixed 500). `GET` answers the newest export's status only (no link, no write), `{export: {id, status, created_at, started_at?, completed_at?, expires_at?, erased_at?, phase?, counts?, size_bytes?, error?} \| null}` (never the starter's sub or the upload id): `queued`, `running`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `erased` (an erasure reached the workspace: its export objects are deleted); `expired` past `expires_at` (completion + 7 days); `failed` with "The export stopped before it finished" for a running job that has not moved for an hour (its invocation timed out or died before writing its row, or its hand-over was throttled past the event age and dropped; no notice was sent; its lock is stale by then, so a new start works), or with "The export did not start" for a queued one whose start never ran within the hour. `POST …/export/link` answers `{link: {id, url, expires_in, expires_at}}`, a fresh 15-minute presigned link (an attachment named `{slug}-export-{date}.zip`), only while the newest export is completed and inside its window (409 otherwise), issued only after its `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` row (kind `export_link_issued`) is written. **The job** (`lambda/jobs/workspace_export/`, VocProcessingStack, invoked by name like `voc-retention`): each invocation claims its generation on the job row with a conditional update (generation 0 only from `queued` and created within the hour, generation g only from `running` at g - 1 and moved within the hour), so a duplicate, stale or late delivery changes nothing (an event older than 40 minutes is dropped too: `maxEventAge`, chosen so that Lambda's throttling retries, up to 40 minutes, plus the 15-minute invocation stay inside the lock's hour, and a late start never runs beside an export that took the freed lock); the row must name the event's workspace. It reads, by Query only, voc-feedback's `gsi1-by-date` one day partition `WS#{ws}#DATE#{day}` at a time (pages of 500), from the earlier of the workspace's earliest date and the first day of its `daily_total` counters to the day after the export started (a workspace that counts feedback but records neither day fails with an error that says to rebuild its counters); voc-aggregates' `WS#{ws}#PLUGIN_CONFIG`, `#FEEDBACK_FORM`, `#IMPORT_RUN` (each run's `#IMPORT_RUN#{run}` detail, the summary where it is gone) and `#JOB#erasure` (`er_…` rows, without `value_hash`) through `ws_table`; and voc-workspaces' `WS#{ws}` (`META`, `MEMBER#`) and `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` through the layer's new `control_query(scope, table, 'meta'\|'audit')`, one read-only page of a partition the layer builds itself. It writes JSONL files of at most 256 MB per entity (`feedback/feedback-0001.jsonl`, …, `plugin_configs/`, `feedback_forms/`, `import_runs/`, `erasure_jobs/`, `workspace/`, `members/`, `audit/`) and a `manifest.json` (every entity's files and count; the counts check, the feedback exported against the sum of the workspace's `daily_total` counters, reported and never fatal; `not_per_workspace` for projects and documents, personas, agents and workflows and settings, phase 3, count 0; `not_exported`: the derived `METRIC#` rows and the operational ones, and the memory and conversations (the open gap below); `excluded`: the credentials, which the job's role cannot read, the erasure value hash and an audit row's email hash (`email_sha256`, dropped from `audit`); `raw_objects`: `not_included_yet`, slice 4b) into one streaming ZIP64 with data descriptors and DEFLATE (`zip_stream.py`), cut into 64 MB parts of one S3 multipart upload, `ws/{ws}/exports/{export id}/workspace-export.zip`. No entry crosses an invocation: two minutes before its 15-minute timeout the job closes the open file, keeps the unsent tail (under 5 MB; a larger one goes up as a part) in `work/tail-{gen}` and its central-directory records in `work/cd-{gen}`, renews the lock, records its progress and invokes itself (also once every entity is written, when too little time is left to complete); the last generation writes the central directory, the ZIP64 end records and the last part, then completes the upload with the ETags ListParts returns (at most 300 generations and 10,000 parts, 640 GB). At each start, before each hand-over and before completing it reads the workspace's `META`: gone or `deleting`, it aborts the upload and ends `cancelled` / `workspace_deleting`. Every end the job writes (completed, failed with the error's type only, cancelled) releases the lock, and when it wrote the row (it was still queued or running) sends the starter a job notice (`job_type` `workspace_export`; no email); an invocation the timeout or memory kills writes nothing, and the export page shows it failed an hour later. **Erasure** (TENETS.md D5; [Source policies](source-policies.md#retention-and-erasure)): an export is a derived copy, so an erasure that deletes any item of a workspace, or de-identifies one of its membership rows, closes that workspace's exports the moment it first meets the workspace, before the item goes or the row changes (a job that names one workspace at its start): it takes the export lock (renewed at each of its hand-overs, which also mark any export that slipped in; a start meanwhile answers 409 "An erasure is running in this workspace: start the export when it ends") and marks every queued, running or completed export `erased`, so no link is signed while the erasure runs; an erasure in one workspace does this at its start, so it deletes that workspace's exports even when it matches nothing there (one under way reads "The workspace's data was erased during the export. Start a new one."). It then runs an `exports` phase there, after the item scan and the memberships (`lambda/jobs/retention/exports.py`): it takes the workspace's export lock for itself (a start meanwhile answers 409), marks every queued, running or completed export `erased`, aborts the open export upload (one already gone, aborted or completed since it was listed, is done), deletes every version under `ws/{ws}/exports/`, sweeps once more, then frees the lock (`deleted_export_objects` on the job and audit rows). An export whose hand-over, or whose last re-check right before it completes the zip, finds its row erased aborts its upload, deletes the work objects it wrote and never completes (the window that leaves is in TENETS.md D5's gaps). An upload nothing aborts is aborted by the raw bucket's `abort-incomplete-uploads` lifecycle rule 7 days after it began. A daily retention window does not (it would clear the exports every day): a window's deleted items stay in an export until it expires. **Layer** (`lambda/shared/ws_repo.py`): `export_job_pk`, `control_query` and `ExportObjects` (the multipart upload, work objects, reads and the presigned download of keys under `ws/{ws}/exports/` only, each object tagged `voc-export=true`; any other key refused before S3). **Bucket** (`lib/stacks/core-buckets.ts`): a lifecycle rule expires objects tagged `voc-export=true` after 10 days (their noncurrent versions 1 day later), and the bucket policy denies every PutObject and PutObjectTagging that sets that tag outside `ws/{one id}/exports/*`, and every copy of an export object to a key outside one (`s3:x-amz-copy-source` `*ws/{one id}/exports/*`, its `/` also `%2F` or `%2f`: a copy keeps its source's tags), so it never reaches `raw/` or `silver/` (D1). **IAM** (`lib/stacks/workspace-export-worker.ts`): the job may Query `gsi1-by-date` only in voc-feedback, on `WS#{one id}#DATE#*` partitions only; GetItem and Query on `WS#{one id}#*` and UpdateItem on `WS#{one id}#JOB#export` in voc-aggregates; Query on `WS#{one id}` and `AUDIT#WS#{one id}` in voc-workspaces; PutObject, PutObjectTagging, GetObject, AbortMultipartUpload and ListMultipartUploadParts under `ws/{one id}/exports/*`, and DeleteObject on `ws/{one id}/exports/*/work/*` only (its own work objects, when it may not go on); KMS; its own invoke and the `jobs` channel's publish. No Scan, no table delete, no list of the bucket, no read of `raw/` or `silver/`, no SSM and no Secrets Manager. Reserved concurrency 10 (free; the lock already allows one export per workspace, and an export runs one invocation at a time, two for the seconds of a hand-over, so about five workspaces export at once before an invoke is throttled; it adds 10 to the account's quota need, 130 with the MCP endpoint's 20: [deployment.md](deployment.md#reserved-concurrency-of-the-mcp-endpoint--c-mcpreservedconcurrency)). A throttled invoke waits in Lambda's retries and one still unrun after 40 minutes is dropped, never silently: a dropped start leaves its row queued, and past the hour `GET` shows it `failed`, "The export did not start. Start it again."; a dropped hand-over leaves its row running, and past the stale mark (an hour after its last progress, when its lock is stale too) `GET` shows it `failed`, "The export stopped before it finished. Start it again.", no async retry, events older than 40 minutes dropped; 1,769 MB (one vCPU, the Python sizing cap; the design's 2,048 MB would buy memory, not speed). The workspaces API gains PutItem, UpdateItem and Query on `WS#{one id}#JOB#export`, GetObject under `ws/{one id}/exports/*` (it signs the link) and the job's invoke by name (`lib/stacks/api-workspace-export.ts`). `voc-retention` gains DeleteObject, DeleteObjectVersion and AbortMultipartUpload under `ws/{one id}/exports/*`, ListBucketVersions for that prefix (`s3:prefix`), and ListBucketMultipartUploads on the bucket unconditioned (that action takes no `s3:prefix` condition; it reaches upload keys and ids only, no data, and the code lists one workspace's exports). The route-gate inventory lists the three routes as workspace-admin only. **Web app**: Workspace settings gains an Export section for the workspace's admins: Start an export, its status (polled while it is queued or running, no longer once it is done or failed), Download (a fresh 15-minute link for each press), the 7-day window and each refusal said in words, in the nine languages. Not yet: the raw and Silver objects (slice 4b); the delete (step 3 on). **Open gap**: the export does not hold yet what 7.15.00 and 7.16.00 made per workspace, its memory (voc-memory's `WS#{ws}#` partitions, the page imports under `ws/{ws}/memory-imports/`) and its members' assistant conversations (`WS#{ws}#USER#{sub}` in voc-conversations): the manifest lists both under `not_exported`, and a later release adds them as entities (an erasure would then reach their copies in an export through the exports phase, which already deletes a workspace's whole export). **Config history.** A workspace's config lists keep their history (TENETS.md D1, D2, D4; [Credentials](#credentials)). A save that changes a plugin's list (`lambda/shared/plugin_configs.py` `save_configs`, from `lambda/shared/workspace_plugin_store.py`) stamps the row `WS#{ws}#PLUGIN_CONFIG` / `{plugin}` with `updated_by` (the caller's Cognito `sub`, from the authorizer claims through `ResolvedRoutes.credentials`, never a body field) and `updated_at`, and keeps the list it replaces as a row `WS#{ws}#PLUGIN_CONFIG#HISTORY` / `{plugin}#{superseded_at}#{random}` (`configs`, the replaced list's own `updated_at` and `updated_by`, `superseded_at`, `superseded_by_sub`; `lambda/shared/ws_repo.py` `plugin_config_history_pk` / `plugin_config_history_key`), what the shared secret's versions kept deployment-wide. Both go in one `TransactWriteItems` through `ws_table`: the new row is conditioned on the row being the one the route read, its list and its `updated_at` exactly as stored (`#configs = :old AND #updated_at = :at`, `attribute_not_exists(#updated_at)` for a row that had none; for a first save, no list, and no history row), so a save that lost a race (its condition, or a concurrent transaction; a list saved away and back since the read included) answers 409 "These settings were changed by someone else while you were editing them. Reload to see the latest, then save again." instead of losing a history entry. Both sizes are checked first and the list is saved before the plugin's credentials, so a 400 or the 409 writes nothing of that plugin; a service fault writing the credentials after that leaves the saved list in place and answers 500. An unchanged list writes nothing, as before. History rows are kept for good (no `ttl`: only `INVITE#` rows carry one). | 7.20.00 | The voc-multiplayer-b track's export-objects slice merged. Phase 4b: the workspace's own objects in its export ([Export and delete](#export-and-delete), step 2). After the JSONL entities the job writes every current object under `ws/{ws}/` except `ws/{ws}/exports/` (`raw/`, `silver/`, `memory-imports/` and any other folder) as `objects/<key without ws/{ws}/>`, each whole in one entry, read through the layer's read-only `WorkspaceObjects` (`lambda/shared/ws_repo.py`: ListObjectsV2 with `Prefix` `ws/{ws}/` only, pages of 1,000, the exports skipped; GetObject only of a key under the workspace, outside its exports, with no empty, `.` or `..` segment; another workspace's key, listed or asked for, is refused before any call). **Checkpoint**: the last key handled (`StartAfter`), so a hand-over mid-page or between pages (a page may hold only exports keys while more follow) never repeats or skips an object; each object is its current version when the listing reached it. **Names**: a key is user data, so its entry name passes the zip writer's rules (`zip_stream.py` `entry_name`: no segment empty, `.` or `..`, also once trailing dots and spaces are dropped, so no absolute path; no Windows reserved device name (CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, COM1-9, LPT1-9, any case, with or without an extension); the same in its NFKC form, which may hold no `/` or `\` the name does not (so a full-width `．．／．．／x` is refused); none of `\:<>"|?*`; no character of the Unicode categories Cc, Cf, Zl or Zp, so no C0 or C1 control, bidi override such as U+202E, zero-width character or line separator; valid UTF-8; at most 65,535 bytes); a key that fails is skipped and listed (`bad_name`, with the rule), and the export goes on. A folder marker (a zero-byte key ending `/`) is skipped quietly. Keys that differ only in letter case or Unicode normalisation stay distinct entries, but can overwrite each other when extracted on a case-insensitive file system (macOS, Windows): the manifest says so. **DEFLATE or STORE** (`lambda/jobs/workspace_export/objects.py` `stored`): STORE only for bytes already compressed, from first principles: images (`image/*` but SVG, which is text), the zip family (zip, docx, xlsx, pptx, odt, ods, odp, epub, jar) and gzip, by content type, else by the key's extension; everything else DEFLATE at level 6. A STORED entry carries a data descriptor (its CRC is known only once it is read), which some streaming readers reject; readers of the central directory (most unzip tools, Python's zipfile) open it: the manifest says so. **Size and time**: an entry never crosses an invocation, so an object is copied whole or not begun. Over 3 GiB it is never included (`too_large`, with its size: about 307 seconds at the conservative 10 MiB/s, twice that with the margin; splitting one would need an entry across invocations, which DEFLATE's state cannot cross). Otherwise the object is opened (its kind comes with its content type), and it is allowed twice its estimate (the margin: a rate measured on JSON is no promise for an incompressible file) at the rate measured for its kind (STORE or DEFLATE, kept in the run state across every invocation of the export; 10 MiB/s until 16 MiB of that kind are measured, and never faster than 10 MiB/s for an object over 256 MiB). One whose allowance exceeds a fresh invocation's budget (the 15-minute timeout less the 2-minute reserve and a minute for its start: 12 minutes; judged by time, not by what the invocation has written) is listed `too_slow` instead of started; one that fits a fresh invocation but not the time left is handed over. So the export never starts what it cannot finish, and every hand-over before an object makes progress. Every body opened is closed, read or not. A read stream that drops partway (a reset or timed-out connection, a short body) resumes from the byte reached with a ranged GetObject pinned to the version first read (`IfMatch` its ETag), at most twice; past that, or when the object was replaced meanwhile (412), the export fails rather than splice two versions into one entry. A key deleted between the listing and its read answers 403 under this IAM, not 404 (the role's ListBucket carries an `s3:prefix` condition a GetObject does not meet): the layer then lists exactly that key (`Prefix` the key, one result, which the condition allows) and reports it `gone` when it is not there, or raises the 403 when it is; one replaced by a version over 3 GiB is listed `too_large`. Only copies of at least 8 MiB count toward a kind's measured rate (a small copy is mostly its request's fixed cost, and would make a large object that fits read as too slow). **Manifest** `objects`: `count`, `bytes`, `not_included` (each `{key, size, reason, detail}`; at most 50, each key cut to 256 characters with `truncated: true`, at most 32 KB serialized: the run state crosses each hand-over in a 256 KB event), `not_included_count`, `versions` (current versions only), `note` and `reading` (the STORE and case notes above; `raw_objects: not_included_yet` is gone). **Load** (S1): one GetObject per object (and a one-key listing for one gone), one ListObjectsV2 per 1,000 keys, and one more GetObject for the object a hand-over falls before; a generation copies what fits in about 13 minutes (at roughly 20-50 ms a small object, some 15,000 to 40,000 small objects, or about 8 GiB at 10 MiB/s), so the ceilings are the run's 3 days (`MAX_RUN`, less an hour: the bucket's lifecycle expires an export's zip 10 days after its upload began and aborts an upload open 7 days, so a run must end with the whole 7-day download window still before the expiry; a run past it fails "Stopped after running for 3 days", its upload aborted), within which `MAX_GENERATIONS` (300, the runaway guard) × the objects of a generation (some 4.5 to 12 million small objects; a test holds the handler's copies of the lifecycle to `lib/stacks/core-buckets.ts`) and S3's 10,000 parts of 64 MiB (625 GiB). The central directory is streamed generation by generation into the last parts (each `work/cd-{gen}` read in turn, never joined), so memory stays at about one 64 MiB part plus one generation's records (about 100 bytes plus the name a record: a few MiB). **IAM** (`lib/stacks/workspace-export-worker.ts`): `s3:ListBucket` on the raw bucket only with `s3:prefix` `ws/{one id}/*`, and `s3:GetObject` on `ws/{one id}/*` (`WORKSPACE_OBJECTS_PREFIX`, `lib/stacks/core-buckets.ts`); the writes stay under `ws/{one id}/exports/*`; KMS decrypt as before (the key the bucket encrypts with). **D5**: unchanged: an erasure deletes the zip (every version under `ws/{ws}/exports/`), which holds the copies, and the raw objects' own erasure (`voc-retention`'s delete of an item's raw and Silver versions) is untouched; the exports are closed before the erasure deletes anything, so no link is signed for a zip holding an object it deletes. A failed read sends nothing and is never kept; the next write reads again. A store made without the check writes nothing inside a workspace. An `active` answer is kept 300 s per warm container and a not-active one 30 s, timed from when the read was sent: the TTLs the hard delete's planned Python status check will use (not merged; today the chat routes resolve each write's workspace fresh, with no status cache). An older read returning late never replaces a newer answer, so it can let through at most one write, inside the run bound below. The stream's DynamoDB client bounds each request at 10 s. **What bounds the last write**: the run's timeout, not the cache. Every run reads the status fresh at its start, so a run that starts after the mark writes nothing; the handler waits for its saves before it returns, so a run that started before the mark writes its last row within one invocation of it (`CHAT_STREAM_TIMEOUT`, 15 minutes, `lib/stacks/api-assistant-lambdas.ts`). The cache never extends that past the run's end; the per-write check only stops such a run sooner. Released by `release_project_claim(table, project_id, *, workspace) -> bool`: a DeleteItem conditioned on the claim naming that workspace (False, nothing deleted, otherwise); its caller is the workspace delete (3g). **Why voc-projects**: the claim sits beside the rows it routes to (`project_pk`'s `PROJECT#{id}` and `WS#{ws}#PROJECT#{id}` are both there), so the create, its readers (the public links, the product-doc extractor, the MCP pins) and the projects API's role need no other table; a table of its own would be one more to provision, grant and back up for one row per project. **Registry**: `WS#{ws}#PROJECT_IDS` / `{id}` (`created_at`) in voc-projects, built by `lambda/shared/project_keys.py` `project_registry_key(project_id, *, workspace)` (`project_registry_pk(workspace)`), read by `lambda/shared/project_tables.py` `iter_registered_project_ids(table, *, workspace) -> Iterator[str]`: a strongly consistent Query of the registry through `scoped_query.query_partition` (`ws_table`), every page, projected to `sk`, ids only; a workspace only. Every claim a workspace holds has its registry row (they are written in one transaction), so the registry lists every claim. **Create** (`api/projects.py` `create_project(body, caller, *, workspace=None)`, `shared/project_writes.py` `create_workspace_project`): with a workspace, the `META` keyed `WS#{ws}#PROJECT#{id}` (its `gsi1pk` `WS#{ws}#TYPE#PROJECT`) is checked to be that workspace's, then ONE scoped transaction (`transact_project_writes(…, workspace=, claim=)` through `ws_table`) puts the claim, the `META` and the registry row, each only if absent: all or nothing, so no claim is ever left without its project. A claim already held cancels it with ConditionalCheckFailed at the claim's index, which `shared/ids.py` `write_with_fresh_id(put_index=PROJECT_CLAIM_INDEX)` takes for a collision: a fresh id, once (a second one propagates, as a same-second collision's does); any other cancellation is raised as it came, not retried. The deployment's creates never put a claim (decided); why that is safe is written in 3h. A clash on the `META` or the registry row while the claim is free (cancelled at index 1 or 2) is not retried either: no create makes one, since the claim is written with both, so it means a row left by hand or a bug, and the create fails rather than mint around it. Without a workspace the create sends the one conditional PutItem it always sent (pinned request for request in `api/test/test_project_create_workspace.py`): no claim and no registry row, now or after the switch. No route passes a workspace. **IAM**: none yet (no role passes a workspace); the grant the switch adds is written in 3h below. **Guard** (`shared/test/test_ws_raw_access.py`): the claim's Put, GetItem and DeleteItem are the layer's own; `PROJECT_ID#` joins the unscoped key prefixes (only the layer spells it), `claim_project_id` is called by `shared/project_writes.py` only, and a literal `workspace=None` to `create_workspace_project` or `iter_registered_project_ids` is counted. Every create condition in the project stores (`api/projects.py`, `shared/project_writes.py`, `shared/document_versions.py`, `shared/document_history.py`) now names `project_writes.NEW_ROW_CONDITION` (the same string). **Load**: a workspace create is one three-item transaction (twice the write units of three puts) instead of one PutItem; the claim keys spread over every project id; a workspace's creates share two partitions of their own, its `META` index partition `WS#{ws}#TYPE#PROJECT` and its registry `WS#{ws}#PROJECT_IDS` (one write per create in each). and the conductor re-checks the approver before every step (`lambda/agents/plan_identity.py` `approver_claims`) and once per decide while a wait is outstanding, before any poll (`plan_decide.py` `_approver_gate`, `plan_identity.require_approver`: no group listing), now with `shared/request_workspace.py` `is_current_member` for a workspace plan: an approver disabled or no longer a member of the active workspace stops the plan, and nothing is read. So a wait reads only run rows of a workspace whose member approved the plan and still is, whether it names the run by a literal id or by a reference. IAM: the agents API and the conductor read `voc-workspaces`' `WS#*` rows held to the resolver's projections (`grantWorkspaceMembershipReads`). Without a workspace every request is the one sent before (the mutation suites pin them unchanged, and `lambda/shared/test/test_job_stores_workspace.py` pins a create's and an update's request for request). **Worker events**: a job's invoke event and an execution's input name the workspace only through `lambda/shared/workspace_routing.py` `workspace_payload` (`lambda/api/projects_handler.py` `_job_event`, every project job's; `assist_jobs.start`); no route resolves a workspace for its project yet (3h), so each is exactly the fields it was. The three state machines pass every state's Lambda a `run_scope` object, beside its existing parameters: their first state (`DocRunScope`, `ResearchRunScope`, voc-agent-run's `RunScope`, a Choice on `IsPresent` `$.workspace_id`) sets `$.run_scope` to `{workspace_id}`, or `{}` when the input names none, since a JSONPath parameter whose path the input lacks fails the state; so a deployment run's input is unchanged, and its states' Lambdas get `run_scope: {}`. voc-agent-run's Map passes it to each plan step's prepare and record; the plan step's own event is still exactly what prepare built. A worker reads either shape with `lambda/shared/ws_repo.py` `event_scope` (an invoke's `workspace_id`, or a state's `run_scope`; a field that names no workspace, a `run_scope` that is not an object, or both at once raises `ValueError`, never the deployment). **Fail closed** (`NotPerWorkspaceError`): `job_handler` keys every job-row call by the event's workspace, then refuses the work (`PROJECT_JOB_WORK_STORE`: it reads the deployment's feedback, prompt labels and company context and writes under the deployment's object prefixes), so the job row in the workspace is marked failed and nothing else is read; the document generator's and the research workflow's states refuse the same way, and their error state marks the workspace's job row failed; an assist in a workspace records its job failed without running its work (`ASSIST_WORK_STORE`); voc-agent-run's conductor and its node and persona-panel steps refuse a state that names a workspace before any row is read (`AGENT_RUNS_STORE`: the agent rows stay the deployment's, and a plan takes its workspace from its own `META`, as before), but for its two failure states (`RecordFailure`, `PlanRecordFailure`, which have no Catch): in a workspace they record the failure only on a row whose `workspace_id` is the run's (a plan's META; an agent's row names none, so they only log), and with a malformed `run_scope` (no scope to compare) they only log too, writing no row on a guess: an agent run left running is failed by the heartbeat's stale-run rule (`STALE_RUN_HOURS`), and a plan's execution input never names a workspace (`launch_plan`); a malformed `run_scope` fails every other state without writing. A job's error state with a malformed `run_scope` fails the deployment's job row (`shared/jobs.py` `error_state_scope`; the update's condition writes nothing where there is none), the only one such a job can have, since a workspace's job row is written only with a scope the layer accepts. An assist event whose `pk` is not a partition of its own scope (`WorkspaceScope.owns`, or not a workspace's key for the deployment) is ignored, nothing read or written. **Agent claims**: `lambda/agents/principal.py` `agent_claims(…, workspace=)` adds `voc:workspace` (the claim plans already carry, `agents/plan_identity.py` `WORKSPACE_CLAIM`) for a run that belongs to a workspace; the node context carries the run's scope to the project-creating step; no run belongs to one yet. **Hard delete registry**: a member's assist rows outside a project in a workspace (`owner_pk`) expire a day after they start (`TTL:`), and a workspace's project jobs and their status partitions go with its projects. **Guard** (`lambda/shared/test/test_ws_raw_access.py`): `STATUS#` joins the unscoped key prefixes (counted once, in `project_keys.py`); `lambda/shared/jobs.py` keeps its four `get_jobs_table` handles, each now used only through `table_view` / `get_scoped_item`; `lambda/agents/plan_waits.py` drops its `SOURCE_RUN#`; a literal `workspace=None` to `create_job`, `update_job_status`, `claim_job_execution`, `recover_job_execution_claim`, `notify_project_job` or `JobContext`, or through its module to `assist_jobs.start`, `start_owned`, `get`, `owner_pk` or `plan_waits.poll`, is counted (none today); no request-facing module calls `event_scope`. **Load**: unchanged on the deployment's path; in a workspace each status is one partition per workspace (`WS#{ws}#STATUS#{status}`), as `STATUS#{status}` is for the whole deployment today, and nothing reads it. Tests: `lambda/shared/test/test_job_stores_workspace.py` (moto: the deployment and two workspaces), `lambda/agents/test/test_state_machine_run_scope.py`, the document generator's and the research steps' handler suites, `voc-datalake/lib/stacks/job-workflows-run-scope.test.ts` (both CDK state machines pinned state by state) |

On success the outcome is recorded beside it (`…#done`, `deidentified`) and the row counts once (`project_rows_deidentified`, so the outcome records and the count agree, but for a step cut by a crash between the write and its outcome record, which a rerun does not redo). A row that changed meanwhile is read again, whole, its changes worked out again, at most 3 times; if something still holds the address the job fails (`RowKeptChanging`) and an admin reruns it (every step is idempotent: a de-identified row matches nothing). Crash safety: the attempt is recorded before the write, so a crash after it leaves a record, never a change without one. **Product documents** (`ws/{ws}/projects/{id}/product_docs/`, `shared/project_keys.py` `product_docs_prefix`): in a workspace an upload is `.md`, `.txt` or an image (the extractor's rules take no PDF there) and an image is never extracted (marked failed, 3f), so each record (`DOCS_PAGE`, 5 a step, projected to its key, `s3_raw_key`, `content_type`, `status`, `s3_extracted_key` and `filename`) is judged onto one of two lists, each capped at 200 keys with its own `more`, so a match is never crowded out: `files_needing_review` when its `filename`, or its text, holds the address (`casefold`; the extracted text when the record says the extractor wrote it, `status` `ready` and `s3_extracted_key` under its folder, else the `.md` or `.txt` upload itself), which also closes the workspace's exports (they copy the document); `files_not_checked` (runs.py, carried while it holds one, counted in `product_docs_not_checked`) when it cannot be judged: an image (its folder listed once, however many it holds), an upload neither text nor image, text not extracted that is no `.md` or `.txt`, a file over 16 MiB, a record whose upload key is not under its folder (its folder listed), or a key the role cannot read (with no `s3:ListBucket`, a missing key, a record pending, failed or never uploaded, answers 403: listed, never a failure). Nothing there is changed or deleted. A document not checked does not close the workspace's exports: its copy in an export's zip stays until the zip expires (10 days), unless another match closed them. Text is decoded strictly (UTF-16 by a byte-order mark or a NUL byte, else UTF-8); text that decodes neither way, and any answer S3 gives instead of the object (a KMS refusal, an archived object), is "not checked"; a failure with no answer (a timeout) fails the job. A free-text research note `author` matches when it holds the address anywhere and is replaced whole; a name equal to the address cut to 120 characters (how a long address was stored as a name before) matches too. **Not reached**: the deployment's own projects (planned, 3g-d below), a reviewer's daily write counter (`DCRATE#{share}#{digest}`: its key holds an unsalted, pseudonymous digest of the address, left to its two-day TTL), the reviewer's board-pool account (it keeps their email; planned, 3g-r), a live member's address copied again from the user directory on a later member write (a member's complete erasure is deleting the user), thread text that names the person, the participants (3g2). An id not shaped like one (a registry row, a link, a series, a product document) is skipped and counted (`project_ids_malformed`). **Lineage** (`lineage.py`, phase `workspace_lineage`, after `lineage`): `copies.py` notes the workspace of every workspace item it deletes (`runs.py` `LINEAGE_WORKSPACES`, carried across hand-overs while it holds one), in a retention window, the deployment's erasure by email or a workspace's own erasure; once the run de-identified a board request, or its list passed 500 workspaces, the phase walks every listed workspace instead (any workspace's artifact may cite a board request; a workspace's own job never does either). In each: every project its registry lists (a deleted one too: its rows stay until its delete sweeps them) and then its company persona library, the project's own partition (`project_partitions` kind `project`; its document versions hold no id lists) read by Query pages of 100 filtered to rows carrying `input_feedback_ids`, `evidence_feedback_ids` or `input_feature_request_ids`; a review or cited id looked up as that workspace's tombstone (`{ws}#{feedback_id}`), a board request id as the deployment's (`feature_requests` / `{id}`: the boards are the deployment's), each mark written through `ws_table` (`mark_artifact`'s `scope`). The deployment's pass sends the same Scan as before and leaves a row whose partition is a workspace's (`deployment_rows`). **Walk** (`shared/project_walk.py`, which the export shares; handler.py `_step_walk`): each phase is steps, one Query page each with its writes, under the job's time budget and `MAX_PAGES`; each cursor state has its own step; a hand-over between steps carries the step's cursor (the listing's cursor, the page of workspace ids, and in the workspace where its link registry, links, project registry, projects and the project's part are: at most 100 ids each); a carried id of the wrong shape, a carried key that is not a map, a one-workspace job's cursor naming another workspace, or a Query that answers with its own start key fails the job. A workspace's own erasure now walks `items`, (by email) `workspace_projects`, `exports` and `workspace_lineage`. Written limits: the run's lists travel in every hand-over's payload, against the 256 KB limit the code assumes (about 30 bytes a workspace id). `LINEAGE_WORKSPACES` keeps at most 500 (`MAX_LINEAGE_WORKSPACES`): past it the run sets `lineage_all_workspaces` and the phase walks every listed workspace instead, as after a board request's de-identification; `ERASED_WORKSPACES` (exports.py) is not capped, so a run that reached about 8,000 workspaces would not fit; the review lists keep 200 keys each. The listing is sorted by name, then id (no other key lists every workspace), and a rename (7.40.00) moves a workspace's listing row to its new name's key, which can put it behind the page already read; a workspace created meanwhile can land there too. So the walk of the listing (both phases, `workspaces.py`) tolerates a moved row: a rename stamps `renamed_at` on the listing row it puts (`shared/workspace_rename.py`, the audit row's time); the walk's cursor carries when it began, less 5 minutes for the clocks' drift (`SKEW`); when the listing ends, one strongly consistent Query of `WORKSPACES` filtered to `renamed_at` or `created_at` since then gives the workspaces it walks again (every step is idempotent), the re-walk's page keys and the rows it walked carried in the cursor (at most 100: `MAX_MOVED`). The pass is repeated from the previous pass's start (less `SKEW`) while a pass finds a row stamped since its own start that the previous pass did not walk with that same stamp, at most 3 passes (`MAX_REWALKS`); past them, or past 100 such rows in a pass, the job fails (`ListingKeptMoving`) and an admin runs it again. One case is not walked: a rename landing between two pages of the last pass (a listing over 1 MB is read in more than one page) that moves its row behind them. A listing row naming no workspace id is counted (`project_ids_malformed`), and a listing page that answers with its own start key fails the job. **IAM** (`lib/stacks/retention-worker.ts`): `dynamodb:Query` on voc-projects (the table, no index) with `dynamodb:LeadingKeys` `WS#{one id}#PROJECT_IDS`, `#PROJECT#*`, `#DCOMMENTS#*`, `#DOCSHARE_IDS` and `#DOCSHARE#*` (`RETENTION_PROJECT_PARTITIONS`), and on voc-workspaces with `WORKSPACES` only; `s3:GetObject` of `ws/{one id}/projects/*/product_docs/raw/*.md`, `…/raw/*.txt` and `…/extracted/*` only (`WORKSPACE_PRODUCT_DOC_READS`; no `s3:ListBucket`, so a missing key answers 403, which the code takes for "cannot be judged"); UpdateItem on voc-projects narrowed from every attribute to two statements (`dynamodb:Attributes`): `LINEAGE_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` (the key, the three id lists, `erased_evidence`) on any partition (the deployment's lineage Scan marks any row it finds), and `PEOPLE_UPDATE_ATTRIBUTES` (the key, `author`, `replies`, `status_by`, `audience`, `created_by_name`, `revoked_by`, `revoked`, `owner_email`, `members`, `research_notes`) on one workspace id's `PROJECT#*`, `DCOMMENTS#*` and `DOCSHARE#*` only (3g-d adds the deployment's partitions to it); a test records every update the job sends against its statement, and lineage.py and `project_people.py` each refuse to send one naming another attribute. What IAM cannot pin (TENETS.md tenet 6): which workspace (`{one id}` is any), the sort key; only the code reads the listed rows and writes a workspace's through its layer. **Load** (per erasure by email, once per job, never on a request path; `.kiro/steering/tech.md`): one Query of the listing per 100 workspaces, and at the listing's end one filtered Query of it per re-walk pass (one when nothing moved) plus the walk again of each workspace renamed or created meanwhile; per workspace one link registry Query per 100 links and one Query per link, one registry Query per 100 projects; per project about four Queries (its META, its product document records per 5, its personas per 100, its series list per 100) plus one per series' thread page of 100; per changed row one UpdateItem and two PutItems (and a Query and another UpdateItem per retry); per product document at most one GetObject (its extracted text, or its text upload), none for an image or another kind; per persona page of 100 one Query. So a deployment-wide erasure by email costs about 4 Queries per project plus 1 per series and 1 per link, and a GetObject per text product document, across every workspace (the persona library counted as one more project). Per lineage workspace: one registry Query per 100 projects, one Query per 100 rows of each project's partition (filtered: reads are billed for the whole page) and one BatchGetItem per page of ids; a run that de-identified a board request walks every workspace so. All on the walked workspaces' own partitions. **Export (7.39 follow-ups)**: `projects.unclassified_fields` keeps at most 50 entries, its `(more)` bucket among them (49 names; it kept 51 entries), and the manifest's `excluded.project_fields` note and `project_fields.py` say the secret-name check reads key names only, never values; the export pages its registry through `shared/project_walk.py`. a link's audience, a link left admitting nobody revoked and a new one made, a reviewer's thread and reply, a member's thread with a reviewer's reply, a name that is the address on an author, a reply, `status_by` and a link's creator and revoker, an owner's and a member's address, case-insensitively; one more Query when nothing moved; a row renamed once walked once more; the pass cap and the moved-row cap fail the job; the hand-over carries the start and the re-walk state), `lambda/api/test/test_workspaces_rename_routes.py` (the listing row stamped `renamed_at`), `lambda/api/test/test_document_review_routes.py` (a nameless member never named by their address), `test_product_context_mutation.py` (the prefix), `test_retention_properties.py` and `test_named_workspace_erasure.py` (the phases), `lambda/jobs/workspace_export/test/test_projects.py` (the bucket), `lib/stacks/processing-stack-retention.test.ts` (the grants pinned) |

A "not found" counts as gone only once a strongly consistent read of the person agrees (one more try otherwise), and a person found gone has a cut erasure's tail finished before its entry is forgotten (`finish_erasure`: the memberships an add committed late, and in a workspace the guard's index row, unlisted while no guard is back); a guard's index row kept after every try conflicted, with no guard back, keeps the entry saved, counted (`participant_index_rows_kept`) and skipped for the run like a refusal, for the next erasure to unlist. The people index's Query follows its pages, at most 10 (past them the job fails, named); without voc-workspaces the deployment's job says `participants_walked: false`. A refusal that does not clear within the run (a workspace person holding asks or tokens, `NotPerWorkspaceError`, which cannot happen until the asks move into their workspace; a person still there after two "not found") saves the entry again on the job's own row (its `ttl` pushed forward), is counted (`participants_not_erased`) and skipped for the rest of the run (the cursor's `refused`, at most 500: past that the job fails, named; lifted before the asks move, 3d); the job goes on and completes with `participants_complete: false`, and every later erasure of the address tries it again. Any other error (a throttle, a service fault or a configuration error: `ParticipantNotErased`; an AWS error) fails the job, the ids still saved. **Saved first**: the ids a step resolves (the deployment's scope, or one listing page's) are added to a string set in the job's own partition, `JOB#erasure` (a workspace's `WS#{ws}#JOB#erasure`) / `PARTICIPANTS#{sha256 of the normalised address}` (`people`: `{workspace id or deployment}#{person id}`, `ADD` and a conditional `DELETE`, so two jobs of one address never drop each other's and a gone row is never re-created) before any is erased, and each is taken off once erased (from the job's row and, for a workspace's person, from that workspace's). The deployment's walk also reads each listed workspace's saved row, in the same step as its guard, and erases what it names; when the walk ends the phase reads its own row again and erases whom it still names, at most `MAX_SAVED` (500) at a time. Every save sets the row's `ttl` 30 days ahead (a failed job's row is removed by the table's TTL, usually within a few days after its `ttl`; a refused entry is saved again, its `ttl` 30 days past the last run that refused it); an emptied row's `ttl` (the job's, or a workspace's the deployment's walk emptied) is brought to one day ahead; the key's hash is the guard's own, unsalted. only the code holds a job to its own): GetItem, Query and ConditionCheckItem of `EMAIL#*`, `PERSON#*`; the people index's Query of `PEOPLE`, `WS#{one id}#PEOPLE`; PutItem `AUDIT#tombstone#participant`, `AUDIT#delete`; Every run ever started is read again by each such erasure: the reads grow with the engineering runs, a Scale note. One person's memory erasure is one step: it reads their partition and every partition their `REF#` rows name whole (at the store's caps about 12,500 rows per connection), with no hand-over inside it; a step cut by the Lambda's timeout fails the job, and a rerun finishes it (follow-up **3g3-b-paging**: a deadline check that hands over between partitions, the `REF#` partitions still to do carried in the cursor). Follow-up **3g3-b-index**, a reverse index `WS#default#CITES#<feedback_id>` / `<run or claim>` written at a run's start and a claim's create, read by the erasure instead of the walk; its trigger: a month's runs list passing 1,000 runs. **The agents' memory** (`lambda/jobs/retention/agent_memory.py`, phase `agent_memory`, after the participants; the deployment's erasure by email without a source only): the memory keys a person by their Cognito `sub` (the engineering stores' person `Actor` is the caller's `subject`, the token's `sub`), so the address is resolved with one `AdminGetUser`, the address as its `Username`: the pool signs in by email or username (`signInAliases: { email: true, username: true }`, core-auth.ts), and Cognito resolves an alias wherever a username is asked for; an email is an alias once verified, one account at most; every pool since #105 matches it whatever its case, so an address the users API stored in another spelling is found; a pool made before #105 (`omitUserPoolUsernameConfiguration`) matches the exact spelling, so the job asks again lower-cased and a third spelling is missed there (erase again with the stored spelling). Only an account whose email is the address and is verified is taken; one found by a username equal to the address that holds it unverified is not erased and is counted (`agent_memory_accounts_not_erased`). The subs are saved first (`JOB#erasure` / `AGENT_MEMORY#{sha256 of the address}`, 30-day `ttl`, emptied to 1 day once done), so a failed job or a hand-over is finished by the next erasure of the address even once the account is gone; each person is erased by the store's own erasure, moved into `lambda/engineering/agent_memory/erasure.py` (`erase_person_memories` now calls it): its tombstone first under the job's legal basis (actor: the admin who started the job, `kind` `erasure`, the job id), then, when any row of theirs is found, the delete record, their name de-identified in other scopes and their partition deleted. The tombstone is a lasting refusal, also for a person the memory never held; an operator lifts it only by deleting that row with the account's own credentials. Counted: `agent_memory_people_erased` (rows deleted), `agent_memory_people_refused` (the tombstone only); the job and audit rows and `GET /settings/erasure` say `agent_memory_walked` and `agent_memory_complete` (walked, no account left unverified); the rerun rule: every such erasure whose row does not say `agent_memory_complete: true`. **Grants** (`lib/stacks/retention-worker.ts`, built only with the engineering agents): in `RetentionEngineeringPolicy`, a customer-managed policy attached to the retention role alone, Query and UpdateItem of `ISSUE_RUN#default` held to `pk`, `sk`, `feedback_id`, `engineering_run_id`, `erased_evidence`; Query of `WS#default#RUNLIST#*` and `WS#default#RUN#*` and UpdateItem of `WS#default#RUN#*` held to `pk`, `sk`, `feedback`, `erased_evidence` (`SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES`, no returned values); Query and an attribute-held UpdateItem in `WS#default#AMEM#*`; `cognito-idp:AdminGetUser` on the user pool. In the role's own inline policy: PutItem of `AUDIT#tombstone#agent_memory_person` and `AUDIT#delete`, PutItem in `WS#default#AMEM#*` held to a confirmation row's attributes, DeleteItem in `WS#default#AMEM#*`. IAM cannot pin the sort key: the memory's put and delete reach any memory row, the runs' update any row of a run's partition (two attributes); the code holds them to the person's rows and each run's `META`. voc-engineering is under the audit-row guard (voc-retention puts only those two audit partitions; no role changes or removes an `AUDIT#` row there). In every build the role's statements that neither delete nor put (the boards' reads and update, the memberships' Scan, update and listing Query, the participants' update) moved into `RetentionReadsPolicy`, a customer-managed policy attached to the role alone, so its inline policy, which keeps every delete and audit put, holds about 7,960 of 10,240 characters with the engineering agents (CDK split it at about 9,780). The bundle gains `engineering/__init__.py`, `engineering/rows.py` and `engineering/agent_memory/{__init__,erasure}.py`, nothing else of that tree. **Log attribution** (7.76.00's follow-up, every deployment): a deployment-wide erasure's or retention window's step inside one workspace (its items and their tombstones, form test responses, memberships, projects walk, participants, exports and lineage marks) is attributed to that workspace (`lambda/jobs/retention/workspaces.py` `inside`, `lambda/shared/usage_meter.py` `attributed`: for the step only, then back), so its lines show in that workspace's trail and not in the deployment admins' System logs; a retention window now runs as the deployment (it was unattributed). Not reached (gaps): a run started between 7.47.00 and 7.51.00 (2026-11-16 to 17) has no runs-list row; the review ids a memory version keeps (`VER#…` `feedback_ids`, create-only); a deleted account's memories; an account whose email changed since, or whose email is unverified and not its username (an admin also erases by the account's current, verified address); a run's `goal` a person typed and the agents' session transcripts, which may carry a review's words. |

(2) the owner; (3) the workspace's admin role, the admin bypass, never the deployment's `admins`; (4) on a `public` project an editor (public means the workspace's members); (5) the members map; (6) the workspace's viewer role caps 2 to 5 at view; then a delegated credential at edit, as in the deployment. **Scope checks** (`WorkspaceMembershipError`, a `NotPerWorkspaceError`, a 500): `require_membership` refuses a workspace's project without a membership, with another workspace's, another person's or nobody's (a blank subject), and the deployment's with one; `resolve_access` refuses a `META` of another scope (a `WS#` `pk` read as the deployment's; a workspace's read with a `pk` that is not its own project partition, none included). **The gate** (`lambda/shared/project_gate.py`): `read_gate_meta` reads the scope's `META` (`WS#{ws}#PROJECT#{id}`); `require_project_level` and `require_viewable_pin` answer a caller `workspace_admits` does not admit the one 404 of a missing project before any read (never 403), and decide a member's on the read `META` (a workspace admin's too: a missing, deleting or deleted project is a 404, and a pin costs that one read). `membership` is a required keyword beside `workspace` in `resolve_access`, `sharing_summary`, `require_project_level` and `require_viewable_pin`; every deployment call spells `membership=None`. Its refusal (`PROJECT_GATE_STORE`) moves to the callers that hold no membership yet: the persona copy, the voice sessions, the persona grounding (as before) and now the participants' project check (`shared/engagement_identity.py` `require_project_access`, which relied on the gate's read refusing). The deployment's path is unchanged, `public` for every signed-in user included, pending [decision 23](#decisions). **The answers' sharing fields** (`api/projects.py`): `sharing_summary`, `_with_sharing_fields` and `_without_emails_unless_manager` take `workspace` and `membership` and pass them on, and `create_project` takes `membership` beside its `workspace` and checks it before anything is written, so a workspace's create answers its sharing fields in its own scope (the scope check found it deciding them on the deployment's policy). **The keyword guard** (`lambda/shared/test/test_project_workspace_keyword.py`): project_access.py's own literal None goes and `api/projects.py` gains 3 (`LITERAL_NONE` 189 to 191); the policy's calls (`MEMBERSHIP_CALLS`) must pass `membership=` by name, which the ratchet does not count (it always stands beside a `workspace=None`, and `require_membership` refuses any other pairing); `MEMBERSHIP_BUILDERS` holds every `WorkspaceMembership(` to project_access.py and workspace.py. Tests: `lambda/shared/test/test_project_gate_workspace.py` (red on 7.100.00), `test_project_stores_workspace.py`, `test_workspace.py`, the guard's. Not yet: the routes passing their resolved workspace and membership (the rest of 3h). The callers still on the deployment's policy, each guarded by the scope check (a workspace's `META` reaching one is refused, never decided as the deployment's) and lifted with its route's slice: `api/projects.py`'s list filter (`list_projects`: the `resolve_access`, `sharing_summary` and email redaction of each listed project), `get_project`, `_viewable_ids`, `get_project_chat_context` and `get_project_members`; and `api/projects_handler.py`'s gate (`_project_access_for`), viewable filter and create route.. Follow-up for the create route's slice: refuse an agent caller in a workspace, as the gate does (`create_project` writes for one today, no route reaching it) |
| 7.102.00 | Phase 3's switch, slice 3h-S1b of [the project plan](#the-project-plan-phase-3) (plumbing, no behaviour change): **the rest of the project-adjacent stores join the keyword guard**. `GUARDED` (`lambda/shared/test/test_project_workspace_keyword.py`) goes from 21 modules to 39: the jobs and their notices, the assists, the personas' stores (the copy's `source_workspace` too), the voice sessions, the human documents, the decisions, the deleted sources' marks, the participants' gate wrappers, the ballots' and plan waits' wrappers and the product docs' extractor. Every caller names its scope: 78 production calls that took the default spell `workspace=None`, the deployment as before, and `api/projects_handler.py`'s four `version_html` calls pass `store.workspace`. **The ratchet** (`LITERAL_NONE`) rises once, 191 in 27 modules to 265 in 35 (net +74), each module's calls in its note. The full list, the tests and the counts are in the [CHANGELOG](../CHANGELOG.md)'s 7.102.00. Not yet: the routes' and jobs' resolved scope (the rest of 3h); the guard's blind spots (below); and the stores still defaulting to the deployment outside the projects' modules, the participants (`participants_store.py`, `participants_lists.py`, `participants_keys.py`, the asks, intake and erasure), `library_keys.py`, `memory_store.py`, the radar stores, `agents_store.py` and the other phases' stores: the same class of risk, each moved by its own phase, so not in this slice (follow-up **3h-S1c**, below). |
| 7.103.00 | Phase 3's switch, slice 3h-S3a of [the project plan](#the-project-plan-phase-3) (no production behaviour change): **the project core routes take their workspace from one seam**, `lambda/shared/project_request_scope.py` `project_scope`, which answers the deployment while its `RESOLVE` flag is off (until the flip, 3h-S12, turns it on with `projects_switched`). `api/projects_handler.py` matches each route by its exact `(method, template)`: the 20 `THREADED_ROUTES` (list and details, create, get, chat context, update, visibility, members, owner, the documents' create, edit, versions, restore and duplicate, the personas' edit, note and protection) act in the workspace; in a workspace each of the 90 `NOT_YET` routes outside `/review/` is refused with its slice before any read: S3b product documents, S3c (company personas, the board, persona copy and grounding, decisions, validation, voice), S4 deletes, S5 comments, review links and reviewer routes, S6 avatars, S7 prototypes, S8 jobs and assists. The Documents Library and the reviewer routes never resolve a workspace. In a workspace a member is added only while a member of the workspace, the owner must be its editor or admin, a viewer and an agent create nothing. The routes stay in `cross_workspace_pending.py`; `lambda/api/test/test_project_routes_workspace.py` proves them with the seam on. `LITERAL_NONE` 265 to 230. Details: the [CHANGELOG](../CHANGELOG.md)'s 7.103.00. |

Today one deployment is one tenant ([TENETS.md](../TENETS.md), tenet 6 gap: "One deployment is one tenant"). Every
signed-in person sees the same feedback, settings, projects and agents, and the Cognito `admins` group administers
all of it. Multi-workspace keeps one deployment (one set of tables, buckets and Lambdas) and puts a workspace on
every row and object, so a team only ever sees its own data.

> **Words.** In this page a *workspace* is the tenant: a team's whole copy of the product (its feedback, settings,
> projects, personas, agents, Radar, boards, memory). The existing docs also say "workspace" for the shared content
> of a deployment (for example "workspace deletes" in TENETS.md D5, or the Prioritization "workspace scoring setup").
> With this design, all of that content belongs to exactly one workspace.

## Decisions

| # | Topic | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Isolation | Soft isolation: enforced in code, not by per-workspace IAM roles |
| 2 | Platform admin | A top-level role. Sees every workspace and can enter one with full admin rights; every entry is audited |
| 3 | Membership | One Cognito user, many workspaces, a different role in each |
| 4 | Invites | Only workspace admins and platform admins invite. An invitee may already have an account |
| 5 | Creating workspaces | Only platform admins |
| 6 | Configuration | Everything per workspace: brand config, categories, dimensions, sources, models, plugins, menu, Radar |
| 7 | Branding | One logo and colour scheme for the whole deployment; no per-workspace chrome |
| 8 | Credentials | Per workspace, encrypted with the customer-managed KMS key. Decision 19 (7.12.00) replaced "one Secrets Manager secret per workspace" with one SSM Parameter Store SecureString per plugin namespace, standard tier (free, at most 4 KB each): see [Credentials](#credentials) |
| 9 | Cost | Cost reported per workspace |
| 10 | Quotas | None for now; workspaces share Bedrock capacity (a busy workspace can slow the others) |
| 11 | Delete | Hard delete of a whole workspace, after offering an export (how it meets D5 is [settled](#tenet-questions-settled-owner-2026-11-12): the controller's own request, D5) |
| 12 | Existing deployments | Not migrated: multi-workspace starts on a fresh deployment |
| 13 | Radar | Per workspace |
| 14 | Sharing (later) | Workspaces stay separate. Workspace B imports from A through a plugin that A approved |
| 15 | URL | `/workspace/{slug}/…`; the slug is fixed when the workspace is created |
| 16 | Repository | Superseded: a branch of this sample, keeping its version line and CHANGELOG rule |
| 17 | Workspace delete | The data controller's own request: stated basis, the slug typed, an audit row, a tombstone per deleted item, the export offered first ([settled](#tenet-questions-settled-owner-2026-11-12)) |
| 18 | Imports and erasure | An erasure in the source does not reach an importer's copy: each workspace is its own boundary (TENETS.md, D8) |
| 19 | Credentials store (owner, 2026-11-12) | A workspace's credentials are SSM Parameter Store SecureStrings, standard tier, under the customer-managed KMS key, one per plugin namespace (`/voc/ws/{ws}/credentials/{plugin}`), not a Secrets Manager secret per workspace; it replaces decision 8's store ([Credentials](#credentials)). The deployment's shared secret is unchanged |
| 20 | Workspace audit rows | Their partition key starts with `AUDIT#` (`AUDIT#WS#{ws}`) |
| 21 | Rollout (owner, 2026-11-12) | Fresh deployments only, each with its upgrade note: the 7.00.00 index re-key, and decision 19's store (an existing deployment's per-workspace `voc/ws/*` secrets are not migrated, [Upgrading](#upgrading)) |
| 22 | An unmark after the switch (7.100.00, 3h-S0) | Settled by design, no longer an open question: the switch is on only while its row names the backfill's current, complete marker (`marker_at` equal to the marker's `completed_at`; `lambda/shared/project_scope.py` `projects_switched`), so the backfill's `--unmark`, the marker's reset on a rollback below 7.48.00, a restore that brings back no marker or another one, or a re-backfill switches the projects off until the switch's check runs again (a restore to after the switch brings the row and its marker back together, and stays switched on); the `--unmark` refuses nothing. The 3h readers treat "off after on" as a refusal by name, never a fallback to the deployment's scope |
| 23 | The deployment's `public` and workspace-only accounts (7.101.00, 3h-S2) | **Open, for the owner.** Should the deployment's `public` visibility stop covering accounts that belong only to workspaces (an account invited into a workspace, with no other reason to see the deployment's projects)? Today `public` on a deployment project grants edit to every signed-in user of the user pool, and a workspace's members are signed-in users. **Default until decided: unchanged** (the deployment's policy, `shared/project_access.py` `_base_role`, is exactly as before 7.101.00). A workspace's own project is decided from its membership alone, `public` meaning its members (3h-S2) |

## Upgrading

Multi-workspace starts on a fresh deployment (decision 12): an existing deployment does not take the release that
re-keys gsi4 and gsi5 (7.00.00, the processor's slice above) in place, and nothing is migrated.

- Why: `gsi4-by-feedback-id` and `gsi5-by-channel` move from the raw `feedback_id` and `source_channel` to
  `gsi4pk` and `gsi5pk`. Changing an index's key is a delete and a create, and DynamoDB allows one index change per
  table update, so CloudFormation refuses the update. And items stored before carry no `gsi4pk` / `gsi5pk`: they
  would be in neither index, so every lookup by id (`GET /feedback/{id}`, the feedback edits, the Data Explorer, the
  assistant) and every channel filter would miss them.
- An operator who must upgrade an existing deployment re-keys in steps, one deploy each: remove gsi4; add gsi4 on
  `gsi4pk`; remove gsi5; add gsi5 on `gsi5pk` (pinning `lib/stacks/core-stack.ts` to each intermediate shape), and
  backfills every `FEEDBACK#` item with `gsi4pk = FEEDBACK#{feedback_id}` and, where `source_channel` is not empty,
  `gsi5pk = CHANNEL#{source_channel}` (a paged Scan and conditional updates) before relying on either index. Until
  the backfill ends, reads by id and by channel miss the items not yet updated.
- Credentials (decision 19, since 7.12.00): a workspace's credentials live in SSM Parameter Store
  (`/voc/ws/{ws}/credentials/{plugin}`) and their config lists in voc-aggregates rows (`WS#{ws}#PLUGIN_CONFIG`), and nothing reads the Secrets Manager
  secrets `voc/ws/{ws}/credentials` that 6.09.00 created per workspace. They are not migrated: this release is for a
  fresh deployment (decision 21). An existing deployment that takes it anyway must save each workspace's
  credentials, app connectors and scrapers again in that workspace, then delete the old `voc/ws/*` secrets by hand
  (each still bills about $0.40 a month until deleted). The deployment's shared secret (`SECRETS_ARN`) is unchanged.
- Conversation owners (since 7.19.00): a workspace's list of conversation owners (`WS#{ws}#CONVERSANTS`) is written
  by every save from 7.19.00 on, so a fresh deployment (decision 21) needs nothing. A deployment that ran 7.15.00 to
  7.18.00 with workspaces in use runs the one-off backfill once after the deploy, from `voc-datalake/`:
  `.venv/bin/python scripts/conversations/backfill_conversants.py` (a dry run: it prints each owner it would list),
  then the same with `--apply`. It scans voc-conversations once for the `CONV#` rows of `WS#*#USER#*` partitions and
  puts each owner's row if absent (`first_at`: their earliest `created_at` there), through the chat routes' own
  writer, so it is safe to run again and keeps a row a save wrote meanwhile. The table: `--conversations-table`,
  else the `CONVERSATIONS_TABLE` environment, else the core stack's `ConversationsTableName` output (`--stack`,
  `--region`). It needs Scan, GetItem and PutItem on the table and the KMS key's use.

## Model

The pool model: one deployment, and every row and object carries its workspace.

- **Workspace id**: `ws_<ulid>`, plus a unique, immutable `slug` for URLs.
- **DynamoDB**: every partition key is prefixed `WS#{ws}#…` (for example `WS#{ws}#SOURCE#webscraper`), and so is
  every GSI partition key (`WS#{ws}#DATE#2026-01-15`). No GSI is ever queried without the prefix.
- **S3**: `ws/{ws}/raw/{source}/{yyyy}/{mm}/{dd}/…`, `ws/{ws}/avatars/…`, `ws/{ws}/exports/…`, and so on for every
  prefix described in [Data Lake Structure](data-lake-structure.md). The raw tier keeps its contract (write-once,
  `If-None-Match`, nothing expires) under each workspace's prefix.
- **KMS**: the one customer-managed key stays (tenet 7); where the platform encrypts data itself it passes
  `workspace_id` as encryption context.

### Control plane

The `voc-workspaces` table lives in `VocCoreStack` and the Lambdas for workspaces, members and the platform console
in `VocApiStack`: the sample keeps to five stacks ([Deployment Guide](deployment.md#cdk-stacks)), so the control plane
gets no stack of its own.

| PK | SK | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `WS#{ws}` | `META` | name, slug, status (`active` or `deleting`), created_by, created_at, secret_status (`ready` from creation since decision 19; it no longer reflects a secret: see Credentials); a trial's `trial` map since 7.71.00 ([Trial workspaces](#trial-workspaces)) |
| `WS#{ws}` | `MEMBER#{sub}` | role (`admin`, `editor` or `viewer`), invited_by, joined_at; since 6.10.00 a member added through the members routes also carries the email and name Cognito had then (removed by an erasure by email since 6.11.00); since 7.71.00 an optional `playbook`, the role playbook suggested when they were added (offered first on their Home, [playbooks.md](playbooks.md)) |
| `USER#{sub}` | `WS#{ws}` | the inverse row behind "my workspaces" (name, slug, role), written, changed and deleted in the same transaction as `MEMBER#` |
| `WS#{ws}` | `INVITE#{id}` | planned, not written: email, role, invited_by, expires_at, and `ttl` (the table's TTL attribute). Since 6.10.00 an add is immediate for a new email too (Invites), so nothing has an invite to accept; the row stays reserved, allowed `ttl`, for a later invite that waits for acceptance |
| `WS#{ws}` | `NOTICE#{sub}` | the member notice's daily guard (Invites; `lambda/shared/member_notice.py`, with `-c emailFrom` only): `sent_at` and `ttl` (a day on), no address or name. The only other row allowed `ttl` (`lambda/shared/test/test_platform_workspaces.py` pins both; the exception awaits the owner's agreement: [Tenet questions, settled](#tenet-questions-settled-owner-2026-11-12)); a workspace's hard delete removes it with the partition's other rows. A deleted user's row is not removed with their memberships: it stays until its `ttl` (24 h) plus DynamoDB's TTL deletion lag (up to a few days), holding the sub only (D5) |
| `SLUG#{slug}` | `META` | slug → workspace id (`workspace_id`, created_at); uniqueness by a conditional put |
| `WORKSPACES` | `{name, case-folded}#{ws}` | the platform console's listing (workspace_id, name, slug, status, created_at; `renamed_at` once renamed, 7.43.00): one partition, sorted by name, so the list is a paged Query, never a Scan. Written once per created workspace and read by platform admins and the retention worker's walks only; a later rename or status change moves or updates it too |
| `PLUGIN#{plugin_id}` | `WS#{ws}` | a plugin of the deployment the workspace enabled (enabled_by, enabled_at; since 6.18.00, Per-workspace configuration): one partition per plugin, which its scheduled ingestor reads to fan out over the workspaces that enabled it; deleted when it is disabled. None is written until the hold lifts (enabling answers 409; Per-workspace configuration below) |
| `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` | `{iso}#{random}` | the workspace's creation (since 6.09.00), membership changes, platform-admin entries, export and delete, share grants: an `AUDIT#` partition, so the audit-row guard (`voc-datalake/lib/utils/audit-row-guard.ts`) lets no role change or remove one |

Creating a workspace (6.09.00, `POST /platform/workspaces`, `lambda/shared/platform_workspaces.py`) writes the
`SLUG#`, `META`, `MEMBER#`, `USER#`, `WORKSPACES` and `AUDIT#WS#` rows in one transaction, each put conditional on
a new key, so a taken slug (409) writes nothing and no workspace is ever half-written in the table.

- **Platform admins** are the Cognito group `platform-admins`, bootstrapped at deploy time the way the first admin
  user is today ([README](../README.md#-initial-login)). Only they create workspaces and see the platform console
  (every workspace, its members and its cost).
- Platform-admin membership is managed on its own, before the group could grant anything (6.07.00 read the group,
  but with no workspace in the table it granted nothing until 6.09.00 let platform admins create workspaces). Built
  in 6.08.00, before any route could create a workspace or a membership:
  - a platform-admin route to grant and revoke the membership, `PUT /users/{username}/platform-admin`
    (`{"platform_admin": true|false}`; platform admins only, granted only to an administrator, audited in
    `AUDIT#platform-admins`, in the route-gate inventory). Since 6.13.00 the web app shows it to platform admins, with
    a box per user (since 7.54.00 in the person's editor on Administration → Privacy & access → Users, its Platform admin section);
  - in the users API: demoting a user also revokes it; demoting, disabling, enabling, deleting or resetting the
    password of a platform admin is for platform admins only (403); and no request may leave the deployment without
    an enabled platform admin (409, also for a platform admin revoking themselves as the last one).
  (Cognito drops a deleted user's group memberships itself; the users API records that delete as a revoke.) As with
  the last-admin guard, these checks read before they write, so two concurrent requests can both pass them.
  Since 6.09.00, the change that creates workspaces, one live check (`lambda/shared/platform_admin.py`: the token's
  group, the live Cognito membership and an enabled account) serves the users API, the `/platform` routes and
  entering a workspace as a platform admin, which runs it on each fill of the 30 s membership cache that would
  elevate the caller: a revoke or a disable refuses new entries at once and ends a cached one within 30 s (a
  failed check enters with the member's own role, or answers the 404 for a non-member).
- Today's `admins` group no longer grants anything inside a workspace: a person's role in a workspace comes only from
  its `MEMBER#` row.

## Request flow

1. A person signs in (one Cognito user pool) and the web app calls `GET /me/workspaces`. With exactly one workspace
   the picker is skipped; with none, it says so and names who can invite them. Since 6.12.00 (the table above) the
   picker and the one-workspace skip are built; a person with no workspace still gets the app as before workspaces,
   because the data path is not keyed by workspace yet. The "no workspace, ask who can invite you" screen comes when
   the deployment-wide mode without the header retires (when every deployment runs inside a workspace:
   [Per-workspace configuration](#per-workspace-configuration)): until then it would stop a working
   app. Since 7.92.01 the page for a workspace address the person cannot open names who can add them (the workspace's
   admins or a platform admin), also when they belong to none.
2. The web app keeps the current workspace in the URL (`/workspace/{slug}/…`) and sends `X-Workspace-Id` on every API call.
3. Shared middleware (a new `workspace` module in the Lambdas' shared package, and its TypeScript twin in the stream
   and plan-step Lambdas)
   resolves the caller's membership (cached in the warm container for a short time), answers **404** when the caller
   is not a member (a 404, not a 403, so ids of other workspaces are not confirmed), and puts `{ws, role}` on the
   request context. A platform admin passes as a workspace admin once the live check confirms them (Control plane),
   and the entry writes an `AUDIT#` row that the workspace's own admins can see.
4. All data access goes through a workspace-scoped repository layer (`lambda/shared/ws_repo.py`: a verified
   `WorkspaceScope`, the key builders over `ws_key(ws, …)`, `s3_key(scope, …)` for objects and `ws_table(scope,
   table)`). A static test (`lambda/shared/test/test_ws_raw_access.py`) counts the raw table and bucket calls it
   knows and the strings that start an unscoped data-path key outside that layer (the 7.00.00 row above lists them
   and what it does not see yet), kind by kind, against an allowlist of the modules not moved yet
   that must match exactly, so a new raw access fails unless its entry is raised in the same change (which review
   refuses) and a removed one must lower it.
5. `require_admin` becomes `require_role('admin')`: a workspace admin, or a platform admin. The route-gate inventory
   (`lambda/api/test/test_route_admin_gates.py`) gains the workspace dimension: every route is classified as
   workspace-scoped, platform-only or public, as well as admin-only or open.

Asynchronous paths carry the workspace explicitly and never infer it from the caller: SQS messages, Step Functions
inputs, Lambda async payloads, DynamoDB Stream records (the workspace comes from the key prefix), memory extraction,
agent runs, plan steps and assist jobs.

The assistant's reads (the stream Lambda calling the domain Lambdas as the signed-in person,
`lambda/stream/src/assistant/tools/internal-api.ts`) carry the caller's own `X-Workspace-Id` since 6.18.00: the
stream request's header (`assistant/runtime/event.ts`, as it came, not judged there; the web app sends it once it is
inside a workspace, Request flow step 2) goes on every domain call as its `X-Workspace-Id` header, never as a claim,
and each domain Lambda answers it exactly as it answers the caller's own request (membership, the 400 and the one
404). So a route that moves into its workspace keeps working for the assistant. Its conversations (since 7.15.00) are the
caller's own inside the workspace: a request that names one keeps them at `WS#{ws}#USER#{sub}` in
voc-conversations (the same `CONV#{id}` and `RUNCANCEL#{id}` sort keys; `lambda/shared/conversations.py`
`conversation_partition`, and the stream Lambda's `assistant/session/record.ts` `callerPartition`, in lockstep), one
that names none at `USER#{sub}`, as before. Both writers check the membership before any read or write: the chat
routes (`lambda/api/chat_handler.py`, through `lambda/shared/request_workspace.py` with `elevate=False`, fresh for a
write, every call through the layer's view of the table) and the stream Lambda, which judges the header itself
(`assistant/session/workspace-member.ts`: the caller's `MEMBER#` row with a known role and an `active` workspace,
read fresh at the start of each run; a malformed id, a non-member or a service subject ends the run with the one 404
before any session row; the chat routes refuse a service subject inside a workspace too). Neither enters as a platform admin (decision 2's entry needs the live check and its audit row): for a
platform admin who is not a member the assistant still answers there (each domain API it reads through admits them,
live-checked), but nothing of the conversation is kept, the stream Lambda reading and writing no session row and the
chat routes (list, open, save, rename, delete, Stop) answering the one 404, until they add themselves as a member;
with no Stop watch, such a run cannot be stopped and runs to its end (at most the stream Lambda's 15 minutes). Both roles read only `WS#*` rows of voc-workspaces. A deleted conversation's
`AUDIT#delete` row stays in the deployment's audit partition and names the workspace. Since 7.19.00 the workspace
keeps a list of who has conversations in it, `WS#{ws}#CONVERSANTS` / `{sub}` with `first_at` in voc-conversations
(`lambda/shared/ws_repo.py` `conversants_pk`, `lambda/shared/conversations.py` `conversant_key` and
`record_conversant`, the stream Lambda's `record.ts` `conversantKey`, in lockstep through
`voc-datalake/schemas/conversation-keys-cases.json`): both writers put the person's row if absent before they write
the conversation, and nothing removes it when the person leaves, so one Query of that partition names every
`WS#{ws}#USER#{sub}` partition, a former member's too. The memory workers mine a
workspace's conversations into that workspace's memories (since 7.16.00; below). Nothing is migrated: the conversations saved before
stay the caller's outside workspaces, at `USER#{sub}`: they are kept, and the assistant's history inside a workspace
starts empty. The web app (in this merge) offers them below that history as "Conversations from before workspaces",
read only and collapsed until asked for, each opened as a plain transcript and never as a thread
(`frontend/src/assistant/components/BeforeWorkspaces.tsx`, read with the `'deployment'` request scope, so without
the header), so nothing done inside the workspace writes to them. For a platform admin who is not a member, the
chat routes' 404 is settled in the web app: the history says the conversations are not saved here, the conversation
in view is marked unsaved, and nothing retries (`frontend/src/assistant/sessions/unsavedThreads.ts`).
Since 7.75.00 the logs routes act in the workspace a request names (`lambda/api/logs_handler.py`): `GET
/logs/validation`, `/logs/processing`, `/logs/summary` and `/logs/scraper/{id}` resolve it first (the one 404 for a
workspace the caller is not in) and read that workspace's own trail, its `WS#{ws}#LOGS#{kind}#{source}` rows
(`lambda/shared/processing_logs.py` `log_partition`, what the processor writes for a workspace's messages) and its
`WS#{ws}#SCRAPER_RUN#{id}` runs (what a workspace's scraper run writes), through the layer's view of voc-aggregates;
the reads stay open to every member, as the deployment's are to everyone signed in. A scraper's runs no longer echo
its id, so another workspace's scraper id answers exactly as an unknown one. `DELETE /logs/validation/{source}`
resolves fresh and clears the workspace's trail of that source for its admins only (`require_settings_admin`: a
viewer, an editor and a delegated caller get 403; the deployment's `admins` group grants nothing there; a platform
admin enters as an admin, decision 2, live-checked and audited), each clear audited first in `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` (kind
`validation_logs_cleared`: the actor's sub, whether as a platform admin, the source and how many rows; a row that
cannot be written clears nothing); the logs role's batch delete is held to `LOGS#validation#*` and
`WS#{one workspace id}#LOGS#validation#*` (lib/stacks/api-engagement-lambdas.ts). A request that names no workspace
reads and clears the deployment's trail, as before; since 7.77.00 that clear is recorded first too, in voc-aggregates'
`AUDIT#delete` (kind `validation_logs`: the actor, the source and how many rows; `lambda/shared/delete_audit.py`
`record_delete_first`, the logs role that partition's one audit writer there). A workspace's clear is kind
`validation_logs_cleared` in `AUDIT#WS#{ws}`: each table's own audit convention, so a search of the audit trail
looks for both kinds. The alerts and the system diagnostics (`/logs/alerts*`,
`/logs/system*`) stay the deployment's: they watch and read its own Lambdas, queues and environment, and resolve no
