workspace (the alerts' writes a platform admin's, below). Since 7.76.00 the system diagnostics show no
workspace's content ([deployment.md](deployment.md#system-errors-administration--system--logs--system-errors),
`lambda/shared/system_log_scope.py`): every log line carries the workspace its code resolved (the Bedrock meter's
attribution, `lambda/shared/usage_meter.py`); a line or a failure queue message that names a workspace is left out
and counted, and a line not attributed to the deployment shows its time, function, level and error type only.
Open (TENETS.md D8's gap): the Logs view is in Administration, which only the deployment's `admins` open; the
deployment-wide jobs that walk every workspace (the erasure by email, the retention window, the scheduled fan-outs)
are still to attribute each workspace they enter (until then a line they write about a workspace's item reads as
the deployment's unless it names the workspace's id); and the jobs, public routes, the global MCP handler and the
stream Lambda that attribute nothing yet are still to attribute what they run in, so that their errors show their
text again (until then, their type only).
In the web app the trail is the open workspace's
(Administration → System → Logs sends its `X-Workspace-Id`), and Clear follows the workspace's admin role there
(`useBrandEditRight`). Memory's store
(since 7.15.00) can keep a workspace's memories: `lambda/shared/memory_store.py` builds every key inside the workspace's
partitions when given one (`WS#{ws}#MEM#company`, `WS#{ws}#MEM#user#{sub}`, `WS#{ws}#MEMSTATUS#…` in `gsi1pk`,
`WS#{ws}#MEMEVT#…`, `WS#{ws}#MEMCURSOR`, `WS#{ws}#MEMIMPORT`; a `Candidate` names its workspace), and every operation on
a stored key or row reads its workspace from that key's `pk` and goes through the layer's view of voc-memory (the same
calls as before for a deployment key, so reinforcing, editing, archiving and merging follow their rows' partition);
retrieval, conflict checks, listing, counts, cursors and imports take an optional workspace. The memory API (since 7.15.00) acts in the workspace a request names: every `/memory` route resolves it
first (`lambda/api/memory_handler.py`, its first middleware; fresh for a write, the warm cache for a read and for
the assistant's `POST /memory/retrieve`, its `used` marks included) and reads and writes that workspace's
memories, the stream assistant's recall and the MCP `memory_search` tool included (both send the header); inside one
its admins curate (the deployment's `admins` group and the `memory_reviewer` flag grant nothing there), a viewer
reads only, and a page import is the workspace's (its record, its page under `ws/{ws}/memory-imports/`, its queue
message naming the workspace). Without the header, the
deployment's memories and rules, as before. Nothing is migrated: the memories kept before stay the deployment's, and
the web app no longer shows them to anyone who has a workspace (the gate always enters one): they are kept and
readable through a call that names no workspace, but memory inside a workspace starts empty, and the assistant
recalls only that workspace's (an upgrade note for the release, as for the conversations). Memory retention sweeps each active workspace's memories too (`lambda/memory/retention/handler.py`, after the
deployment's, the workspaces from the platform's listing). The memory workers (since 7.16.00) extract in a
workspace: the scanner queues a workspace conversation's session with its workspace (`workspace_id`, from the
conversation's `WS#{ws}#USER#{sub}` key; its cursor in that workspace) while that workspace is active, and the extractor reads the workspace the
message names (`ws_repo.message_scope`: one that is not a workspace id is dropped), the conversation and the imported
page only there, and writes the memories there, only while that workspace is active (`lambda/shared/workspace.py`
`is_active`: a workspace being deleted gets no write, not even a cursor) and the conversation's owner still writes
there (`stored_role`: a viewer's and a removed member's sessions give nothing);
agent runs and project chats, which carry no workspace yet, drop a message that names one ([memory](memory.md)). Company context's store (since 7.18.00) can keep a workspace's: `lambda/shared/company_context.py`
`company_context_key` and `my_context_key` (`WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#company_context`, `WS#{ws}#USERCTX#{sub}`, the `config`
row only: onboarding and briefing preferences keep `USERCTX#{sub}`), and its reads and writes take an optional
workspace. No route passes one: the document, research and persona generators read the company context and run on
the deployment's projects, so the routes move with projects (the same release), not before. Plan steps carry it too (since
6.18.00): the approval's `X-Workspace-Id` is kept on the plan (one that is not a workspace id is the one 404) and each step names it to the
domain Lambdas the same way ([Assistant plan mode](assistant-plan-mode.md#identity-and-permissions)). And the global
MCP tools (since 7.10.00): a token minted inside a workspace (`POST /connect/tokens` with `X-Workspace-Id`), and an
OAuth sign-in approved inside one (`POST /connect/oauth/consent`, the grant row), each resolved fresh by
`lambda/shared/mcp_workspace_pin.py` with the person's own membership (a platform admin who is not a member gets 403,
"add yourself as a member first", since the credential's calls carry no group), pins it on its row (`workspace`, `lambda/shared/mcp_global_tokens.py`
`token_workspace`), and every call the endpoint delegates sends it as `X-Workspace-Id` (`lambda/shared/mcp_delegate.py`
`build_proxy_event`), from the row, never from the MCP request; a token minted with no workspace sends none, as
before. A delegated credential has no membership of its own: `lambda/shared/workspace.py` resolves the person it
acts for (`member_subject`: the minter; an agent's owner too, once agent runs carry a workspace), so removing the minter from the workspace stops
the token there (the one 404), and it acts at most as an `editor` (an admin's credential administers nothing, as for
projects), capped after the membership cache the person and their credential share; it is never a platform admin's
candidate, even if its claims carried the group (no live check, no entry audit row in its person's name). The token and sign-in lists, detail and revoke stay the person's own, across the workspaces their
credentials name (each names its `workspace_id`).

## Roles

| Role | Can |
|---|---|
| Viewer | Read the workspace's feedback, dashboards, projects (the projects shared with them: private projects stay with their members) and documents |
| Editor | Everything a viewer can, plus write: projects, documents, personas, agents, imports a non-admin may run today |
| Admin | Everything an editor can, plus the Administration tabs, members and invites, export and delete of the workspace |
| Platform admin | Create workspaces, see the platform console, enter any workspace as an admin (audited) |

What an editor may write follows today's split between admin-only and open routes; the route-gate inventory pins it.
Since 6.10.00 the members routes check the role (`require_workspace_admin`, `lambda/shared/workspace.py`: a member
admin or an entered platform admin; the deployment's `admins` group grants nothing there), and the inventory
(`lambda/api/test/test_route_admin_gates.py`) has a workspace-admin table for them. Every member, a viewer
included, reads the member list; only admins add members, change roles or remove others; any member may leave.

## Invites

Built in 6.10.00 (`POST`, `PUT` and `DELETE /me/workspaces/current/members[/{sub}]`, `lambda/shared/workspace_members.py`).

- Workspace admins and platform admins invite, with any workspace role. Editors and viewers cannot (403).
- An email that already has an account gets its membership row at once, and the answer says `account: 'existing'`.
  With outbound email on (`-c emailFrom`, [Deployment Guide](deployment.md)), since 7.92.00 that account is then told by email
  (`lambda/shared/member_notice.py`, through the deployment's one SES v2 path, `lambda/shared/outbound_mail.py`,
  which the participant asks use too): one plain-English email from "VoC Analytics" to the address Cognito holds for
  the account (never one the request names), subject `VoC Analytics - You were added to "<workspace>"`, saying it was
  added to the workspace (its name in quotes, control characters stripped, the subject's prefix fixed; a name that
  reads as a link or an address, with a scheme, `www.`, an `@` or a dotted host, is shown as the workspace's slug
  instead, since an admin chooses the name and the deployment signs the email) as admin,
  editor or viewer, with the link `https://<frontend>/workspace/<slug>/`, the email's one address (an HTML part marked
  `lang="en"` beside the plain text). It names no inviter and no other member. At most one per workspace and account per 24 hours: before the send, a
  conditional put of `WS#{ws}` / `NOTICE#{sub}` (`sent_at`, `ttl` a day on) that succeeds only when no such row is
  live, so removing and adding someone again does not mail them again; a send that fails keeps the claim. The send is
  best effort and comes after the membership is written: SES off, a refused or failed send, or the guard all leave
  the add as it is; each outcome is logged (the workspace id and the outcome only: never the address, the sub or a
  hash of it, since an unsalted hash of a sub is undone by anyone who can list the subs) and counted
  (`WorkspaceMemberNotices` by `Outcome`). The answer carries `notified: true|false`, and the members page says
  either "We emailed them a link to the workspace" or, as before, "No email was sent: let them know".
- A new email gets a Cognito account (`AdminCreateUser`, which sends the temporary password) and the membership row
  at once (`account: 'created'`), when the caller is in the deployment's `admins` group (a platform admin is): any
  other workspace admin gets 403 for a new email until the data path is keyed by workspace (phase 2), because until
  then every account reads the whole deployment. It gets Cognito's invitation only, never the notice above, and the
  answer says `notified: false` (the field is in every add's answer). The
  account joins no deployment group (neither `admins` nor `users`): inside a
  workspace only the `MEMBER#` role counts. The creation is recorded before the account exists: an `AUDIT#WS#{ws}`
  row of kind `account_created` (the actor and the email's sha256: no role can change or remove an audit row, so a
  raw address there could never be erased), written before `AdminCreateUser`, so a row that cannot be
  written creates no account. If the membership then fails (its audit row or its transaction), the account stays,
  recorded; adding the email again finds it.
- Membership is immediate in both cases, so no `INVITE#` row is written (Control plane).
- Removing or demoting a workspace's last admin is refused (409), the admin themselves included. Any member may leave
  (remove themselves). A person with no workspace left gets the app without a workspace (until phase 2, Request
  flow step 1).
- A change takes effect at once in the container that made it; other warm containers serve the member's cached
  role to reads for up to 30 s (`MembershipCache`, `lambda/shared/workspace.py`). The members writes never use the
  cache: they read the caller's role from the table, and each transaction also checks that the acting admin is still
  admin when it commits (403 when they were demoted in between).
- Trade-off, accepted with decision 4 (an invitee may already have an account): adding by email tells any workspace
  admin whether the email has an account in this deployment, and its name (201 for one with an account, 403 for a
  new email from an admin outside `admins`). It is one deployment's own directory, which only `admins` list in full
  (`GET /users`); a multi-company deployment would answer both cases alike.
- Deleting a user (`DELETE /users/{username}`, since 6.11.00) removes their memberships too, in this order: every
  refusal first (the users API's own: the caller's own account, the last administrator, a platform admin's rules;
  then the last member admin of any of their workspaces, 409, which names the workspace), then a platform admin's
  revoke audit row, then per workspace its `member_removed` audit row (`via: user_deleted`) and one transaction of
  the `MEMBER#` and `USER#` deletes, and only then the Cognito account (`AdminDeleteUser`). So a refusal, or a fault
  before the account goes, leaves the account as it was: a fault part-way leaves the memberships not yet removed (the
  ones removed stay removed, audited), and the delete can be asked again. A platform admin may delete a workspace's
  last admin: the workspace then has no member admin (the audit row says `left_without_admin`) until a platform admin
  enters it (decision 2) and makes a member admin, or adds one (`PUT` or `POST /me/workspaces/current/members`).
  Other warm containers serve the deleted user's cached membership to reads for up to 30 s.
- A member admin whose Cognito account is disabled still counts as an admin when another admin is demoted or
  removed: the check reads the table, not Cognito (one Cognito call per admin on every demotion and removal would be
  the alternative), and a disabled account may be enabled again. Their rows stay; deleting the account removes them.
  Known gap: a user deleted before 6.11.00 kept their rows, so they still count as another admin. To clean up, check
  each workspace's members against the users list and remove any member whose account is gone.
- Erasure (D5, since 6.11.00): an erasure by email without a source (`POST /settings/erasure`, `voc-retention`,
  docs/source-policies.md) removes the `email` and `name` of every `MEMBER#` row that holds that email (compared
  case-insensitively: a row keeps Cognito's spelling) and keeps the row, as the feedback boards keep a de-identified
  request: a membership is access configuration, and the account itself is the users API's to delete. Each row first
  gets a contentless tombstone in `voc-aggregates` (`AUDIT#tombstone#workspace_members` / `{ws}#{sub}#{job}`:
  the workspace, the run, the legal basis; one per job, so a later erasure never overwrites an earlier record), and the job and its audit row count `member_rows_deidentified`. While the
  account exists, the members list fills an emptied row's email from Cognito, as for a workspace's creator. The
  workspace's audit rows hold subs only (an `account_created` row the email's sha256), so they hold nothing to erase.
  The rows are found by a paged Scan of `voc-workspaces`: the table has no email index, and the email can be on a row
  whose account changed its email or was deleted before 6.11.00, which a look-up of the account by email (Cognito
  `ListUsers`, then the `USER#` rows) would miss. It runs once per erasure job, never on a request path, 100 items a
  page, projecting the key and the email only, within the job's time budget and hand-over cursor; the table holds
  the control plane (workspaces, members, their audit), far smaller than the feedback tables the same job scans.
- History (D2: access rules are primary data): a removal deletes the `MEMBER#` and `USER#` rows and a role change
  overwrites `role`, but each first writes its `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` row with the target, the role before and after, the
  actor and the time, append-only under the audit-row guard: the membership's whole history is kept there.

## Public and non-user entry points

| Entry | Today | With workspaces |
|---|---|---|
| Feedback form `/feedback-forms/{id}/config\|submit\|iframe` | form id | a globally unique form id; a `FORM#{id}` lookup row gives the workspace |
| Voting sessions | session id | the same pattern: `VOTE#{id}` gives the workspace |
| Webhooks `/webhooks/{plugin}` | one per plugin | `/webhooks/{plugin}/{ws}`; the webhook secret lives in the workspace's secret |
| Email inbox (SES) | one set of addresses | `{slug}@<inbound domain>`, routed to the workspace by the receipt rule |
| MCP `/mcp/global` | token → user | the token row also pins one workspace (a token is minted inside a workspace) |
| Scheduled ingestion | one rule per plugin | one tick per plugin fans out over the workspaces that enabled it |

## Per-workspace configuration

- Every workspace kind of settings in the table below moves under `WS#{ws}#…`; the deployment kinds keep their key.
  The `METRIC#…` rows in `voc-aggregates` move under `WS#{ws}#…` with the data path (phase 2).
- Plugins and menu items stop being chosen at deploy time: every plugin Lambda is deployed, `cdk.context.json` becomes
  the ceiling (what this deployment offers), and each workspace's settings pick a subset of it at run time.
  Since 6.18.00 the workspaces API has the routes of a workspace's plugins (`lambda/shared/workspace_plugin_settings.py`),
  and since 7.14.00 a workspace's admins enable them (below):
  `GET /me/workspaces/current/plugins` answers every plugin the deployment offers (its `ENABLED_SOURCES`, rendered
  from `cdk.context.json`; the ceiling) with whether the workspace enabled it, for any member; `PUT
  /me/workspaces/current/plugins/{plugin_id}` `{enabled}` turns one on or off, for its admins only (403 for an editor
  or a viewer; 404 for a plugin the deployment does not offer). An enabled plugin is a row `PLUGIN#{plugin_id}` /
  `WS#{ws}` in `voc-workspaces` (`enabled_by`, `enabled_at`), the partition the plugin's scheduled
  ingestor reads to fan its tick out over the workspaces that enabled it (phase 2); disabling deletes the row (access
  configuration, not customer data). Each change writes its `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` row first (kind `plugin_enabled` or
  `plugin_disabled`), then one transaction that also checks the workspace is `active` and a member admin still admin
  (409 when the plugin changed meanwhile, 403 when the admin was demoted meanwhile); asking for what holds changes
  and records nothing. Disabling deletes the row and keeps no version of it: its history (who turned which plugin
  on or off, and when) is the workspace's append-only `AUDIT#WS#` rows, as for a membership (D2). The web app lists them on the workspace's settings page (since 7.02.00: Workspace settings in the header's
  workspace menu, with Enable and Disable for its admins); its menu does not read them yet; today every plugin of
  the ceiling still deploys as before (the ceiling stays the one `cdk.context.json` sets).
  **Since 7.14.00 (the ingestion hold lifted):** a workspace's own ingestion runs. From 6.18.00 to 7.13.00 enabling
  answered 409 and wrote nothing, and every run in a workspace (Run now, a scraper's run, an S3 import's reprocess
  naming one, a workspace form's create and submission, a workspace webhook's delivery) answered the same 409, while
  a workspace's items were not yet processed, retained, aggregated, erased and replayed in it; every one of those is
  in since (7.00.00 to 7.13.00), so 7.14.00 removes the flag and its one check (`PER_WORKSPACE_INGESTION`,
  `hold_workspace_ingestion`, `INGESTION_NOT_YET`; no flag is kept) and gives the workspaces role its PutItem on
  `PLUGIN#*` (`WORKSPACE_WRITE_PARTITIONS`, `lib/stacks/api-workspaces-lambda.ts`). An enable writes `PLUGIN#{plugin_id}`
  / `WS#{ws}` (`enabled_by`, `enabled_at`), the shape the ingestors' fan-out reads (its workspace from the sort key),
  so the plugin's next scheduled tick runs in the workspace; a disable deletes it and the next tick no longer does.
  Run now and a scraper's run in a workspace run only a plugin the workspace enabled (`require_plugin_enabled`: 409
  "Enable this plugin for the workspace first" otherwise, before anything is written or invoked), and a delivery on
  a workspace's webhook (`/webhooks/{plugin}/{ws}`) is taken in only when the workspace enabled that plugin (else the
  bad signature's 401, before any secret is read or the body parsed), so a disable stops its runs and its deliveries.
  A workspace form's submission is not gated on a `PLUGIN#` row: a form is not a plugin. The S3 import is not a
  workspace plugin (`DEPLOYMENT_ONLY_PLUGINS`): the plugins list leaves it out, enabling it is the unknown plugin's
  404, and Run now for it in a workspace answers one 400. Its own API serves a workspace since 7.86.00 (a request
  with `X-Workspace-Id` acts on the workspace's files under `ws/{ws}/` of the import bucket, its admins only), and an
  upload there is ingested in the workspace, gated by the workspace being active, not by a `PLUGIN#` row (the
  workspace's admins chose to upload it). A request without a workspace runs deployment-wide as before.
- The model picker is per workspace; the model allowlist stays deployment-wide.
- Brand configuration (name, products, the tone the AI uses) is per workspace; the logo and colours stay global.

Every settings row and its scope (since 6.18.00; `voc-datalake/lambda/shared/test/test_settings_scopes.py` holds this
table and the files that spell each key to the code, so the files still to move only shrink). A workspace kind moves
to `WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#<kind>` in phase 3; a deployment kind keeps `SETTINGS#<kind>`. The first kind has moved
(6.18.00): `resolved_problems`. A request to `GET` or `PUT /settings/resolved-problems` that names a workspace
(`X-Workspace-Id`) acts in it (`lambda/shared/request_workspace.py`, the workspaces API's rules: the one 404 for a
malformed id, a missing workspace or a caller who is not a member; a platform admin enters as admin, checked live and
audited) and reads and writes `WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#resolved_problems` / `config` in `voc-aggregates` (the key from
`lambda/shared/workspace_settings.py`): any member reads (still only the problems under the categories they may see),
an editor or an admin marks one resolved or not (403 for a viewer). A request that names none (no header, or a blank
one) is the deployment as before: the deployment-wide row `SETTINGS#resolved_problems`, which every signed-in user
reads and marks, as today. That is what the web app sends with no workspace (since 6.12.00 the web app
opens "as before, with no header" on an address without a slug or for a person with no workspace), what a deployment from before workspaces
gets (decision 12: no migration), and what an MCP token minted with no workspace sends; it never reaches a
workspace's row. So nothing changes for those, and a workspace starts with no marks of its own: the deployment's
marks are not copied into it (a mark expires anyway, and setting it again is one click). This mode goes when every
deployment runs inside a workspace (a first workspace made at bootstrap, at the end of phase 2; agreed with the
multi-workspace lead); a later kind that
moves before then decides it again, for its own readers. Upgrade note for the release: nothing to do; the deployment's marks stay where
they are and keep working without a workspace, and a workspace's members see only their workspace's marks. The
settings API reads `voc-workspaces`' `WS#*` rows and puts only a new entry audit row in `AUDIT#WS#*` there, through
the stack's one workspace-resolution managed policy (`voc-datalake/lib/utils/workspace-resolution-grants.ts`), which
every domain API that moves into its workspace attaches; the audit-row guard now guards a customer-managed policy in
itself, like a role, and names this one a writer of that partition only. The Lambda gets `WORKSPACES_TABLE` and
keeps the Cognito reads it already had. The brand follows (6.18.00), the same way: with
`X-Workspace-Id`, `GET /settings/brand` reads `WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#brand` for any member and `PUT` writes it for the
workspace's admins only (`require_settings_admin` in `lambda/shared/request_workspace.py`: an admin of the workspace;
the deployment's `admins` group grants nothing inside a workspace, decision 2); without it, the deployment's
`SETTINGS#brand` and its `admins`, as before. A new workspace starts with an empty brand (nothing is copied). In the web
app (since 7.02.00) a workspace's admins edit its brand on the workspace's settings page (`/workspace/{slug}/workspace-settings`,
Workspace settings in the header's workspace menu), whether or not they are in the deployment's `admins`; its other
members see it read-only there. The deployment-wide brand is edited in the platform console (Administration → Workspaces → Deployment brand, `/admin/workspaces/deployment-brand`: the "Deployment default brand" card), whose calls send no `X-Workspace-Id` from any address
(`frontend/src/api/brandApi.ts`, the client's `'deployment'` request scope), for the deployment's `admins`, the gate
the server applies to a request without the header. Still on
the deployment's brand until they move: the engagement API's participant emails and feedback boards
(`lambda/shared/engagement_config.py` `brand_name`, which reads a workspace's brand row when given one since
7.31.00; no route passes one yet, so it moves with the participants' settings, phase 3). The categories, the
dimensions and the source profiles moved in 7.08.00 ([The configuration cut-over](#the-configuration-cut-over-70800),
below the table). The cross-workspace suite runs the resolved problems', the brand's and (since 7.08.00) the
categories', dimensions' and sources' routes (its check 3 expects, for a route whose case says why, the deployment's
answer with no row of either workspace touched instead of the 400; for each `PUT`, 403 to a caller outside the
deployment's `admins`). Radar's configuration can be read per workspace the same way (since 7.10.00,
decision 13): `lambda/shared/radar_keys.py` keys a workspace's scan settings and brand list at
`WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#radar` (`config`, `brands`; the deployment's `SETTINGS#radar` without one), and `load_settings` (`shared/radar_settings.py`),
`load_brand_list` (`shared/radar_brands.py`), the brief's `effective_brands` (`briefing/radar_cards.py`, with that
workspace's source profiles) and the processor's brand labels (`shared/radar_routing.py` `BrandLabels`, cached per
workspace, a failed read forgetting only that workspace's copy) take an optional scope and read through `ws_table`.
Radar's views can too (`lambda/shared/radar_reads.py`): a workspace's reviews, markers, daily counters and
releases are the deployment's keys inside its partitions (`WS#{ws}#RADAR#{brand}#…`, `WS#{ws}#RADARID#{brand}`,
`WS#{ws}#RELEASE#{brand}`, `scoped_pk`), and so are the counters of its own feedback that the comparison reads
(`WS#{ws}#METRIC#…`, as `lambda/shared/ws_repo.py` `metric_pk` builds them); every reader of them
(`shared/radar_store.py` `daily_rows`, `sum_counter`, `recent_items`; `shared/radar_reviews.py`'s list,
cursor and detail; `shared/radar_counts.py`; `shared/radar_views.py`'s totals, trend, themes and comparison, both
sides; `shared/radar_releases.py` `list_releases`, `count_releases`; `shared/radar_all.py`) takes an optional
`workspace` and reads through `lambda/shared/ingest_tables.py` `table_view`: `ws_table` with one, which refuses a
partition or a cursor outside it before DynamoDB (a cursor of another partition is a 400; a marker pointing outside
the workspace reads as absent), the table itself without, both with one key condition (the same rows as before).
Since 7.73.00 Radar acts in the workspace a request names: every `/radar` route (`lambda/api/radar_handler.py`, its first
middleware) resolves it first (fresh for a write, the warm cache for a read) and reads and writes that workspace's brand
list, settings, reviews, releases, last scan and version row (`WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#radar` / `version`, so a write in one
workspace invalidates only its own cached Radar reads; `shared/data_version.py` `radar_version_key`); its admins change
the brand list and settings and start a scan (the deployment's `admins` group grants nothing there); the Exa key routes
answer 409 inside a workspace (a workspace keeps no Exa key yet: its scans use web search and the brands' feeds). The
scan job (`lambda/jobs/radar_scan/handler.py`) runs in the workspace its event names (`workspace_id`, `message_scope`: a
"Scan now" names the request's resolved workspace, `shared/scoped_keys.py` `named_in`; one that is not a workspace
id is refused): its brands, profiles, settings, its own lock, cadence and brand states (`WS#{ws}#RADARSCAN`,
`WS#{ws}#RADARSCAN#{brand}`, `shared/radar_keys.py`) and its release timeline (`WS#{ws}#RELEASE#{brand}`). The
schedule's tick first starts one asynchronous run per active workspace (the platform's listing,
`shared/workspace_listing.py`; a listing or invoke fault is logged and counted, `RadarScanFanOutFailures`, and never
stops the deployment's scan), then scans the deployment's as before. A workspace's scan writes only while the
workspace is active (`ws_repo.workspace_is_active` before it writes and before each brand; one that stops being active
mid-scan writes nothing more, its lock left to expire), and a run that waited over an hour to start is dropped. No producer stamps a Radar brand in a workspace
yet: its scrapers, connectors and uploads and its source profiles still refuse a Radar brand, and the processor still
refuses a workspace message that carries one (`WorkspaceRadarRefusedError`), so a workspace's Radar has its news scan,
releases and reads, and its own reviews (their writer and their producer) arrive with that follow-up. Each per-brand
partition a workspace's Radar writes is named first in its owner list, `WS#{ws}#RADAR_OWNED` (`shared/radar_keys.py`
`owned_row`), which the workspace's hard delete reads. Projects
are next (since 7.11.00, groundwork only): every key of a project's rows (`PROJECT#{id}`, its documents' and personas'
`gsi1pk`, `TYPE#PROJECT`, where a document is listed and a document row's four keys) is built in one module, `lambda/shared/project_keys.py`
(but the product-doc extractor, a stdlib-only bundle that spells its own), so the move gives each builder a scope in
one place, and the raw-access guard (`lambda/shared/test/test_ws_raw_access.py`) now counts every module that opens
the projects' or jobs' table (`get_projects_table`, `get_jobs_table`) or spells `PROJECT#` / `TYPE#PROJECT`, all phase
3. Nothing moves yet. Since 7.21.00 the key builders and the shared project stores take a keyword-only `workspace`
(plumbing: no caller passes one; the status row lists which stores, and the document-version stores and the access
gate refuse one). The rest of the move, and the rule for project ids, is
[The project plan](#the-project-plan-phase-3) below. The deployment kinds' writes need the deployment's `admins` and a live platform admin (since 6.18.00): `PUT /settings/appearance` and the three alert writes
(`PUT /logs/alerts`, `POST /logs/alerts/recipients`, `POST /logs/alerts/recipients/remove`) call `require_admin`,
`require_platform_admin` and the live check (`lambda/shared/platform_admin_gate.py`: the live Cognito membership and an
enabled account of the same person), so no workspace admin or plain administrator changes what every workspace
shares (403); the reads stay as they were (the theme for everyone, the alerts' status for administrators). The settings
and logs APIs read the caller's groups and account in Cognito for it (`AdminListGroupsForUser`, `AdminGetUser`). The
web app shows the default theme read-only to everyone else (Administration → General → Default theme) and
the alerts read-only to the other administrators (Administration → System → Logs → Alerts, which only administrators see), each with a notice naming platform
admins (`useIsPlatformAdmin` reads the token's group, only a hint: the server decides), and a 403 on either save (a
membership revoked since sign-in) says the same. The bootstrapped first admin is a
platform admin (Control plane); on an older deployment, add the people who manage the theme and the alerts to
`platform-admins` first.

| Kind (`SETTINGS#…`) | Scope | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `brand`, `company_context`, `design_system` | workspace | the brand the AI writes for, the team's vision and objectives, its design tokens and references (decision 6; `brand` moved, a request with no workspace keeps the deployment's row, as before) |
| `categories`, `dimensions`, `sources` | workspace | the team's taxonomy and source profiles (decision 6; moved in 7.08.00: read and written per workspace through `lambda/shared/workspace_config.py`, a request or message with no workspace keeps the deployment's row; a workspace's profile sets no retention window) |
| `resolved_problems` | workspace | the problems a team marked resolved (moved; a request with no workspace keeps the deployment's row, as before) |
| `app_feedback` | workspace | the form a team's in-app micro-feedback goes to, and after which actions it shows (7.66.00, [micro-feedback.md](micro-feedback.md); a request with no workspace keeps the deployment's row) |
| `adoption` | workspace | the leadership view's time-saved baselines: the hours each kind of artifact takes by hand, and the admin's note (7.70.00, [leadership-view.md](leadership-view.md); a request with no workspace keeps the deployment's row) |
| `email_intake` | workspace | the team's inbox caps (its address is `{slug}@`, [Public and non-user entry points](#public-and-non-user-entry-points)) |
| `fallback_owner` | workspace | the member an agent's new project falls back to |
| `radar` (voc-radar: config, brands, version) | workspace | decision 13; since 7.73.00 read and edited per workspace (`lambda/shared/radar_keys.py`, the Radar routes and scan); a request with no workspace keeps the deployment's rows, as before |
| `model` | workspace, over a deployment default | the team's model picks (per surface, and the global pick); the default pinned at deploy is `model_default` (below) and the allowlist stays deployment-wide (decision 6) |
| `participants` (voc-participants, pk `SETTINGS`: engagement, board audience) | workspace | the team's engagement settings |
| `appearance` | deployment | one logo and colour scheme (decision 7): a platform admin's setting, not a workspace admin's |
| `model_default` | deployment | the model pinned at deploy (`-c defaultModelId`, `lambda/custom_resources/model_pin.py`), below every pick: split from `model` (7.01.00) so the picks can move into each workspace; a deployment from before the split keeps its pin in `model`'s `model_id`, which still outranks it |
| `model_quotas`, `image_model_quota` | deployment | the AWS account's Bedrock quotas, cached; every workspace shares them (decision 10) |
| `alerts` | deployment | alarms on the deployment's own Lambdas and their recipients: the deployment's operations (no alarm is about one workspace; a workspace's own trail is its logs, per workspace since 7.75.00) |
| `engineering` (voc-agents, `SETTINGS#engineering`) | deployment | the engineering agents' hard cap at each flow's monthly budget (`lambda/engineering/settings.py`, only with `-c engineeringAgents=true`): the engineering agents and their flows are the deployment's administrators' until they move per workspace ([3g3-c](#the-project-plan-phase-3)); so are, since 7.50.00, their runs' transcripts, run manifests and the transcripts' index in voc-eng-workspace (`ws/default/runs/<run>/<agent>/commits/<sha>/_transcripts/`, `ws/default/runs/<run>/_manifest/`, `ws/default/transcripts-index/<flow>/<day>/<run>.json`), kept forever and in no workspace's export or delete until 3g3-c; and, since 7.51.00, a run's direction (`WS#default#RUN#<run>` / `DIRECTION#v<nnnnnn>`), its steps' assertion records (`ASSERT#<step>#<attempt>`), a parent's child runs (`CHILD#<child>`), the runs list (`WS#default#RUNLIST#<yyyy-mm>` / `RUN#<started_at>#<run>`) and a child run's seeds (`ws/default/runs/<run>/_seed/<task>/`); and, since 7.52.00, a repository connection's knowledge docs' renderings (`WS#default#REPO#<connection>` / `KDOCS`, `KDOCSLOG#<n>`), its tenets' versions (`TENETS`, `TENETS#<v>`, `TENETSLOG#<v>#<event>`, `TENETSRUN#<run>`), the docs writes' claims (`WS#default#DOCS#<connection>`) and the understanders' tenets records (`…/commits/<sha>/_tenets/`); and, since 7.52.00, the Registry's cards made from a skill (voc-engineering `WS#default#AGENTCARD#<id>`, `…#AGENTCARD#_list`); 7.52.00's integration adds no partition: a person's own skill version is a version row of the skills' partitions, and the gates' recorded tool versions, the template sources and a run's expansion are reads of these, the bundle and the runs' gate records |
| `engineering_setup` (voc-agents, `SETTINGS#engineering_setup`) | deployment | the engineering setup guide's progress (7.50.00, `lambda/shared/agents_engineering_setup_routes.py`, only with `-c engineeringAgents=true`): what the deployment's administrators chose for each step of the guide, which sets up the deployment's engineering agents; it moves with them ([3g3-c](#the-project-plan-phase-3)) |

### The configuration cut-over (7.08.00)

The categories, the dimensions and the source profiles are each workspace's (the readers since 7.07.00, both
directions since 7.08.00). Every read and write of the three goes through one module,
`lambda/shared/workspace_config.py` (`get_config_item` and `put_config_item`: a workspace's row
`WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#{categories,dimensions,sources}` / `config` in `voc-aggregates` through `lambda/shared/ws_repo.py`
`ws_table`, else the deployment's `SETTINGS#{kind}`), from each kind's owner, each reader with an optional workspace
scope (the caches keep one entry per workspace).

- **The writers.** `GET` and `PUT /settings/categories`, `/settings/dimensions` and `/settings/sources`
  (`lambda/api/settings_handler.py`) resolve `X-Workspace-Id` first (`request_workspace`, the brand's rules: the one
  404 for a malformed id, an unknown or `deleting` workspace and a non-member; a platform admin enters as admin,
  live-checked and audited; a write resolves the role from the table). With it, any member reads the workspace's row
  and only its admins write it (`require_settings_admin`; the deployment's `admins` group grants nothing there); the
  categories' owners and every field of the profiles are shown to its admins only (others get the redacted view, as
  the deployment's non-administrators do). Without it, the deployment's rows and its `admins`, exactly as before. A
  workspace's profile sets no retention window and no Radar brand (400, `shared/source_profiles.py`
  `workspace_profiles_problem`): `voc-retention` applies the deployment's windows to every item, a workspace's
  included (7.03.00), so a workspace's own window would delete nothing, and a workspace's Radar takes no reviews yet (so a
  workspace's profile also sends no `retention_legal_basis` and no `radar_domains`, checked on the list as sent). A
  delegated caller (an MCP token, an agent, a plan step) never saves a workspace's settings. A save bumps the
  workspace's own `config_version` (`WS#{ws}#METRIC#meta`, since 7.78.00; before, the deployment's one counter, which
  refreshed every workspace's cached reads), which the response cache checks, so no cached answer outlives it; the Lambdas' per-container
  caches of the three (the processor's, the categories' and the profiles') catch up within their 5-minute TTL.
- **The readers.** The processor (`lambda/processor/handler.py`) reads, for a message that names a workspace, that
  workspace's categories (the model's instruction), dimensions, and source profile (its PII policy when the message
  carries no `pii_policy_applied`, its dimension defaults and tags), and keeps its inline raw copy under that
  workspace's raw-copy policy; a message that names none reads the deployment's rows, exactly as before. The plugin
  ingestors' source policy and raw archive (`plugins/_shared/source_policy_gate.py`, `raw_archive.py`,
  `raw_original.py`) read the run's workspace's profile, and so do a delivery routed to a workspace's webhook and a
  workspace form's submission (its PII policy, its opt-in raw copy, its preset category routing); a deployment-wide
  run, webhook or form reads the deployment's. A workspace's profile at ingestion (`cached_source_profile_strict` with
  a scope) carries the deployment's retention window for that source, so the raw-copy mode (`item` or `file`) follows
  the window that will delete the items. The feedback routes' access checks (`lambda/api/metrics_handler.py`,
  `lambda/api/feedback_edit_handler.py`, the feedback forms' stats and submissions) pass the request's workspace to
  `scope_for_event` / `scope_for_caller`, so a workspace's restricted sources and categories gate its own reads; the
  metrics routes' configured categories and dimensions, the edits' category and dimension checks, and a scraper's or
  a form's label check read the request's workspace too. The caller's own category-access row stays the
  deployment's (the users API's, phase 3).
- **A workspace with no configuration of its own reads none** (nothing is copied, as for the brand). Its feedback is
  classified with the built-in default categories, has only the built-in dimensions, and every source takes the
  workspace default profile, which REDACTS personal data (privacy by default, TENETS.md tenet 6 and D5: a new
  workspace never stores PII in full just because nobody saved a profile; `shared/source_profiles.py`
  `WORKSPACE_DEFAULT_PII`), kept, not restricted, with the deployment's retention window. The deployment's own
  default stays as it was (PII allowed; the email inbox's redact). The read and the write agree: what its settings page shows is exactly what its pipeline applies,
  and the deployment's rows are never its fallback. This follows the brand (6.18.00): a fallback would let a change of
  the deployment's rows reconfigure every workspace that never saved its own, and would show the deployment's category
  owners (their emails) inside each workspace.
- **The web app.** The editors are split by whose rows they edit (`frontend/src/workspace/configScope.ts`).
  Administration's Categories, Dimensions and Source policies edit the DEPLOYMENT's rows from any address, for the
  deployment's administrators, as before workspaces: their calls send no `X-Workspace-Id` (the client's
  `'deployment'` request scope in the categories, dimensions and source-profile API calls, as the platform console's
  deployment brand since
  7.02.00), since those rows still classify and redact all feedback outside workspaces; with them stay the category
  reprocess and the erasure panels, both the deployment's. Inside a workspace each of the three says plainly that it
  shows the deployment's settings, not the workspace's, and links to the workspace's page
  (`frontend/src/workspace/WorkspaceConfigNotice.tsx`). The workspace's settings page (`/workspace/{slug}/workspace-settings`)
  edits the workspace's own rows (its header), for its admins by the role the server applies (`/current`), whether
  or not they are in the deployment's `admins`; its other members read who changes them. There the source profile
  editor offers no retention or Radar field, and no erasure panels show; the categories editor offered no reprocess
  until 7.13.00, which adds the workspace's own (the table above).
- **Not yet.** The categories' generator
  (`POST /settings/categories/generate`, suggestions saved through the editor) stays the deployment's route (the
  category reprocess was too, until 7.13.00); the
  retention job reads the deployment's windows only; the dev mock's `/settings/categories`, `/dimensions` and
  `/sources` ignore `X-Workspace-Id`; the stream Lambda, the briefing, agents, memory, Radar, the users API's category
  access and the other phase-3 readers still read the deployment's rows.

### The deployment's Data sources (7.50.00)

Since 7.54.00 the deployment's own sources are the **Deployment** scope of Administration → Sources → Source settings
(`/admin/sources/plugins?scope=deployment`, `frontend/src/pages/Settings/DataSourcesPage.tsx`; one source's card
`/admin/sources/plugins/<source>?scope=deployment`, such as the GitHub Issues card the readiness fix and the setup guide
open), from any address. Inside a workspace (`/workspace/{slug}/admin/sources/plugins`) a platform admin chooses it with the
scope switch **This workspace | Deployment** above the cards; outside a workspace there is nothing to switch, the page
is the deployment's. It replaces 7.50.00's separate tab (`/admin/data-sources`), which is not aliased: that address
opens Administration's home.

- **Who.** Platform admins only: the switch and the scope (`adminPaths.ts` `?scope=deployment`) show only to an
  administrator whose token names `platform-admins`. For anyone else the address loses its `scope` and the page shows
  this workspace's sources, so a link to the scope shows nothing of it. `useIsPlatformAdmin` is the page's hint only;
  the server's gate is unchanged: a request without `X-Workspace-Id` is the deployment's, for the deployment's
  `admins` (`lambda/api/integrations_handler.py`).
- **No workspace on any call.** The Deployment scope is the same source cards under the deployment's request scope
  (`frontend/src/workspace/configScope.ts` `'deployment'`), as Administration's categories editor is. No call its
  cards make sends `X-Workspace-Id`, inside a workspace too:
  - the credentials' status, read and save (`GET /integrations/status`, `GET` and `PUT
    /integrations/{source}/credentials`);
  - the schedules and their pauses (`GET /sources/status`, `POST /sources/{source}/reset`). The schedule switch
    (`PUT /sources/{source}/enable` and `/disable`) never sends it, in either scope.
  - the GitHub Issues card's **Sign in with** one of the deployment's GitHub Apps (its `github_app_id`, and the Apps'
    list, `GET /agents/engineering/github-apps`).

  The API already acted deployment-wide on a request without the header, so no route changed. Each scope's reads are
  cached under their own keys (`scopedKey`), never shared with a workspace's, and each orders its own cards by health.
- **What it feeds.** These sources ingest the deployment's feedback: deployment-wide, outside every workspace. That
  is the only feedback the engineering automations read (Bug fixer, Feature builder, Issue triage). Their runs take
  only the deployment's feedback (D8) until the engineering agents move per workspace ([3g3-c](#the-project-plan-phase-3)). So the
  GitHub Issues source that feeds them is set here: it lists the repositories they work on and is signed in with the
  deployment's GitHub App ([github-issues.md](github-issues.md#signing-in-with-a-github-app-74500)). The page says
  so above the cards, in the Deployment scope and outside a workspace.
- **A workspace's Data sources differ.** Inside a workspace, the **This workspace** scope (the default) is that
  workspace's. Every call names it except the schedule switch: its credentials in its own parameter, its own pauses,
  Run now in it ([Credentials](#credentials)). Its GitHub Issues card offers no App sign-in, since a run in a
  workspace ignores `github_app_id`. Its Advanced fold (Email inbox, Radar, Design-system tokens) is in this scope
  only. Which plugins a workspace runs is set on its Workspace settings page (its `PLUGIN#` rows, above). The
  Deployment scope says plainly that it changes nothing of the workspace the app is in.

### The project plan (phase 3)

Since 7.21.00 the project key builders and the shared project stores take a keyword-only `workspace` (the 7.21.00
status row lists them), since 7.27.00 so do the document versions, their history and their series (3b below), and
since 7.29.00 the job stores (3c), since 7.31.00 the participants' people and lists and persona copy (3d), and since
7.37.00 the comment threads, persona review, review links and the keys of a project's objects (3e), and since 7.38.00
the product-doc extractor takes a workspace's upload (3f); the access gate, a job's work, the agent runs, the
participants' asks and a review link's reviewer routes refuse one.

**Project ids stay globally unique** (`proj_` ids, `lambda/shared/ids.py` `write_with_fresh_id`). A project made in a
workspace also gets a deployment-wide claim row, `PROJECT_ID#{id}` naming its workspace, put if absent, and a row in
the workspace's registry `WS#{ws}#PROJECT_IDS` / `{id}` (which the export, the delete and an erasure Query). The
scoped keys are a second layer. The claim row is what gives a public link and an MCP pin their workspace with no
ambiguity, and what confirms the workspace a product-doc upload's key names (3f), and no two workspaces can hold one id; the deployment's creates never put a claim,
so a reader that knows only an id also looks up the deployment's `PROJECT#{id}` row and refuses when both exist (3h).
A reader that misses the scope gets a 404, never another scope's project. Existing `PROJECT#` rows stay the deployment's: nothing is backfilled
(decision 21, fresh deployments).

The rest, in order, each slice plumbing with no change in behaviour until the switch:

- **3a, claim and registry** (done, 7.26.00: the status row above): the claim row and the registry above. The claim
  is outside the workspace, so it needed a layer change: `transact_project_writes` (through `ws_table`) refuses any
  entry outside the workspace, so the scoped transaction of `lambda/shared/ws_repo.py` now takes exactly one more
  entry, a `PROJECT_ID#{id}` / `CLAIM` Put (`attribute_not_exists(pk)`) that only the layer builds
  (`claim_project_id`), in the same transaction as the `META` and the registry row: all or nothing, so no claim is
  ever left without its project. Its reader is `project_claim`, its conditional delete `release_project_claim`. Its
  failure rule: a claim already held cancels the create at the claim's index, and a fresh id is minted, as a
  same-second collision is; any other cancellation is not retried and writes nothing. No IAM yet: no role passes a
  workspace (the grant is the switch's, 3h). The deployment's creates never put a claim (decided; the switch's
  safety argument is in 3h).
- **3b, versions and history** (done, 7.27.00: the status row above): the `DOCUMENT_VERSIONS#PROJECT#{id}` counter,
  allocation, legacy assignment and revision rows built in `lambda/shared/project_keys.py` with the workspace, and
  `shared/document_versions.py`, `shared/document_history.py` (with `series_versions`, which `shared/document_series.py`
  `latest_version` reads through) and `shared/human_documents.py` threaded, their `require_deployment` refusals
  removed. The stores beside them that were not per workspace then refused one: a series' comments and its persona
  review (threaded in 3e), the access gate (3h). `project_partitions` lists the partitions keyed by the project id alone, each with
  its kind and table (no caller yet). Still on the deployment's keys: `api/projects.py` `delete_document` and the
  project delete (they sweep only the deployment's versions partition), and the project bundle's
  `scripts/project_bundle/remap.py` `_allocated_ids` and `exporter.py` (both build the key through `project_keys`, but
  only the deployment's). In a workspace a revision delete goes through `WorkspaceTable.batch_delete` (since 7.28.00,
  the hard delete's one batch-delete path).
- **3c, jobs** (done, 7.29.00: the status row above): voc-jobs' status index per workspace (`gsi1pk`
  `WS#{ws}#STATUS#{status}`, from `project_keys.py` `job_status_pk`, now `shared/assist_jobs.py`'s spelling too), the
  job stores threaded (`shared/jobs.py`, `shared/job_notices.py`, `shared/assist_jobs.py`, `agents/plan_waits.py`),
  and the workspace on every worker event: a job's invoke carries `workspace_id` (written only by
  `workspace_payload`), and the three state machines (`voc-document-workflow`, `voc-research-workflow`,
  `voc-agent-run`) pass every state's Lambda a `run_scope` object their first state sets from the input's optional
  `workspace_id` (a JSONPath parameter whose path the input lacks fails the state, so a deployment run's input stays
  as it was); a worker reads either shape with `ws_repo.py` `event_scope`. The job rows are per workspace; the work is
  not yet (`PROJECT_JOB_WORK_STORE`), nor the assists' (`ASSIST_WORK_STORE`), nor the agent runs (`AGENT_RUNS_STORE`:
  the agent rows are the deployment's), so a worker handed a workspace marks its own job row failed, or refuses, and
  does nothing else. `agents/principal.py` `agent_claims` carries `voc:workspace` for a run that belongs to a
  workspace; none does yet. Two plan changes ride with it: a plan approved in a workspace waits on its source and
  scraper runs in that workspace (`plan_decide.py` passes the plan META's workspace to `plan_waits.poll`), and
  approving one needs membership in that workspace, re-checked before every step and wait. The switch (3h) passes
  the request's resolved membership to the job starters and lifts the job refusals.
- **3d, participants and persona copy** (done, 7.31.00: the status row above): voc-participants' `LIST#`, `PERSON#`
  and `EMAIL#` rows and its `gsi1` `LISTS` and `PEOPLE` partitions per workspace (`lambda/shared/participants_keys.py`),
  with the project links, the erasure and the public unsubscribe (which reads its workspace from the stored token
  row), and the workspace's index of them, `WS#{ws}#PARTICIPANTS`, which the hard delete's `participants` entry
  sweeps; the ballots' gate helpers (`api/ballots_handler.py`), `shared/engagement_identity.py` and
  `shared/engagement_config.py` `brand_name` take the workspace, and the project gate they reach still refuses it;
  persona copy within one workspace, and the promote into the company library (`shared/persona_copy_routes.py`
  `promote`, the library's routes being `shared/company_personas_routes.py`) with its avatars under `ws/{ws}/`. Still
  the deployment's, refusing a workspace: the asks, their recipients, tokens and send counters, a new person (the
  engagement settings that decide who may be added), and the ballots' own rows (each ballots helper refuses one
  itself, whatever the project gate decides). A project's links in voc-participants are swept with the project
  (`project_partitions`), not through the index. The slice that moves the asks, their recipients and the tokens into
  their workspace must first have `erase_person` erase a workspace person's asks and tokens (it refuses such a person
  today, and the erasure by email, 7.72.00, would then report it as not erased on every run). Before the asks move,
  that slice also lifts the erasure by email's cap of 500 refused people in one run (past it the job fails, named:
  the cursor carries the run's refusals; the cap holds only while no workspace person can hold asks): it keeps them
  in the job's own saved row rather than the cursor, or ends the phase with `participants_complete: false` at the cap.
- **3e, series, comments, links and objects** (done, 7.37.00: the status row above): a series keeps its id in every
  scope (`shared/document_series.py` `series_id`: project ids are unique), and its comment partition is
  `WS#{ws}#DCOMMENTS#{project}#{series}` in a workspace (`shared/project_keys.py` `comments_pk`), listed in the
  project's own partition (`COMMENT_SERIES#{series}`), so a delete finds every comment partition by a Query;
  `shared/document_comments.py`, `shared/document_review.py`'s persona review and the review routes' helpers
  (`shared/document_review_routes.py`) are threaded, their refusals gone. A review link made in a workspace is the
  workspace's own row `WS#{ws}#DOCSHARE#{id}`, listed in `WS#{ws}#DOCSHARE_IDS`, and its `DOCSHARE#{id}` row stays
  deployment-wide as a routing row naming its workspace, the layer's second entry outside a workspace
  (`shared/ws_repo.py` `route_share_id`, one `OutsideEntry` with 3a's claim, in the same transaction; the route carries the
  link's `ttl`, the registry row none), so the board's Cognito trigger (`lambda/auth/board_triggers.py`) and the
  reviewer routes resolve it from the id alone; both still refuse a workspace's link (`REVIEWER_ROUTES_STORE`) until
  the switch adds the status check. Every S3 key builder of a project's objects (avatars, product documents,
  prototypes) gives the same key under `ws/{ws}/` in a workspace; an avatar's URL is accepted only for a key of the
  reader's own scope and bucket, and no workspace avatar's or prototype's URL is signed yet. The hard delete's `docshares` entry releases the
  workspace's routes before its projects entry, which sweeps each project's comment partitions first.
- **3f, the product-doc extractor** (done, 7.38.00: the status row above): it is triggered by S3, not by an event
  that could carry a field, so a workspace's upload (`ws/{ws}/projects/{id}/product_docs/raw/…`, `api/product_context.py`
  `product_doc_raw_key`) reaches it through rules of its own (`ws/` with each upload extension as the suffix), and the
  key alone names the workspace: the extractor takes it only when the project's claim (`PROJECT_ID#{id}` / `CLAIM`)
  names that same workspace and the workspace is active, and writes only that workspace's record and text. Its own
  spelling of the keys (it cannot import `shared/`) is pinned to shared/'s by `schemas/project-keys-cases.json`, which
  both test suites read (as `schemas/conversation-keys-cases.json` pins the stream Lambda's conversation keys), the
  claim's and the status's reads included. Still refused in a workspace: an image, whose description reads the model
  picks (`SETTINGS#model`), until the picks move into the workspace; then its `bedrock_usage` line names the workspace
  too (it counts to `UNATTRIBUTED` today). Such a record is marked `failed` in its workspace with its own reason
  ("Images can't be described in a workspace yet", `WORKSPACE_IMAGE_MESSAGE`), never left `pending` for the API's stall
  check to advise an upload that cannot help; still, before 3h lets a workspace upload, either the picks have moved,
  or the upload route (`api/product_context.py` `create_upload_url`) refuses an image in a workspace before it
  writes the record. Three more checks hold in a workspace, each refusing with nothing more written: the record's
  `s3_raw_key` must be the event's key, the `extracting` write must land (any failure, not only a refused
  condition), and the workspace's status is read again just before the text is put, so a delete that began meanwhile
  gets no text under its prefix. Unlike the public links and an MCP pin, the extractor does not know only an
  id: its key names the scope (`projects/…` the deployment's, `ws/{ws}/…` one workspace's) and the claim confirms it,
  so it needs no part in 3h's refusal of an id held by both, and a workspace's upload never reads the deployment's
  `PROJECT#{id}` partition.
- **3g, export, delete and erasure**: the workspace export's project entities (**3g-a, done, 7.39.00**: the status
  row above; `lambda/jobs/workspace_export/projects.py`), found through the registry with each row's `deleted_at`
  (`registered_project`, a deleted project skipped and counted), each project's rows through `project_partitions`
  (7.27.00), so the export reaches `WS#{ws}#DOCUMENT_VERSIONS#PROJECT#{id}` and holds the saved revisions (they hold
  customer text), and each project's comment partitions, found through the `COMMENT_SERIES#` rows of its partition
  (3e: the threads hold customer text and reviewers' addresses), and the workspace's review links, found through
  `WS#{ws}#DOCSHARE_IDS` (each link's audience lists email addresses); the hard delete's projects entry (done,
  7.28.00: [Export and delete](#export-and-delete), step 4; its comments and links since 7.37.00), and an erasure
  (**3g-b, done, 7.43.00**: the status row above, with the lineage marks below), reaching them
  (`lambda/jobs/retention/project_people.py`, phase `workspace_projects`): an erasure by email without a source (the
  deployment's walks every workspace the platform lists; a workspace's own walks that workspace) de-identifies the
  link audiences that name the address (a link left admitting nobody is revoked), the threads and replies it wrote,
  every name field that is the address, and a project `META`'s `owner_email` and `members` entries, each change
  recorded before and after, and any such change closes that workspace's exports, which the `exports` phase deletes,
  as an erasure that matches a workspace's feedback item already does: so the copies an export holds since 7.39.00 go
  with them. Deleting one project keeps its
  registry row, marked deleted (`deleted_at`), and its claim, so its id is never reused and the registry still lists
  every claim; only the workspace's delete releases claims (`release_project_claim`, for every id the registry lists)
  and removes the registry rows, in that order for each id: release the claim, then delete its registry row, so a
  delete that crashes resumes from the registry and finds every claim still held. The delete role's grants on
  voc-projects: `dynamodb:DeleteItem` and `dynamodb:GetItem` with `dynamodb:LeadingKeys` `PROJECT_ID#*` only (the
  claims), and `dynamodb:Query` of `WS#{one id}#PROJECT_IDS` (the registry). `iter_registered_project_ids` yields ids
  only, for the delete, which reaches every id; since 7.39.00 a read projected to `REGISTRY_PROJECTION`, each row
  read with `registered_project`, carries `deleted_at`, so the export skips deleted projects; the erasure (7.43.00)
  reads them too, its address walk and its lineage marks alike (a deleted project's rows stay until its delete sweeps
  them). The per-project delete that follows that rule is the project
  routes' own (3h, which threads the workspace into them). `lambda/jobs/retention/lineage.py` (an erasure's marks on
  the personas, research and documents that cite an erased review) marks a workspace's artifacts in a phase of its
  own since 7.43.00 (`workspace_lineage`): for each workspace whose item the run deleted (every listed workspace
  once it de-identified a board request), its projects through the registry and its persona library, each project's
  own partition by filtered Query, a review's tombstone `{ws}#{feedback_id}` and a board request's the deployment's,
  every mark through `ws_table`; no Scan. Since 7.48.00 (3g-d) the deployment's pass reads its own projects by Query
  too, and never a workspace's rows. A workspace's product documents (3f): their records are in the project's own partition and
  their uploads and extracted text under `ws/{ws}/projects/{id}/product_docs/`, which the hard delete's objects sweep
  and the export's read of `ws/{ws}/*` already reach. In a workspace an upload is a `.md` or `.txt` file or an image
  (no PDF: the extractor's rules take none there) and an image is never extracted; so since 7.43.00 an erasure by
  email reads each text upload or its extracted text and lists for review (`files_needing_review`) those whose text
  or filename holds the address (and closes the workspace's exports), and lists apart (`files_not_checked`) what it
  cannot judge: each folder of images once, another kind of upload, text it cannot read; it changes and deletes
  nothing there.
- **3g-d, the deployment's own projects' erasure and lineage by Query** (**done, 7.48.00**: the status row above):
  every real reviewer address is in the deployment's rows today (no workspace project exists until the switch): a
  reviewer's threads in `DCOMMENTS#{project}#{series}`, a link's audience and names in `DOCSHARE#{id}`, a `META`'s
  `owner_email` and `members`, `projects/{id}/product_docs/`. The deployment kept no list of its projects that a
  delete leaves alone (the delete's fence removes a `META`'s `gsi1pk` and `gsi1sk`, so `gsi1-by-type` no longer names
  a deleted or half-deleted project), no list of its commented series and no registry of its links, and a revoke
  deletes the series' `SHARE` pointer, so a revoked link was found from nothing. Since 7.48.00 a project's create puts
  the project in the deployment's project registry (`PROJECT_IDS` / `{id}`, put if absent before its `META`,
  `lambda/shared/project_writes.py` `register_deployment_project`; the delete never removes it), the link create puts
  the link in the deployment's link registry (`DOCSHARE_IDS` / `{share id}`) and its series in the deployment's series
  registry (`COMMENT_SERIES_IDS` / `{project}#{series}`) in its own transaction, and a series' first thread puts its
  series there if absent, as a workspace's do (`lambda/shared/document_share.py`, `lambda/shared/document_comments.py`:
  the row read first, eventually consistent, so a listed series costs a read, never a write; a persona review lists it
  once for all its threads), and so does the project bundle's import for the project and its series
  (`voc-datalake/scripts/project_bundle/importer.py`); a test holds every writer of a comment partition and every
  maker of a project to it (`lambda/shared/test/test_comment_series_listed.py`; the verification fixture's project,
  which holds no person's data and expires by its `ttl`, is the one exception). The series registry is one
  deployment-wide partition, not a list in each project's partition as a workspace's is: the deployment's project
  delete empties the project's partition and leaves its comment partitions, so a list kept there would lose a deleted
  project's threads, which still hold their reviewers' addresses. What was made before is listed by a one-time
  backfill (`voc-datalake/scripts/projects/backfill_review_registries.py`: three Scans of voc-projects, the links, the
  comment partitions, then the projects' `META` rows, a put if absent each, then a marker, `DOCSHARE_IDS` /
  `MIGRATION#review-registries`, with when, the counts and the release it was taken under; dry run by default,
  idempotent, resumable from its own progress row, `MIGRATION#review-registries#progress`, each update conditioned on
  its run's id, a resume under a new run id put on the condition of the old one, the marker's put and the progress
  row's delete one transaction on the same condition), which an operator runs once after the deploy. It writes the
  marker only at the end of one applied walk nobody took over, and only once there is exactly one projects API and one
  assist twin, the writers of links, threads and projects, each reporting `APP_VERSION` 7.48.0 or later, its last update
  applied (`LastUpdateStatus` `Successful`), last updated, when the walk began, longer ago than the longer of their
  timeouts plus 5 minutes (read with the operator's own credentials, read-only; judged before an applied walk starts
  and again at its end, so a writer updated during the walk voids it), so no container of the older release could
  write a row behind the Scans. A custom resource of VocCoreStack deletes the marker when it is created (the upgrade)
  and deleted (a rollback below 7.48.00); `--unmark` deletes it for a rollback done another way. Until 7.93.00 (3g-d2,
  below: since then nothing Scans; an erasure by email without complete registries fails by name, and any other run
  owes its lineage pass), without a complete
  marker (its `completed_at`, its three counts and a `release` of 7.48.0 or later) the erasure and the
  lineage pass found the same rows by those Scans themselves (`lambda/shared/review_registries.py`: strongly
  consistent, six attributes projected, a series judged by the walker's own rule, `shared/project_walk.py`
  `is_series_ref`), so either way they reach every row the stores had written when they read that part (provided every writer since the backfill ran 7.48.00 or later: a VocApiStack-only rollback below 7.48.00, or a bundle imported from an older checkout, breaks this until `--unmark`; a
  thread written to a series already walked is the next erasure's, as in a workspace; the conservative choice: the erasure
  does not write the marker). The erasure job's row of the deployment's erasure by email without a source says how it
  found the deployment's rows (`deployment_rows_found`) and whether it walked them (`deployment_projects_walked`); no
  other job writes either. The erasure then walks the deployment's projects through `PROJECT_IDS` (or the projects'
  Scan: a deleted or half-deleted project's `META` and leftover rows too) and its persona library, with the same
  de-identification and the same code as a workspace's (`lambda/jobs/retention/project_people.py`, its `deployment`
  cursor). Its product documents have a rule of their own: the deployment allows PDF uploads and describes images
  (the extractor writes text for every kind there), so the erasure judges the extracted text of every kind (a read
  grant on `projects/*/product_docs/extracted/*` only) and lists as not checked only a document that has none. The
  deployment's lineage pass moves to the same Queries (until 7.48.00 a Scan of the whole of voc-projects, a Scale
  finding), and `UpdateItem` is pinned to the deployment's and the workspace patterns as well as to its attributes;
  the Scan grant is narrowed to the table and the six attributes, the deployment's Query grant to its partitions and to
  the attributes the walks project, and no index is granted. Not reached: the erasures an admin runs again (every erasure by
  email without a source created before the 7.48.00 deploy finished, or whose row does not say
  `deployment_projects_walked: true`; the job keeps only the address's hash), the project bundles' files on an operator's
  machine (3g-bundle), a prototype pin's reply written before 7.48.00 naming a member by address (3g-pin, reached
  since 7.98.00 but for a pin whose prototype row and pin form were both deleted, 3g-pin2; a reply since 7.48.00
  names the username, else "Team member"; TENETS.md D5), an id cited inside a document's `derivation` (its
  `input_feature_request_ids` there; the lineage filter reads the top-level lists only; 3g-lin1), and a persona
  job's `result` in voc-jobs, which holds the persona's lineage ids until the job row's 7-day `ttl` (3g-lin2). Review
  round 3: the deployment's Query grant requires `dynamodb:Select` `SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES` (every deployment Query
  sends it, `shared/project_walk.py` `query_page`), the attribute-limited UpdateItems `dynamodb:ReturnValues`
  `NONE`; the project delete sweeps its objects before its rows, and an erasure lists a deleted or half-deleted
  project's product documents folder as not checked (an object S3 refused outlives its record, and the delete still
  finishes); the erasure job's API view returns the two fields; a job naming a malformed `workspace_id` fails; a
  routing row naming no workspace id is counted malformed; the marker's reset leaves the backfill's progress row,
  which cannot earn a marker across a later deploy (the writers are judged against when the walk began).
- **3g-d2, the Scan fallback removed** (done in 7.93.00, status row above): every run that names no workspace and can delete or replay starts
  with the `registries` phase (the backfill's marker, then the gap check: the live projects on `gsi1-by-type`, keys
  only, each looked up in `PROJECT_IDS` by one BatchGetItem a page; bounded and handed over like the other phases).
  The deployment's erasure by email fails there, by name, before it deletes anything (it cannot be owed: the address is
  not stored); every other run deletes and, without complete registries, owes its deployment lineage pass in one
  durable row that the next complete run replays and clears. The worker's Scan grant on voc-projects and the walks'
  use of the Scans are gone (the Scans moved into the backfill script, which stays for the deployments that upgrade
  late; a new deployment, with no project on its first deploy, gets a complete marker from the marker's seed,
  `lib/stacks/review-registries-marker.ts`). The check cannot see an unlisted review link, an unlisted comment series or an
  unlisted deleted project (none is on the index): those stay guarded by `--unmark` alone. The marker's reset did not
  move: VocApiStack is at its 425 gate (3g-d3). Follow-ups from its review round 2: **3g-d2-mark**, a replayed mark
  names the replaying run (`erased_evidence.job_id` and `mode` are the run that made the mark, not the run that
  deleted the item), so the owed row's runs are the record of who owed it; the fix writes the owing run's id into the
  mark when a replay makes it. **3g-d2-reowe**: a run whose owed row could not be written (`owe` failed: past 1,000
  sources, a row that kept changing, an AWS error) says `lineage_owed` with a reason that no later run makes its marks,
  and its audit row lists its `erased_sources`, but nothing replays them; the fix is a backfill-style operator script
  that owes the sources of such audit rows again.
- **3h-S0-history, keep the replaced switch rows** (planned; TENETS.md D4): a rerun of the switch's check after a new
  marker replaces the stale switch row, so the earlier check's record (when, the release, who applied it) is lost; the
  check prints it before the replace (`scripts/projects/switch_projects.py`). The fix keeps each replaced row as
  history (a row of its own beside `PROJECTS_SWITCH`, put in the same transaction).
- **3g-d3, the marker's reset beside the writers** (planned; after 3g-d2; not a 3h blocker): move the reset's
  AwsCustomResource (`lib/stacks/review-registries-marker.ts`) from VocCoreStack into VocApiStack, so a VocApiStack-only
  rollback below 7.48.00 deletes the marker itself and no longer needs `--unmark` (docs/deployment.md). 3h does not need
  it: its upgrade check runs the `registries` phase's check itself (3h, below), so a marker an older single-stack
  rollback left standing over registries that miss a live project refuses the switch, and the rollback case it cannot
  see (a series or a deleted project only) is the one `--unmark` already covers, switch or not. It costs that stack
  five resources (the custom resource, its policy, and CDK's singleton provider function, role and role policy, unless
  the stack already holds an AwsCustomResource by then); measured in 7.93.00, the stack's largest shape
  (`-c performanceTier=true`, every plugin) is 425, the gate itself (`api-engagement-budget.test.ts`), so it waits
  until the stack gives five back. The release that moves it deletes the marker twice (the new resource's create, the
  old one's delete), so its upgrade note must say to run the backfill again after it.
- **3g-p, a deployment project's delete reaches its comment partitions and links** (planned; older than workspaces,
  a D5 gap): `delete_project` empties the project's partition and keeps its tombstoned `META`, but leaves its comment
  threads (`DCOMMENTS#{project}#{series}`, reviewers' addresses, no `ttl`) and its links' rows. The fix: the delete
  sweeps the comment partitions `COMMENT_SERIES_IDS` names for the project (a Query of the registry by its
  `{project}#` sort-key prefix) and revokes, then deletes, the links `DOCSHARE_IDS` names for it, then prunes those
  registry rows, last, so a delete stopped anywhere resumes from them. `DOCSHARE_IDS` keeps each link's raw id, the
  secret its URL carries, after the link expires: the same fix prunes an expired link's registry row once its row is
  gone. Once registry rows can be pruned, a thread's read of its series' row (`list_deployment_series`, eventually
  consistent today, which costs at most a repeated put) must become strongly consistent, or a thread written just
  after a prune could find a stale row and leave its series unlisted.
- **3g-lin1, the ids inside a document's `derivation`** (planned; older than workspaces, a D5 gap): a document's
  `derivation` map holds its own `input_feature_request_ids`, which the lineage marks never read (their filter and
  update name the top-level lists only), so an erased board request's id stays there. The fix: the lineage pass
  filters on, and removes from, the `derivation` copy too (its attribute added to the worker's update grant).
- **3g-lin2, a persona job's `result`** (planned; older than workspaces, a D5 gap): a persona job's row in voc-jobs
  keeps the generated personas' lineage ids in its `result` until the row's 7-day `ttl` deletes it, and no erasure
  reads voc-jobs. The fix: the erasure's lineage pass reads the deployment's and the walked workspaces' unexpired
  persona job rows and removes the erased ids, or the job row stops keeping them once the personas are saved.
- **3g-pin, prototype pin replies written before 7.48.00** (done in 7.98.00, status row above; it was a 3h blocker
  since 7.93.00): since 7.48.00 a reply names its writer by their username, else "Team member", never the address
  (`api/projects_handler.py` `_pin_actor`); a reply written before (`PINS#` in voc-aggregates) could name a member by
  address, and no erasure reached it. Now an erasure by email without a source walks the pins of the deployment's and
  the walked workspaces' prototypes and of each scope's pin forms (`lambda/jobs/retention/pins.py`, through keys only)
  and replaces such a name with "Team member", as for a thread's author.
- **3g-pin2, a pin no key names any more** (planned; a D5 gap, narrow): a pin is found through its prototype's row or
  its pin form's row. When both are gone (the prototype or its project deleted, and the form deleted from the forms
  page, which leaves the form's pins), no key names the pins' partition and the erasure cannot reach a reply there
  that names a member by address. Since 7.55.00 a form's delete writes `AUDIT#delete` / `feedback_form#{form}`, which
  still names it: the walk does not read those rows yet, so the gap is every such pin, and reading them (one keys-only
  Query in the forms pass) would narrow it to forms deleted before 7.55.00. The fix: that Query, then the form's delete
  (and the project's) de-identifying or deleting the form's pins, or a pin form registry no delete removes. An operator
  backfill that de-identifies every reply name written before 7.48.00 is the complete alternative (an owner decision:
  a migration, not a per-person erasure under a legal basis).
- **3g-pin-once, each live prototype's pins read once** (planned; Scale): the erasure reaches a live prototype's pin
  form both through the prototype and through the forms partition, so it reads those pins twice (the second walk
  changes nothing). The fix: in the prototypes pass, one keys-only read of each prototype's `FORM#` row, walking its
  pins only when the form is gone.
- **3g-bundle, the project bundles' files** (planned; a D5 gap that stays outside the stacks): an exported bundle
  (`voc-datalake/scripts/project-export.py`) copies a project's threads, reviewer addresses included, into files on
  the operator's machine, which no erasure reaches. The fix is procedural and in the scripts: the export writes the
  files' expiry and a warning, the import refuses a bundle older than its window, and the runbook says to delete a
  bundle once imported.
- **3g-ui, the erasure panel's review list** (done in 7.97.00, status row above; it was a 3h blocker): before it,
  Administration showed `files_needing_review` under the label of import originals and whole-file uploads, and the
  erasure API dropped the job row's `more` flag and did not return `files_not_checked`, so a job that listed product
  documents, or more than the first 200 keys, read as fewer import files than it held. The API now returns both lists
  with their `more` (`settings_handler.py` `erasure_job_view`), and the panels (`erasureParts.tsx`, the rules in
  `erasureOutcome.ts`) name each kind (an upload kept, an import's originals folder, a product document or library
  item that holds the address, one not checked, out of their total) and say, tied to each list, when it is cut (the
