  job keeps no count past the 200 keys of each list, only `more`: the panel says to check the rest by hand). They show
  every optional counter above 0, and a follow-up list with each reason and what to do (TENETS.md D5's rules); when in
  doubt the panel says something is still needed:
  - **the operator's**, listed first and before any rerun: owed lineage whose `lineage_owed_reason` says the
    registries are not backfilled or incomplete or its owe could not be recorded (without a code, any registries check
    but `complete`); a non-zero `participant_guards_malformed` (mend the rows, or erase by id the person each names; a
    rerun does not mend them); an index row kept again by a rerun of the same erasure that ended before this one
    started; a non-zero `project_ids_malformed` (skipped registry, link or series ids). A completed job that owes
    lineage therefore reads **Completed: needs the operator** (the worker writes `failed_before_marks` only on a failed
    run; the panel keeps that branch as a defence);
  - **submit again** an erasure by email without a source whose `participants_complete` is not true (with the kept
    index rows counted; a kept index row needs only this, a kept-again one is the operator's), beside 7.48.00's rule
    (`deployment_projects_walked` not true, the deployment's only) and 7.84.00's (the agents' memory not walked). The
    words: submit it again with the person's address and legal basis, since the job keeps only a hash of the address
    (no button submits it); a workspace's job from that workspace; only the deployment's participants rerun adds "if
    this deployment has its own erasure request for this address; otherwise the workspace that received the request
    submits it from there";
  - **check the account in Users**, not a rerun: `agent_memory_accounts_not_erased` accounts hold the address
    unverified. The words: in Users, confirm the account is the person's and verify its email, or, if it is not
    theirs, change or remove the address on it; then submit this erasure again with the same address and legal basis
    (a later run of the same erasure that reads `agent_memory_complete` true resolves it). Every deployment erasure by
    email that walked the agents' memory says the same of an account whose email changed, or whose email is unverified
    and is not its username: the address does not reach it. An erasure by email that finished before 7.84.00 on a
    deployment with the engineering agents carries no agent-memory field, so the panel shows no agent-memory rerun for
    it: run it again as 7.84.00's upgrade notes say (the view saying whether the agents run is 3g-ui-api's);
  - **files to review**: either list's keys or `more`, or a product document not checked;
  - **lineage owed** a later run makes (completed or failed), in the admin's language from the rows'
    `lineage_owed_reason` code (since 7.97.00, `lambda/shared/lineage_owed.py` `owed_code`, which the worker writes
    and the view applies to a 7.93.00 or 7.94.00 row, so an owe that could not be recorded always reads `owe_failed`).
    A replay (`lineage_owed_replayed`) says so, and a `review_registries` result other than `complete` is shown. On a
    failed job whose owed lineage a later run made, the note adds that the erasure itself still failed: see its error.
  A completed job with any of these reads, in that precedence, **Completed: needs the operator**, **Completed: run
  again**, **Completed: check the account in Users**, **Completed: files to review** or **Completed: lineage owed**,
  never plain "Completed". The client infers what a later job resolved, only on positive proof from a completed job of
  the same scope started after the older one ended (a job started while it ran may have read the owed row before it
  was written): owed lineage with a code other than `owe_failed`, when the later job replayed it or read complete
  registries owing nothing; each rerun step on its own, when a later run of the same erasure (value hash, field,
  source, workspace) reads `participants_complete` true with no row malformed or kept, `deployment_projects_walked`
  true, or `agent_memory_complete` true (the reruns read as done only once none is left; the Users step is never
  counted as a rerun). A missing field is never proof; malformed project ids are never resolved (the counter is on a
  row only once above 0). Each panel lists only its scope's jobs (inside a workspace that workspace's,
  outside one and in the whole-source panel the deployment's), as the server does, and only the newest 20
  (`MAX_LISTED_ERASURE_JOBS`): an older job's follow-ups are on its job row (`JOB#erasure`, 7-day `ttl`) and its
  `AUDIT#retention` row. A daily retention window's replay writes no erasure job, so the panel cannot see it; owed
  lineage also shows in the `retention-runs` alarm (when alerting is on), the job row and the `AUDIT#retention` rows.
- **3g-ui-open, jobs with an open follow-up stay listed** (planned, nothing built; a follow-up of 3g-ui, not a blocker
  of the switch): `GET /settings/erasure` lists the newest 20 jobs of the scope, so a job whose follow-up is still open
  (a rerun, the operator's, owed lineage) drops out of the panel once 20 newer jobs exist, and is read only on its job
  and audit rows. The list would also return the open ones past the 20 (or the panel would say how many older jobs
  still need something).
- **3g-ui-api, the follow-ups computed by the server** (planned, nothing built; a follow-up of 3g-ui, TENETS.md
  tenet 4's gap; it takes in what was 3g-ui-owed): the follow-up rules live in the web app only
  (`erasureOutcome.ts`), so an API or MCP client reads the raw job fields and must apply them itself. `erasure_job_view`
  would return `follow_ups` and `outcome` computed in Python, the TypeScript rule held to it by shared test cases (one
  JSON of rows and expected reports both suites read). With it, two things the panel cannot see today: whether a later
  run made an owing job's marks, from one GetItem of the owed row (`JOB#retention` / `LINEAGE_OWED`: whether it still
  names the job's run, so a daily retention window's replay counts too: `lineage_still_owed`), and whether the
  deployment runs the engineering agents, so an erasure by email finished before 7.84.00 there shows its agent-memory
  rerun.
- **3g-r, the board pool's accounts** (planned, nothing built; older than workspaces, not a blocker of the switch): a
  reviewer signs in to a review link, and a supporter to a board, with an account in the board user pool that keeps
  their email, and no erasure reaches it. An erasure by email deletes the board-pool user with that address (an admin
  delete of the pool's user, recorded on the job and audit rows like every other erasure step), or, where the pool
  cannot be changed by the worker, lists the account for review on the job row.
- **3g-s, a late hand-over** (planned, nothing built; older than this slice, not a blocker of the switch): the
  retention worker hands over to itself by an asynchronous invoke and has no `maxEventAge`, so a throttled hand-over
  can arrive up to 6 hours late. That is past the one-hour rule after which the whole-source lock and the start check
  (`shared/source_erasure.py` `blocking_job`) read a job as stale, so a lock can read stale while its job still runs.
  The fix: a `maxEventAge` of about 30 minutes on the worker, or the worker failing a resumed hand-over whose job row
  reads stale.
- **3g2, a participant's erasure by email** (done, 7.72.00: the status row above; no longer a 3h blocker): until
  then voc-retention never reached voc-participants, and a participant was erased by its person id only, so the same
  address in two workspaces was two unrelated people. The deployment's erasure by email looks the address up in the
  deployment's guard and in each listed workspace's `WS#{ws}#EMAIL#{sha}` (three reads per workspace: the guard's
  GetItem, a Query of that scope's people index and the GetItem of its saved row, bounded by the workspace listing
  and its re-walk, no Scan) and erases each person found with `erase_person` in that scope, under the erasure's legal
  basis, a workspace's exports closed first; a workspace's own erasure (7.09.00), given an address, looks up its own
  scope the same way (`lambda/jobs/retention/participants.py`, phase `participants`). The address is normalised as
  the guard is (`shared/engagement_common.py` `normalize_email`), not with retention's casefold, so the hash is the
  guard's. The erasure job saves the person ids it resolves, with their scopes, before it erases any, in the job's
  partition under the guard's hash (one listing page at a time, each id taken off once erased; its `ttl` 30 days
  from the last save), so a later erasure of the address finishes them by id after the guard is gone (an erasure
  deletes the guard first of the person's rows); the deployment's erasure also reads each workspace's saved row, and
  a person found already gone has a cut erasure's tail finished (the late memberships, a workspace guard's index
  row). An entry the erasure refuses (a workspace person's asks or tokens; a person still there after two "not
  found"), or whose guard's index row stays kept with no guard back, stays saved, is counted
  (`participants_not_erased`, `participant_index_rows_kept`), and the job completes saying `participants_complete:
  false` (since 7.72.03 so does a job that skipped a malformed guard, index or saved row,
  `participant_guards_malformed`, which an operator mends first: the 7.72.03 row's rerun rule); any other error fails the job, and so do more than 500 refused in one run (a cap that holds only while no
  workspace person can hold asks: lifted before the asks move, 3d). It ships with the boards' gap (TENETS.md D5): a
  board supporter, and a +1's customer contact, promoted to a list is made a participant
  (`shared/feature_requests_promote.py` `promoted_emails`), so an erasure by id leaves their board +1s and the next
  promote re-creates the participant; the erasure by email reaches both (the `boards` phase re-keys the +1s without
  the address, and removes a matching `contact_email` from a +1 or a request, first, so no later promote finds it;
  since 7.72.03 a +1's contact is judged as the +1 is read again, never as the scan saw it, and an edit between that
  read and the re-key is never lost: the 7.72.03 row above).
  Lifting `upsert_person`'s refusal of a new person in a workspace (`PARTICIPANT_SETTINGS_STORE`) is now unblocked by
  this slice; it still waits for the engagement settings to move into their workspace, and is not lifted here. Before
  that lift, and before the asks, their recipients and the tokens move into their workspace (the stores that refuse a
  workspace with `PARTICIPANT_ASKS_STORE`), `erase_person` must erase a workspace person's asks and tokens (it refuses
  such a person today, `NotPerWorkspaceError`, and the erasure by email would then keep refusing them), and the
  erasure by email must keep its run's refusals in the job's own saved row rather than the cursor, or end the phase
  with `participants_complete: false` at its cap of 500, instead of failing the job.
- **3g2-raw, an edit's raw copy behind an erasure** (narrowed in 7.72.03, not closed; found in 7.72.03's review; not a
  blocker of the switch): the boards' edit routes write, then archive (`shared/feature_requests_routes.py`
  `put_support_route`: `put_support` then `archive_support`; `put_request`: `update_request` then `archive_request`),
  each version naming whatever the row then held (a supporter's or reporter's address, a contact). Built in 7.72.03:
  the boards phase sweeps every matched row's raw folder once more after its change (a re-key, a contact's REMOVE, a
  request's de-identify), whether the change held or not (an earlier edit's version stays the erasure's to remove
  even when a later edit or a delete made the change not hold), and a re-keyed +1's old id refuses a later edit
  (`put_support(replacing=True)`, conditional on the row existing), so no archive follows it. What remains, and only
  this: (a) an edit whose write lands before the erasure's change (or its attempt) and whose archive lands after the
  second sweep, i.e. the erasure's read, write and sweep all run between the route's DynamoDB write and its one S3
  put; (b) a version archived before a job that crashed between its change and its second sweep. Either copy names the
  address and no rerun finds it once the row no longer matches. The fix to build: the route, after archiving, reads its
  row again (strongly consistent) and deletes the version it just wrote when the row is gone or no longer holds what
  the version named (one GetItem per edit, and a delete only in the race); with it, the erasure's second sweep covers
  the crash case only. TENETS.md D5 names the gap until then.
- **3g2-orphan, the raw copies of a deleted request or +1** (planned, nothing built; older than 7.72.03, found in its
  review; not a blocker of the switch): deleting a request or a +1 (`DELETE /feature-requests/{id}`,
  `DELETE …/supports/{id}`) keeps its raw copies (the raw lake keeps history), and an erasure by email finds a board
  row's raw folder only through the row, so the copies of a row deleted before the erasure, which name its reporter,
  supporter or contact, are never reached by it (only a whole-source erasure of `feature_requests` removes them). The
  fix to build, either: the delete routes delete the row's raw folder with the row (the copies then follow the row, as
  the erasure's do), or the erasure lists the boards' raw prefix by the address's own folders (which needs an index:
  a folder is named by the row's id, not the address). TENETS.md D5 names the gap until then.
- **3g2-serial, two of the deployment's erasures by email at once** (planned, nothing built; found in 7.72.03's
  review): nothing serialises them (a workspace's erasure holds its `LOCK` row, `shared/erasure_jobs.py`, and an
  erasure of one source its own, `shared/source_erasure.py`; the deployment's erasure by email holds neither), and the
  contact's REMOVE (`boards.py` `remove_fields`) does not move a row's `updated_at`, so a re-key by one job (the +1's
  own address) racing a contact REMOVE by another (its contact) can put the contact back under the new id, the second
  job's row reading complete. A rerun of that erasure finds the row again (it still holds the address). The fix: the
  REMOVEs set `updated_at` too (the re-key's condition then sees them), or a deployment-wide lock for erasures by email.
- **3g3** was one slice, split in three (7.80.00): the general agents first, then their lineage and memory erasure,
  then the engineering agents. The line between them: an **engineering agent** is a voc-agents agent row with
  `engineering` settings (`shared/agents_store.py` `is_engineering_agent`), and everything it alone uses is 3g3-c.
- **3g3-a, the general agents and their workflows per workspace** (done, 7.80.00: the status row above): inside a
  workspace the agents, their pause and resume history, their workflows with every revision, and the keys its runs
  will use (none written yet; the agents sweep already takes them) are that workspace's own (`WS#{ws}#AGENT#…`,
  `#WORKFLOW#…`, `#RUN#…`, the listing index's `WS#{ws}#AGENTS`, `#WORKFLOWS`, `#RUNS_ACTIVE`, and the registry
  `WS#{ws}#AGENT_REGISTRY`, every key built by shared/ws_repo.py); the agents and workflows routes resolve
  `X-Workspace-Id`; the workspace export carries `agents_workflows` and the hard delete has its `agents` sweep. Inside
  a workspace no agent is seeded, no engineering agent or flow is kept (refused: the deployment's until 3g3-c; the
  template copy a new agent gets is never one there), an agent's `owner_sub`, when a request names one, is one of the
  workspace's current members, and no run starts: an agent there is kept and edited, never turned on or run (enable,
  Run now, cancel and decision answer 409 `runs_not_in_workspace`), since the heartbeat and the run interpreter read
  the deployment's partitions only (`AGENT_RUNS_STORE`, below, unchanged). The web app's agents and workflows calls
  and the assistant's agents tools send the `'deployment'` scope (no `X-Workspace-Id`), inside a workspace too, so the
  Agents page, the run monitor and the assistant keep the deployment's agents everywhere; a workspace's own agents are
  reached through the API alone until 3g3-ui-agents. An MCP token minted inside a workspace sends its header to the
  agents API too: its `agents_list` is the workspace's agents, and it is not offered `agents_run` (docs/mcp.md). "Build
  from this PRD" is not offered inside a workspace, and the
  agents API builds only a PRD of the deployment's own projects (`_require_deployment_prd`, below). The plans' routes
  (the assistant's plan mode, each plan its approver's, its workspace on its `META` since 7.29.00) are not agents and
  stay as they are. **D5**: a workspace's agents and workflows are its artifacts (TENETS.md D5) and go with its hard
  delete (the `agents` sweep, every row they have); `DELETE /agents/<id>` and `DELETE /workflows/<id>` only archive
  one (the runs' history kept, as in the deployment), so a person-level delete of one agent or workflow in a workspace
  is a follow-up, 3g3-delete. Follow-ups:
  - **3g3-ui-agents**, the Agents page for a workspace: each control gated on the person's workspace role (any member
    reads, its admins write), the engineering pages on the deployment's scope, a notice that automations can be set up
    there but not run yet, Turn on and Run now disabled with that reason, Pause and Resume hidden; the agents and
    workflows calls in workspace scope then.
  - **3g3-r**, an agent's runs in its workspace (after 3h, since it needs the project stores per workspace): a run's
    start checks its agent's `personas.fixed` ids against the run's workspace; `AGENT_RUNS_STORE` lifted, the run
    interpreter, its nodes and the persona panel reading the run's workspace; `principal.call` sends the run's
    `X-Workspace-Id` to the APIs it calls; the heartbeat walks the workspaces (a Scale note: one listing Query per
    active workspace per tick, so the tick's reads grow with the workspaces, which needs a registry of the workspaces
    with agents turned on or a cap per tick); its `owner_sub` re-checked as a current member at each start; each run's
    registry row `RUN#{run}`, which the `agents` sweep already deletes.
  - **3g3-plans**, the plans' rows in the export and the hard delete.
  - **3g3-delete**, a real delete of one agent or workflow in a workspace (D5: its rows, its runs' rows and its
    registry row), beside the archive.
- **3g3-b, the agents' lineage and memory erasure** (done, 7.84.00: the status row above; no longer a 3h blocker):
  with `-c engineeringAgents=true` the erasure's lineage pass reaches the review ids an engineering run keeps, its run
  `META`'s `feedback.ids` and an `ISSUE_RUN#` claim's `feedback_id` (the agent run row keeps only a count), each
  removed and the row marked `erased_evidence`, only the deployment's tombstones applying (D8), in every erasure of the
  deployment (a rerun of an older one included); and the deployment's erasure by email reaches the agents' memory: the
  address resolved to its account's `sub` with one `AdminGetUser` (a verified email only), saved first, then
  `erasure.py` `erase_person_rows` (the store's own erasure) under the job's legal basis; voc-engineering is under the
  audit-row guard. The deployment-wide walks attribute each workspace they enter (the log follow-up of 7.76.00). Left,
  each named in the status row, each a follow-up: **3g3-b-old-runs**, a run started before the runs list (7.47.00 to
  7.50.00) has no list row to find it by; **3g3-b-versions**, a memory version's own `feedback_ids`; **3g3-b-accounts**,
  a deleted account's memories (no address names it any more: the account's `sub` recorded with the erasure request, or
  the memory erased by `DELETE /users/{username}` before the account goes, would close it); **3g3-b-index**, the
  reverse index that replaces the walk of every run (its trigger in the status row's Load); **3g3-b-paging**, one
  person's memory erasure handing over between partitions (today one step, about 12,500 rows per connection at the
  store's caps; a rerun finishes one the timeout cut); **3g3-b-text**, a run's
  typed `goal` and the agents' session transcripts, which may carry a review's words.
- **3g3-c, the engineering agents' data per workspace** (planned, nothing built; after 3h, the switch): with
  `-c engineeringAgents=true` the engineering agents are the deployment's. They are for the deployment's
  administrators only and read no `X-Workspace-Id`; a workspace may not hold one (refused since 7.80.00). Their
  `WS#default#…` rows and `ws/default/…` objects are their own fixed engineering scope
  (`lambda/engineering/connectors/identity.py` `WORKSPACE`), in tables and a bucket of their own, not a workspace of
  this design: 3g and 3h must never take them for a workspace's keys or sweep them as one. **Why it follows the
  switch**: the engineering agents read only the deployment's own data, and the server holds them to it. An
  engineering agent's runs take only the `DATE#` and `CHANNEL#issue` partitions, never a workspace's `WS#{ws}#…` (D8).
  A PRD reaches a run only through a Run now or a run request (the MCP tool `engineering_run_request`, then its
  confirm), and since 7.80.00 the agents API refuses both, before anything is asked or started, unless the PRD's
  project is one of the deployment's: it reads the project's `PROJECT#{id}` `META` (`agents_handler.py`
  `_require_deployment_prd`, its role's GetItem held to `PROJECT#*`) and answers one 404 for a workspace's project,
  no project or one being deleted. The web app's "Build from this PRD" is not offered inside a workspace either, but
  the server's check is the guard. So after the switch no workspace's data reaches a deployment agent's run, and
  nothing of theirs is a workspace's to export or delete. This slice moves each into the workspace it
  belongs to, through the workspace layer:
  voc-engineering's rows (runs, spend and budget alerts, conversations, the board, skills and scorecards, a person's
  own skill versions among them since 7.52.00's integration, the agents' memory, and since 7.52.00 each repository connection's knowledge packs with their docs' renderings,
  `WS#default#REPO#<connection>` / `KPACK#…`, `KDOCS`, `KDOCSLOG#<n>`, its tenets' versions, `TENETS`, `TENETS#<v>`,
  `TENETSLOG#<v>#<event>`, `TENETSRUN#<run>`, and the docs writes' claims, `WS#default#DOCS#<connection>`: the routes
  of `lambda/shared/agents_engineering_knowledge_routes.py`, and the Registry's cards made from a skill,
  `WS#default#AGENTCARD#<id>` with their listing `WS#default#AGENTCARD#_list`); the voc-eng-workspace bucket's objects
  (the knowledge packs' `ws/default/knowledge/<connection>/…`; since 7.52.00 the understanders' tenets records,
  `ws/default/runs/<run>/<agent>/commits/<sha>/_tenets/attempt-<n>/tenets.json`; since 7.50.00 also every agent session's transcript,
  `ws/default/runs/<run>/<agent>/commits/<sha>/_transcripts/attempt-<n>/<chunk>.jsonl`, each run's manifests
  `ws/default/runs/<run>/_manifest/v<nnnn>.json` and the transcripts' index `ws/default/transcripts-index/<flow>/<day>/<run>.json`,
  and since 7.51.00 a child run's seeds `ws/default/runs/<run>/_seed/<task>/seed.json` and `patch.diff`, with the
  run's `DIRECTION#v<n>`, `ASSERT#<step>#<attempt>` and `CHILD#<child>` rows and the runs list's
  `WS#default#RUNLIST#<yyyy-mm>` rows in voc-engineering:
  kept forever by the owner's decision, no expiry, so the only removals are this slice's hard-delete sweep and an
  erasure of a person's data where it requires one, D5; a transcript holds repository, issue and model text, redacted
  when written, and may hold a handle a person wrote on an issue); in voc-agents, the hard cap `SETTINGS#engineering` (a deployment
  setting, "Per-workspace configuration" above) and the repository connections with their audit and CI attestation
  rows, and, since 7.45.00, the deployment's GitHub Apps (`CONNECTIONS#default` / `GITHUB_APP#<app_id>`, with their
  `github_app_created` and `github_app_removed` audit rows) and the connect flow's single-use setup states
  (`CONNECTIONS#default#SETUP` / `STATE#<hash>`, which voc-agents' TTL removes a day after they are minted), and,
  since 7.47.00, the engineering agents (agents with `engineering` settings: the seeded Bug fixer and Feature builder,
  their seed markers `AGENTSEED#bug-fixer` and `AGENTSEED#feature-builder`; since 7.52.00 an agent's pause and resume
  history, `AGENT#<id>` / `LOG#<at>#<n>`, any agent's, and an engineering run's `expansion`) and the GitHub issues' run claims
  (`ISSUE_RUN#default` / `<owner/name>#<n>`, one per issue, ever, and since 7.50.00 its triage stage's
  `<owner/name>#<n>#triage` beside it and the issue's trusted triage `ISSUE_TRIAGE#default` / `<owner/name>#<n>`: the
  claim of a workspace's issue must be that
  workspace's, never shared across workspaces; until this slice ships, an engineering agent's runs take only the
  deployment's own feedback, D8: `DATE#` and `CHANNEL#issue`, never a workspace's `WS#{ws}#…` partitions; and Build
  from this PRD's `prd` reference, `{project_id, document_id}`, must name its workspace, and until this slice ships the
  agents API refuses one whose project is not the deployment's (7.80.00, above), so after the switch a workspace's PRD
  never reaches a deployment agent's run); each connection's SSM credential,
  `/voc/engineering/{connection_id}`, gaining the `{workspace_id}/` prefix docs/autonomous-engineering.md promises,
  and the Apps' parameters too (`/voc/engineering/_github-app/<app_id>/key` and `…/webhook`); since 7.57.00 the
  public repositories read only and the forks (connection rows of kind `public_read` and `fork`, a fork's owner a
  person of the workspace) and the upstream pull requests' records (`WS#default#WRITES#<run>` /
  `UPSTREAM_PULL_REQUEST`); the github_issues
  source's `github_app_id` (a deployment-wide run's sign-in with one of those Apps: a workspace's run ignores it, and
  the token function refuses a request naming a workspace, until the Apps are a workspace's); and the checkout cache
  under the raw bucket's `engineering/cache` prefix. With
  them: the workspace export of these families (beside 3g3-a's `agents_workflows`), a hard-delete sweep for every
  family above (and its grants), and an engineering agent allowed in a workspace. Until it ships the raw-access guard
  counts these modules' accesses, and the engineering routes stay on the not-per-workspace list
  (`lambda/api/test/cross_workspace_pending.py`).
- **3h, the switch** (no longer blocked by 3g-pin, done in 7.98.00 (an erasure by email reaches a prototype pin's reply
  written before 7.48.00 that names a member by address); 3g-pin2 is not a blocker: a pin a workspace holds is written
  after the switch, so its replies never name a member by address, and the gap is the deployment's older pins (this
  holds while 3h moves no deployment project, or its pins, into a workspace and the bundle importer stays the
  deployment's: any later move carries the pins' de-identification with it); nor by
  3g-ui, done in 7.97.00 (an admin reads what an erasure left for review and rerun), by 3g-d2, done in 7.93.00, by 3g3-a, done in 7.80.00, or by 3g3-b, done in
  7.84.00; 3g-d3 is not a blocker (3g-d3 says why); 3g3-c follows it, since the engineering agents read only the deployment's own data, D8. Before the switch, the
  server's PRD check must hold: a Run now or a run request naming a PRD whose project is not the deployment's is
  refused (done in 7.80.00, `agents_handler.py` `_require_deployment_prd`, tests in
  `lambda/api/test/test_agents_engineering_agents.py` and `test_agents_engineering_run_requests.py`), and 3h must
  keep it. It is no longer blocked by 3g-b, which made an
  erasure by email reach the addresses the export copies, 7.43.00, by 3g-d, which made it reach the deployment's own
  projects, 7.48.00, or by 3g2, which made it reach the participants, 7.72.00. 3g-d2 removed the erasure's Scan
  fallback (7.93.00: every run that names no workspace and can delete or replay checks the review registries first, in its `registries`
  phase), and the switch's upgrade check is a second guard, which 3h builds (**done in 7.100.00, slice 3h-S0**:
  `scripts/projects/switch_projects.py`, run by the operator, not in a Lambda, so with no 15-minute ceiling; the switch
  is on only while its row names the current, complete marker, `lambda/shared/project_scope.py` `projects_switched`): it refuses to switch a deployment whose marker is not there and complete
  (lambda/jobs/retention/registries.py `marker_present`), and runs the same gap check to its end (`registries.step`:
  every live project on `gsi1-by-type` in `PROJECT_IDS`, one index page and one BatchGetItem a step), refusing on a
  gap with the same next steps (`--unmark`, the backfill, then the switch again). Its time budget: one step per 100
  live projects, two requests each (at a few tens of milliseconds a request, 10,000 live projects take some seconds),
  run by the operator with no Lambda ceiling; past 100,000 steps (10 million live projects) it refuses to switch,
  named, rather than switch unchecked. An `--unmark` after the switch turns it off by design, the safe direction:
  the open question whether an unmark may undo the switch is settled that way.) The project routes, the MCP routes (`api/mcp_tokens_handler.py`,
  `shared/mcp_oauth_consent_routes.py`), the briefing (`briefing/build.py`, `briefing/handler.py`) resolve
  `X-Workspace-Id` and pass `workspace=` to every store (**the project core routes done in 7.103.00, slice 3h-S3a,
  behind one seam that answers the deployment until the flip, 3h-S12**: `shared/project_request_scope.py`
  `project_scope`, its `RESOLVE` flag off; the list and details, the get, the chat context, the update, the sharing
  routes, the documents' create, edit, versions, restore and duplicate, and the personas' edit, note and protection;
  every other project route is refused by name in a workspace until its slice, `api/projects_handler.py` `NOT_YET`
  naming each one's: S3b, S3c, S4 to S8; the MCP routes and the briefing are S9 and S10. Those refusals are a
  `NotPerWorkspaceError`, a 500, while no workspace can reach them; the flip, 3h-S12, empties `NOT_YET` and turns any
  refusal it still keeps on purpose into a 4xx whose text says what to do); a project's product documents
  (`api/product_context.py`) are threaded too: `create_upload_url` writes the record in the workspace's partition with
  its `ws/{ws}/…` upload key (and refuses an image there until the model picks move, 3f), the product-context reads
  (`list_docs`, `build_product_context_block`, `build_visual_brief_block`) and the stall check (`_fail_if_stalled`)
  read and fail the workspace's records, a document's delete (`delete_doc`) deletes the workspace's record, then its
  upload and its text by their `ws/{ws}/` keys, and the projects API's role gains the S3 grants on
  `ws/{one id}/projects/*/product_docs/*` that the deployment's `projects/*/product_docs/*` has (the upload's presigned
  put, the text's read, the delete); the per-project delete (`api/projects.py` `delete_project`,
  and `delete_document`'s revision sweep) reaches the partitions keyed by its id through `project_partitions`, the workspace's
  `WS#{ws}#DOCUMENT_VERSIONS#PROJECT#{id}` included, and before them the comment partitions the project's series list
  names (3e: the list is in the project's own partition, so it goes last), and before them it revokes the project's
  review links, every one its registry names for the project, revoked ones too (since 7.37.00 each registry row names
  its link's `project_id` and `series_id`: the link's routing row released, its own row deleted, and its registry row
  pruned or kept as 3g's rule for ids decides), so no link of a deleted project still admits a reviewer; the refusal of
  a workspace's links (`REVIEWER_ROUTES_STORE`) lifts in its two readers together: the reviewer routes once the
  projects API reads the workspace's status before every read of the workspace's link, document or threads as well
  as before every write, and the board's sign-up trigger, which gains the same status check (a GetItem of the
  workspace's `META` status, its own grant) and then reads the link from `WS#{ws}#DOCSHARE#{id}`, its GetItem on
  voc-projects regaining `WS#*#DOCSHARE#*` (`dynamodb:LeadingKeys`, held to `DOCSHARE#*` since 7.37.00) with that
  read; a workspace reviewer's daily counters (`WS#{ws}#DCRATE#{share}#{digest}`, a count only) are left to their
  two-day `ttl`, as `test/families.py` says, and no sweep is planned for them; a deleted
  comment's `AUDIT#delete` row (`shared/document_review_routes.py`) names its workspace once one is passed; a workspace's
  avatars and prototypes are under `ws/{ws}/`, which the `/avatars/*` and `/prototypes/*` CloudFront behaviours do not
  serve, so this slice decides how they are served (CloudFront behaviours for `/ws/*/avatars/*` and
  `/ws/*/prototypes/*` only, never `/ws/*`, which would serve every object of every workspace; or presigned S3) and
  lifts both refusals together: `get_avatar_cdn_url` (whose own-workspace and bucket checks are in place since
  7.37.00, `_workspace_avatar_url` the one place that gives None) and `prototype_signed_url`; the roles that read those
  objects gain S3 grants on `ws/*/prototypes/*` and `ws/*/avatars/*` (their grants in
  `lib/stacks/api-job-lambdas.ts` and `lib/stacks/agent-runtime.ts`); and the project bundle's `remap.py` and `exporter.py` read a
  project's version partition in its scope; MCP tokens and grants record the workspace they are minted in
  (`api/mcp_global_handler.py`, `shared/mcp_oauth_routes.py`). The assistant's project tools and the chat-context route
  reach projects through the projects API with `X-Workspace-Id`, so the switch covers them; a test pins that they send
  it. The access gate decides a workspace's project from that workspace's own membership (its admin bypass the
  workspace admin's, never the deployment's `admins`, and `public` meaning the workspace's members), and its refusals
  go; `require_viewable_pin` threads the workspace to `read_gate_meta` (**done in 7.101.00, slice 3h-S2**: the
  route hands in the membership it resolved, built only by `shared/workspace.py` `membership_of`; a non-member and an
  agent get the one 404 before any read (the agent's refusal carries 3g3-a's owner decision 1, no run in a
  workspace, and is revisited with 3g3-r); a removed member loses the project within the 30 s membership cache, at
  once when the route resolves fresh; the workspace's viewer role caps at view; a META of another scope is refused;
  each route passes its workspace and membership in its own later slice, and the callers that hold none yet keep
  refusing a workspace themselves, `PROJECT_GATE_STORE`; the deployment's `public` is unchanged pending
  [decision 23](#decisions)). In its slice, the project members route (`POST /projects/{id}/members`, and the
  ownership transfer) accepts only the workspace's current members (a strongly consistent read of the person's
  `MEMBER#` row; 400 otherwise), and its picker (`GET …/members/candidates`) lists only them, so a project's members
  map never names someone outside its workspace (the gate would give them nothing anyway) (**done in 7.103.00, slice
  3h-S3a**, behind the seam, at the moment of each write: a member added is a member then, and someone removed from
  the workspace later stays in the map and gets nothing; the new owner must be an editor or an admin there, and a
  previous owner who left is not re-added; the picker reads the workspace's status once and each directory match's
  `MEMBER#` row, so a member past the directory's first page per search is not listed (follow-up: a search of the
  workspace's own `MEMBER#` rows, with its Query grant); the create refuses a viewer and an agent there too).
  `workspace` becomes a required keyword (no
  default) in the gate and every store (done in 7.99.00, slice 3h-S1, for the gate and the stores listed in
  `GUARDED`: every call that took the default names `workspace=None`, the deployment, until its route or job resolves
  its scope; the other project-adjacent stores in 7.102.00, 3h-S1b, below). Two checks keep a store from dropping
  its workspace: a static check that every
  resolved route, and every non-API Lambda that reads projects (the Cognito triggers, retention), passes `workspace=`
  to every store it calls, with a guard test that fails when a module reading `get_projects_table` passes none (the
  call sites; done in 7.99.00, `lambda/shared/test/test_project_workspace_keyword.py`: every call of a guarded store
  from `lambda/`, `plugins/` and `scripts/` passes `workspace=` by name, a module's own calls included, whether or
  not it reads `get_projects_table`, a held scope is passed on, and the literal Nones are a ratchet; its blind spots
  below); and the per-store scope tests (`lambda/shared/test/test_project_stores_workspace.py`), which catch a
  store that drops its workspace on an inner call (a row written or read in the wrong scope). The projects API's role
  gains the claim's own grant (3a's rows, 7.26.00): `dynamodb:PutItem` and `dynamodb:GetItem` on voc-projects with
  `dynamodb:LeadingKeys` `PROJECT_ID#*` only (`ForAllValues:StringLike`), in a statement of its own, so the claim
  grant itself reaches no `PROJECT#` or `WS#` partition (a Put inside TransactWriteItems is authorized as PutItem).
  Every role that reads a project by its id alone (the public links, an MCP pin) gains `dynamodb:GetItem` on
  voc-projects with `dynamodb:LeadingKeys` `PROJECT_ID#*`, for `project_claim`. A project delete in a workspace
  marks its registry row `deleted_at` and keeps its claim (3g's rule). **Switch safety** (the deployment's creates
  never put a claim, decided in 3a): no workspace project can exist before the switch, since no route passes a
  workspace until then; a project id starts with the UTC second it was minted in and carries 32 random bits
  (`shared/ids.py`), so a workspace's new id equals an older deployment id only if the clock steps back to that
  second and the 32 bits match too; and after the switch a reader that knows only an id (the public links, an MCP
  pin; not the product-doc extractor, whose key names its scope, 3f) looks up both the claim and the deployment's
  `PROJECT#{id}` row, and refuses when both exist. The test of that refusal belongs to this slice. A workspace's
  image upload waits on 3f's condition: the model picks moved into the workspace, or the upload route refusing an
  image there before it writes a record. **The jobs at the switch** (3c's refusals): the
  job starters (`api/projects_handler.py` `_job_event`, `_started_job`, `assist_jobs.start` / `start_owned` / `get`)
  pass the request's resolved membership, and `PROJECT_JOB_WORK_STORE` (`shared/jobs.py` `job_handler`, the document
  generator's and the research workflow's states) and `ASSIST_WORK_STORE` (`shared/assist_jobs.py`) lift in this slice,
  each once every store its work reaches (the feedback reads, the prompt labels, the company context, the objects
  under `ws/{ws}/`, 3e) takes the workspace, never before; the static check above covers the job starters and
  `agents/conductor/plan_decide.py` (every `plan_waits.poll` names the plan's workspace or, for a project job, none,
  until this slice moves the project jobs). The job workers' and the projects API's IAM on voc-jobs is checked to
  reach the `WS#{ws}#PROJECT#*` and `WS#{ws}#ASSIST#*` partitions and their status index partitions, pinned by a CDK
  test, before any starter passes a workspace. Beside them, the two refusals the 7.37.00 merge added for voc-wiki's
  paths lift here too, each only once what it reaches takes the workspace: `REVISE_FROM_COMMENTS_STORE`
  (`shared/document_revision.py` `run_revision`, `read_evidence`) once its evidence read does (today `read_evidence`
  queries the deployment's feedback on `gsi4-by-feedback-id`, `feedback_id_partition(None, …)`); and
  `PROTOTYPE_BUILDER_STORE` (`shared/prototype_versions.py` `PrototypeStore` and its object reads and writes,
  `shared/prototype_drafts.py`, `shared/prototype_feedback.py`, `shared/prototype_incorporation.py`) once a draft's
  config (`prototype_drafts.draft_config`, with the workspace named in the draft job's event) and its objects do, and
  once the pins below are in a workspace. Revise from comments' stores and the object helpers are
  already passed the same `workspace` behind the refusal. The Prototype builder's are not: `PrototypeStore` keeps no
  workspace, and `version_from_html`, `restore_prototype_version`, `save_walkthrough`, `draft_config` and the draft Apply still
  reach the deployment's keys, so 3h must keep the store's workspace and pass it through those methods before the
  builder's refusal lifts. Serving a workspace's prototypes needs, besides
  `/ws/*/prototypes/*`, a behaviour for `/ws/*/prototypes/*/drafts/*` whose narrower draft CSP wins over it (as
  `/prototypes/*/drafts/*` does today, `lib/stacks/core-cdn.ts`), and `prototype_draft_signed_url` lifts together with
  `prototype_signed_url`. **Prototype pins block this slice**: a prototype version's pin form is written to the
  deployment's `FEEDBACK_FORM` / `FORM#{form_id}` row and its pins to `PINS#{form_id}` / `PIN#{pin_id}` in
  voc-aggregates (`shared/prototype_pins.py` `pin_form_item`, `pins_pk`), outside any workspace, and pins hold tester
  text. Before the builder's refusal lifts, either this slice scopes both under `WS#{ws}#` (the pins store gaining a
  keyword-only `workspace`, through `ws_table`), or 3g sweeps them through the project's forms (a form names its
  `project_id` and `document_id`) for the export, the delete and an erasure; until one of the two ships, 3h does not.
  `AGENT_RUNS_STORE` (agents/conductor, agents/nodes,
  agents/persona_panel) lifts only when the agents' runs move into a workspace (3g3-r: the agent rows are a
  workspace's since 7.80.00, 3g3-a, but no run starts there), a slice after this one; then
  `agents/principal.py` `call` sends the run's workspace as `X-Workspace-Id` (as the plan step sends its plan's), not
  only the `voc:workspace` claim `agent_claims` already builds.
- **3h-S1b, the rest of the stores join the guard** (**done in 7.102.00**, with `persona_overlap.py`,
  `deleted_sources.py` and `lambda/product_doc_extractor/handler.py` found beside the list; `LITERAL_NONE` 191 to
  265, every new literal a deployment-only call; the version row says what moved): the other project-adjacent modules
  that still default to `workspace=None` (`shared/jobs.py`, `job_notices.py`, `company_personas.py`,
  `persona_save.py`, `persona_copy.py`, `persona_copy_routes.py`, `persona_replace.py`, `persona_grounding.py`,
  `voice_sessions.py`, `human_documents.py`, `assist_jobs.py`, `engagement_identity.py`'s gate wrappers
  (`require_project_access`, `can_view_project`), `decision_records.py`, and the wrappers in `api/ballots_handler.py`
  and `agents/plan_waits.py`, among others) lose the default and join `GUARDED`
  (`lambda/shared/test/test_project_workspace_keyword.py`), their callers naming their scope, so no store the switch
  lifts a refusal for can still fall back to the deployment. With it: `api/projects_handler.py`'s `version_html`
  calls that hold a `PrototypeStore` pass `store.workspace` (the ratchet's count goes down), and
  `test_project_keys.py` pins `persona_keys` against the keys hand-built before 7.99.00 and that leaving `workspace`
  out raises `TypeError` (its default and explicit-None assertions became one).
- **3h-S3c, the projects API's other workspace-wide and persona routes** (follow-up, filed in 7.103.00; planned): the
  routes 3h-S3a leaves refused in a workspace that belong to no other slice (`api/projects_handler.py` `NOT_YET`,
  `3h-S3c`): the company persona library (`/company-personas/*`, its reserved container), the prioritization board
  (`/projects/prioritization*`, whose rows and ballots `BALLOT_ROWS_STORE` keeps the deployment's), the persona copy
  and promote (`shared/persona_copy_routes.py`) and the personas' grounding (evidence pack, freshness, refresh), the
  decisions (create, edit, pursue), the Validate step (`shared/project_validation_routes.py`) and the voice sessions
  (`shared/voice_session_routes.py`, `/projects/voice/usage` included). Each takes the request's scope through the
  seam and its stores' workspace, or keeps a named refusal the flip turns into a 4xx. When it moves the board's row checks, it threads both the gate and the reads (`projects_handler.py` `_gate_meta_batch_chunk`'s callers decide the gate in the request's scope but read with the deployment's keys today, unreachable while refused); the same holds for 3h-S7 with `_pin_prototype_row` and the recompose row check. Open with it: `_require_ai_writing` reads the caller's and the owner's AI-writing mode from the deployment's aggregates in a workspace (the person's own setting; an agent is refused there anyway).
- **3h-S1c, the other phases' stores still defaulting** (follow-up, filed in 7.102.00): outside the projects' modules,
  stores still take `workspace` with a default, the deployment: the participants (`shared/participants_store.py`,
  `participants_lists.py`, `participants_keys.py`, `participants_asks.py`, `participants_intake.py`,
  `participants_erasure.py`), the Documents Library's keys (`shared/library_keys.py`), the agents' memory
  (`shared/memory_store.py`), the radar stores (`radar_store.py`, `radar_releases.py`, `radar_reviews.py`,
  `radar_views.py`, `radar_counts.py`) and the agents store (`shared/agents_store.py`), with others of their phases
  (`company_context.py`, `category_gate.py`, the memory extractor's and radar scan's handlers). The same class of
  risk as 3h-S1b's: a call that forgets the request's scope falls back to the deployment without a sign. Each joins
  `GUARDED` (or a guard of its own phase) when its phase's switch comes near, not in 3h, which moves projects only.
- **An owner per pending literal None** (follow-up, filed in 7.102.00; not built yet): each `LITERAL_NONE` entry of
  the keyword guard names who lowers it, a 3h slice id (the route's or the job's that resolves its scope) or
  `by-design` (the bundle scripts, the backfill, a credential's pin until 3h-S9), checked by the guard, so the
  pending Nones burn down per slice and an entry no slice owns shows.
- **The research steps' scope** (note, 7.102.00): each research step checks its run's scope first
  (`lambda/research/research_step_handler.py`, `require_deployment(event_scope(event), PROJECT_JOB_WORK_STORE)`) and then names the
  deployment's job row by a literal `workspace=None`. When its slice lifts that refusal, the step passes the scope it
  checked to its job-status and notice calls instead, as its error step already passes the run's scope; unchanged
  until then.
- **3h-S9, a credential's pin in a workspace** (planned): today a token's or a grant's project pin is checked against
  the deployment's projects (`api/mcp_tokens_handler.py` `_token_project`, `shared/mcp_oauth_consent_routes.py`
  `approve_consent`, each `require_viewable_pin(…, workspace=None)` marked `# deployment:`), even for a credential
  minted in a workspace. This slice confirms what the MCP server does with a workspace credential whose pin names a
  deployment project (refuse it, or check the pin in the credential's workspace), then passes the credential's
  workspace and drops the markers.
- **The guard's blind spots** (3h, with the switch): `test_project_workspace_keyword.py` cannot see a store's method
  called on an instance (`store.method(…)`; the method's signature is held, its calls are not), `import shared` then
  attribute access, a guarded name re-exported by a module outside `GUARDED`, `getattr` or `importlib`, a name
  rebound by assignment, and a scope held as an attribute or under another name (`store.workspace`, `ctx.scope`,
  `self.scope`: the held-scope rule sees the bare names `workspace`, `scope` and `ws` only). Before the switch lifts a store's refusal, each such call into it is either rewritten to a
  form the guard resolves or covered by that store's scope test (`test_project_stores_workspace.py`).

Load: inside one workspace every project's `META` write lands in that workspace's one `WS#{ws}#TYPE#PROJECT` index
partition, as every project's does in `TYPE#PROJECT` today, and since 3a every create also writes one row in the
workspace's registry, `WS#{ws}#PROJECT_IDS`: a second hot partition per workspace, one write per create.

Load of the product-doc extractor (3f, 7.38.00): its `ws/` rules match by suffix, so beside each workspace upload
(five reads: the claim, the status twice and the record in DynamoDB, the upload's S3 GetObject; three writes:
two updates of the record and the text's PutObject; a refused update adds one GetItem of the record, and one refused
because the record is gone adds the text's DeleteObject) it is also invoked, and exits
after the key check with no AWS call, for every other object under `ws/` that ends in `.gif`, `.jpg`, `.md`,
`.png`, `.txt` or `.webp`: its own `.txt` output (one per text extraction, as under `projects/` today), a
workspace's persona avatars (`ws/{ws}/avatars/…`, in the raw data bucket), and any `.txt` or `.md` original the S3
import copies under `ws/{ws}/raw/` (since 7.86.00, a workspace's upload or reprocess keeps its whole-file
original there). It has no reserved concurrency, so those
invocations share the account's unreserved concurrency with every other function; at 10x uploads the claim reads
spread over one partition per project (`PROJECT_ID#{id}`) and the status reads over one per workspace (`WS#{ws}`),
no hot key. A later simplification: S3's notifications sent to EventBridge, whose rules match a key by wildcard
(`ws/*/projects/*/product_docs/raw/*`), would invoke it for the uploads alone, at EventBridge's per-event price.

## Credentials

Decision 19 (since 7.12.00; it replaces decision 8's "one Secrets Manager secret per workspace"): a workspace's
credentials are SSM Parameter Store SecureStrings, standard tier (no monthly fee, at most 4 KB each), encrypted with
the deployment's customer-managed key, one per plugin namespace, so no parameter grows with the number of plugins.
A plugin's config list is not a credential and is not in SSM: it is a row of voc-aggregates.

- **Layout.** The prefix is `/voc/ws` (`/<prefix>-voc/ws` with `-c deploymentPrefix`, the convention of the Radar
  Exa key in [radar.md](radar.md)):

  | Where | Holds |
  |---|---|
  | SSM `/voc/ws/{ws}/credentials/{plugin}` (`lambda/shared/workspace_ids.py`, `lambda/shared/workspace_credentials.py`) | the plugin's keys as one JSON object, bare (`{"api_key": "…", "app_name": "…"}`): every `<plugin>_<key>` the shared secret holds for that plugin but `configs`, its webhook secret (`webhook_secret`) included; at most 4096 bytes (UTF-8) |
  | voc-aggregates `WS#{ws}#PLUGIN_CONFIG` / `{plugin}` (`lambda/shared/plugin_configs.py`, through the workspace layer) | the plugin's `configs` list as stored (its JSON array text): the webscraper's scrapers (`webscraper_configs` in the shared secret), an app plugin's connectors or review pages (`app_reviews_ios_configs`, `app_reviews_android_configs`, `web_reviews_configs`); the deployment's own limits (a scraper 8 KiB, the scrapers 48 KiB in all, any list at most the shared secret's 64 KiB), far under the 400 KB item; `updated_at`, and `updated_by` (the sub of the member who saved it) |
  | voc-aggregates `WS#{ws}#PLUGIN_CONFIG#HISTORY` / `{plugin}#{superseded_at}#{random}` | every list a save replaced: its `configs`, its own `updated_at` and `updated_by`, `superseded_at` and `superseded_by_sub`; kept for good (no `ttl`), exported as `plugin_config_history` |

  Why the lists are rows: no field of any list holds a secret. A scraper holds URLs, selectors, pagination, a
  schedule and labels, and the webscraper builds its own request headers (no header, cookie, token or auth field
  is read from a config); an app connector holds an app id or package name, a display name, a schedule and a Radar
  brand; a review page a public https URL. Every member already reads them whole (`GET /scrapers`,
  `GET /integrations/{source}/apps`). A config that ever needs a credential keeps it under its own key, in the
  plugin's credentials parameter, never in the list.

  The routes still see `<plugin>_<key>` keys (`lambda/shared/workspace_plugin_store.py` maps them), so their merge is
  the one they did on the secret: read, change the plugin's keys, write back; only what changed is written (the
  parameter with `PutParameter`, `Type=SecureString`, the customer-managed key, `Overwrite`, `Tier=Standard`; the
  row in one `TransactWriteItems` with the history row of the list it replaces, the row conditioned on being the one
  the route read (its list and `updated_at`), and before the parameter, so a concurrent save answers 409 and writes nothing of that
  plugin). A value over its limit is refused with a 400
  that says what to shorten, before anything is written.
- **Integrity of the rows.** Any role that may write voc-aggregates could write a config row, so a run trusts no
  row: the web_reviews ingestor re-validates every target on every run (`shared/web_review_targets.py`
  `validated_target`, each page through `checked_url`), and the webscraper fetches every URL through
  `shared/http_utils.py` `fetch_public_url` (the URL policy on each request and each redirect). The app-review
  ingestors take no URL from a row: an app id or package name only reaches the store's own fixed host
  (`apps.apple.com`, `play.google.com`) through its client. Their id format is not re-checked at run time; with
  a fixed host that is a malformed request to the store at worst, not a reach to another host (a gap kept as is).
- **Nothing is made with a workspace.** A parameter or row exists once something is saved in it; one that does not
  exist (`ParameterNotFound`, no item) reads as nothing saved: an empty list or no keys on the routes,
  `WorkspaceCredentialsNotSet` for a run (not counted by the breaker), a webhook delivery refused like a bad
  signature. The 409s a workspace answered while its secret was pending, the pending state and its recovery steps
  are gone.
- **`secret_status`.** The create still writes `secret_status: ready` on `META` and the listing row and answers
  it, but nothing reads it: it no longer reflects a secret, nothing is created asynchronously, nothing gates on
  it, the resolver does not read it, and since 7.12.00 the platform console shows no badge for it. The field
  itself goes later.
- **Readers.** A run in a workspace reads its own plugin's credentials parameter (one GetParameter) and its config
  row (one strongly consistent GetItem), fresh on every run (`plugins/_shared/base_ingestor.py`); a delivery to `/webhooks/{plugin}/{ws}` reads the plugin's credentials
  parameter of the workspace its stored `META` routes it to, kept 60 s per workspace and plugin in the warm container (a missing or
  empty value kept too, a failed read not, at most 256 names: `plugins/_shared/base_webhook.py`); the integrations and
  scrapers APIs read and write the request's resolved workspace's parameter and row of the route's plugin
  (`GET /integrations/status` reads every plugin's: one GetParametersByPath, one Query of the config partition). A
  deployment-wide run, delivery or request still uses the deployment's shared secret (`SECRETS_ARN`), unchanged.
- **IAM** (`voc-datalake/lib/utils/workspace-secrets.ts`), each grant on exactly one workspace id segment (`ws_`
  and 26 `?`, as the DynamoDB `LeadingKeys` conditions use it): an ingestor role may `ssm:GetParameter` only
  `…/credentials/{plugin}` of the plugins it runs (`voc-datalake/lib/stacks/ingestion-roles.ts`), the webhook role
  only `…/credentials/{plugin}` of its webhook plugins (`voc-datalake/lib/stacks/api-stack.ts`), each with a
  `kms:Decrypt` statement conditioned on SSM (`kms:ViaService`) and those names
  (`kms:EncryptionContext:PARAMETER_ARN`), which is no second layer: the same roles hold the key unconditioned
  (below). The integrations and scrapers roles may `ssm:GetParameter` and
  `ssm:PutParameter` on `…/credentials/*` and `ssm:GetParametersByPath` on that path, with `kms:Decrypt` and
  `kms:Encrypt` through SSM for those names; no delete. A standard SecureString is encrypted by SSM calling KMS
  directly, so no `kms:GenerateDataKey` is granted. The config rows need no new grant: every ingestor role already
  reads voc-aggregates and writes its run rows there, and the integrations and scrapers roles already read it and
  PutItem their run rows (table-wide grants that cover `WS#{ws}#PLUGIN_CONFIG` and `#PLUGIN_CONFIG#HISTORY`, a
  conditioned Put inside a transaction included; narrowing them is a separate change).
  Where IAM separates plugins and where it does not: a plugin with a role of its own (a schedule, Bedrock, the
  browser, job notices) reads only its own credentials parameter; the plugins that share `IngestionLambdaRole`
  may each read the others' (the shared role names all their parameters), so between them, as in the shared
  secret, the name the code builds from the plugin's own identity is the boundary. IAM never separates workspaces
  (any one id): only the code, which builds each name from the verified scope, the resolved membership or the
  routed `META`, keeps a run, a request or a delivery to its own workspace (decision 1's soft isolation). The
  workspaces API holds no Secrets Manager or SSM grant at all. These roles also hold the deployment key
  unconditioned for their other resources (queues, tables, the shared secret), so the SSM-only key statements
  state the parameters' reach rather than narrow it.
- **Service limits** (per account and region, shared by every deployment there, prefixed copies included, and by
  anything else that uses Parameter Store; [deployment.md](deployment.md#ssm-parameter-store-limits-a-workspaces-credentials)):
  GetParameter's default throughput is about 40 transactions a second (a quota that can be raised: the paid
  higher-throughput setting); a run makes one GetParameter, a webhook delivery at most one per workspace and plugin
  every 60 s in a warm container. A run's SSM client retries a throttle adaptively, up to 10 attempts
  (`lambda/shared/aws.py` `get_ssm_run_client`); the APIs' and the webhooks' (`get_ssm_client`) make 2 attempts
  (standard mode) with 2 s connect and 4 s read timeouts, so the route with the most SSM calls,
  `PUT /integrations/{source}/credentials` (a read then a write), takes at most 2 × (2 × 6 s + 1 s of backoff) = 26 s
  and answers within API Gateway's 29 s. At most 10,000 standard parameters: one per plugin with saved
  credentials in each workspace, across every deployment in the account and region.
- **Cost**: none per workspace. Standard-tier
  parameters are free at the standard throughput (the higher-throughput setting, if raised, bills per call). The fixed-cost guard
  (`voc-datalake/lib/fixed-cost-guard.test.ts`, `runtimeFixedCostFindings` in
  `voc-datalake/lib/test-support/fixed-cost-rules.ts`) now refuses every grant of `secretsmanager:CreateSecret` or
  `kms:CreateKey` in the app. The workspace delete job (phase 4) will delete a workspace's parameters by path and
  its config rows with its partitions.

## Cost per workspace

- The aim: each Bedrock model call logs `workspace_id`, the model and its tokens (`shared/converse.py` and the
  stream Lambda's model client already centralise those calls). A daily rollup writes `WS#{ws}#COST` rows by date,
  and the platform console shows cost per workspace per day and month (tokens × list price, labelled an estimate).
- **Slice A, the usage line (decision 9).** Every successful Converse or InvokeModel call on a Python
  `bedrock-runtime` client made by `shared/model_config.py` `scope_bedrock_client` (the one through
  `shared/aws.py` `get_bedrock_client`, and the capacity probe's) logs one INFO line `bedrock_usage`
  (`shared/usage_meter.py`, a botocore `after-call` hook beside the inference-scope hook; no caller changed), with
  numbers and ids only, never a prompt or a response: `workspace_id`, `model_id` as sent (after the inference scope),
  `operation`, `service_tier` (the one Bedrock answered with, else the one asked for, else `default`), `input_tokens`,
  `output_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens` and `cache_write_tokens` (`USAGE_FIELDS`). Converse reads the response's
  `usage`, InvokeModel the `x-amzn-bedrock-*-token-count` headers. An answer whose usage cannot be read logs
  `bedrock_usage_unreadable` with the operation and the reason, and the call goes on: logging never raises into the
  caller. The product-document extractor, which cannot import `shared/`, logs the same line for its one Converse
  call (counted `UNATTRIBUTED`; `product_doc_extractor/test/test_usage_lockstep.py` pins the fields).
- **The stream Lambda's line** (since 7.82.00). ConverseStream is not metered on the Python side: no Python code
  streams. The TypeScript stream Lambda writes the same line itself (`lambda/stream/src/lib/usage-meter.ts`, its
  message and fields held equal to `usage_meter.py`'s by a test): one per streamed turn (from the turn's last event,
  operation `ConverseStream`) and one per plain Converse call (the persona consult, the focus group, the plan step;
  `meteredConverse`), with the model id as sent. Its attribution is the request's: the workspace `X-Workspace-Id`
  names (an entered platform admin's included, as `resolve_workspace` attributes it), `DEPLOYMENT` for a run that
  names none, `UNATTRIBUTED` for anything else; a plan step's is the workspace its plan was approved in. Every
  invocation starts `UNATTRIBUTED`; a call counts to the attribution current when it started. The line is written
  raw to stdout, one JSON log event with no runtime prefix, as a Python Lambda's line is. A turn stopped or cut before
  its last event (where its usage comes) is not counted: an undercount, never a guess.
- **Not counted yet.** The image clients of
  `shared/avatar.py` and `shared/image_model_status.py` are not counted either: an image is priced per image, a
  follow-up.
- **The attribution rule.** A call is counted to the workspace its context names, `DEPLOYMENT`, or `UNATTRIBUTED`.
  `resolve_workspace` names the request's workspace (`UNATTRIBUTED` until it resolves), `request_workspace` names
  `DEPLOYMENT` for a request that names none, and `ws_repo.message_scope` names the message's workspace,
  `DEPLOYMENT` for a deployment-wide message and `UNATTRIBUTED` for a refused one. `scope_from_message` only builds
  a scope and attributes nothing. A test pins those call sites. Every invocation starts `UNATTRIBUTED`
  (`measure_invocation_cost` resets it, so a warm container never carries the last workspace on),
  `shared/concurrency.py` runs each task in a copy of its caller's context, and a raw thread pool's tasks never
  attribute (pinned by the test above), so their calls stay `UNATTRIBUTED`, never a wrong workspace. So an area whose handler resolves its workspace another way stays `UNATTRIBUTED` until its owner
  calls `usage_meter.attribute(scope)` at its handler entry: the project, document, research, competitive-watch,
  briefing, memory and agent handlers. The stream Lambda (TypeScript) logs its own line since 7.82.00 (above).
- Slice A logs tokens only and prices nothing. Slice B adds the dated price list, the daily rollup that uses it,
  `GET /platform/costs` and its stack.
- **The price list (slice B).** `shared/model_prices.py` holds one dated `PriceList`: when and where it was read
  (`as_of`, `source_url`: the public Amazon Bedrock pricing page), its basis (region and tier), and USD per million
  tokens by model id as sent, then service tier (input, output, cache read, cache write; `Decimal`). A figure comes
  only from that page or the AWS Price List API that publishes it, never from memory. **Since 7.79.00 three models
  are priced**: Haiku 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 and Opus 5.5, on the standard tier of the global (cross-region) inference
  profiles, read on 2026-11-16 from the Price List API (the engineering agents' cost caps' read,
  `lambda/engineering/runtime/caps.py`, held equal by a test). The other allowlisted models are in `unpriced` (their
  figures not read yet): their lines' `estimated_usd` is `null`, a day's is the priced models' sum and reads
  `complete: false`; the tokens are still counted. An EU deployment's `eu.…` ids have no price either. `complete`
  means every counted call is priced, not that every call is counted (the stream Lambda, the product-document
  extractor and images are not counted yet: above, and the rollup below). To fill it in, a deployer reads the page for their
  region, adds each model id their deployment sends (the `model_id` of the `bedrock_usage` lines: `eu.…` in an EU
  deployment) with its four prices per tier, removes it from `unpriced`, sets `as_of` to the day read and `basis` to
  the region, and redeploys; a test fails for an allowlisted model that is neither priced nor unpriced with a reason.
  A rollup records the `price_as_of` it priced with; a backfill reprices the days it reruns. A model of the
  deployment's own (`-c customModels`, 7.65.00, [deployment.md](deployment.md#bring-your-own-model--c-custommodels))
  is priced from the registry, where its price is required: `price.in` per million input tokens (the cache reads
  and writes too) and `price.out` per million output tokens, on every tier; it is never unpriced.
- **The rollup (slice B).** `voc-cost-rollup` (lambda/jobs/cost_rollup/, VocProcessingStack) runs at 02:15 UTC and
  rolls up yesterday and the day before (a line that arrives late is counted the second time). It finds the
  deployment's `voc-*` Lambda log groups as Administration → System → Logs does, runs one Logs Insights query per 50 groups (four at
  a time), the day's `[00:00, 24:00)` UTC filtered on `@timestamp` to the millisecond (whatever Insights rounds the
  query's own window to), then `filter message="bedrock_usage" and ispresent(model_id) and ispresent(operation) |
  stats …` by `workspace_id`, `model_id` and `service_tier`, and sums the batches. A day is written only when every
  query completed under Insights' 10,000-row cap; otherwise nothing of it is written and the run fails once the other
  days are done (the next tick, or a backfill, reruns it); a day whose read or write AWS refuses is recorded as failed
  with the error's code, and the other days still run. Each row is one whole-item PutItem (no ADD,
  so a rerun overwrites it), kept with no TTL: `WS#{ws}#COST` / `YYYY-MM-DD` through the workspace layer,
  `DEPLOYMENT#COST` and `UNATTRIBUTED#COST`, each with `by_model`, `totals`, `estimated_usd` (the priced models'
  sum, `null` while none is priced), `complete`, `unpriced_models`, `price_as_of`, `rolled_up_at` and `schema` 1. An
  imported model's line (`-c customModels`, `source: "imported"`) is `complete: false`, and so is its row and every
  sum it is in (7.66.00): Custom Model Import bills the Custom Model Units of each active copy by 5-minute windows,
  not the token, so its registry price per token is the operator's own conversion, an estimate. A
  workspace that is not active gets no row: its calls go to `DEPLOYMENT#COST`'s `deleted_workspaces`. A day with no
  metered call has no row. During a delete's 21-minute fence a day can be counted twice: rolled up while the
  workspace was active (its own row) and again after it turned `deleting` (the deployment's `deleted_workspaces`),
  until the delete sweeps the workspace's row; avoiding it would take a read of the existing row (a GetItem grant),
  not worth it for a window that short. **Backfill**: invoke `voc-cost-rollup` asynchronously with
  `{"days": ["YYYY-MM-DD", …]}`, each day over and at most **29 days back** (the log groups keep 30 days; an older day is refused, its lines gone).
  Not read: the product-document extractor's line (its log group is VocCoreStack's, not a `voc-*` one), so those
  calls are not in the rollup yet.
- **The route (slice B).** `GET /platform/costs` on the workspaces API, for platform admins only (`require_admin`,
  `require_platform_admin`, then the live check), `?from=` and `?to=` (UTC days, at most 92, `to` not after today,
  default this month), `?limit=` (1 to 25, default 20) and `?cursor=` (the workspace listing's). One Query per
  workspace on the page, run concurrently; the first page also carries `deployment` (with `deleted_workspaces`) and
  `unattributed`. The answer is labelled an estimate: `estimate: true`, `label` ("Estimate from list prices, not a
  bill"), `currency` USD, `price_as_of`, and per entity `days` and a `total` with its `by_model` (no month sums: the
  92-day range lets the console sum days); a dollar figure is a decimal string, `null` while a part with calls is
  unpriced and nothing priced, `"0.000000"` for a complete day or total with no calls. Each read
  logs `platform_costs_read` with the caller's sub and the range (no AUDIT row: it changes nothing).
- **The console's page (since 7.83.00).** The platform console shows it: Administration → Workspaces → Costs
  (`/admin/workspaces/costs`, platform admins only, like All workspaces; `frontend/src/pages/Settings/PlatformCostsSection.tsx`,
  read by `frontend/src/api/platformCostsApi.ts`). It says first that it is an estimate from list prices, not a bill,
  with the day the price list was read and a link to it. A period of UTC days (the last 30 days by default, this month,
  last month or the last 90 days; never over the route's 92) and one table: the calls outside any workspace (the
  deployment's own, the workspaces no longer active, the calls not attributed; the first page's), then each workspace
  by name, 20 at a time (Show more workspaces). Each row has its calls, input and output tokens and estimated cost:
  "Incomplete" when part of it is not priced, with the models named; "Unpriced" when nothing of it is, never $0. When
  the period spans months, each row's months are summed in the browser from its `days`, the way the server sums a
  range (to the millionth; unknown while no day with calls is priced; incomplete once one day is); the inactive
  workspaces' row has no days, so only its total. A workspace row the page cannot read is left out and counted on the
  page; an answer it cannot read at all, or a first page without its deployment rows, is a load error with Try again,
  never an empty page; a 403 says platform admins only. The dev mock serves the route (`frontend/mock-costs.js`).
- **The hard delete** sweeps a workspace's `WS#{ws}#COST` rows with its settings (they are its data).
- Also counted: reviews ingested, scraper and browser sessions, Comprehend and Translate characters. (A workspace's
  credentials parameters cost nothing a month: decision 19.)
- Scale today (this repo keeps no separate load model, so the notes live here): a workspace's erasure (7.09.00)
  Scanned the whole voc-feedback table, so N workspaces' live erasures were N whole-table Scans, whatever each
  workspace's size. Since 7.96.00 it reads only its own index partitions (`lambda/jobs/retention/item_walk.py`;
  docs/source-policies.md, "Workspace items"): by source id one Query of `gsi4-by-feedback-id`'s
  `WS#{ws}#FEEDBACK#{id}`; by author, CSV row or email the reprocess's day walk below, one Query of
  `WS#{ws}#DATE#{day}` a day from the day before its `created_at` to today. Each Query filters on the index with the
  Scan's own filter (`shared/retention.py` `erase_filter` in the workspace; both indexes project every attribute) and
  returns the keys only; each hit is read again from the table with one strongly consistent GetItem and decided on
  the row as that read returns it: erased only if that row still matches (`erase_matches`); a row the filter rules
  out is never read again. The delete itself is unconditioned, as before (a row changed between that read and its
  delete is still deleted, as with the Scan). Its cost is its own workspace's: a Query is billed on the full size of
  the index items it reads, filtered or not, so keys only saves the bytes returned, not read units; the walk reads
  every review of the workspace once from the index (eventually consistent; one Query a day it has existed) and only
  the hits again from the table (strongly consistent); by source id one Query of the review's id partition and a
  GetItem per hit, whatever the table's size; one live job per workspace still bounds it. Roughly, for 1,000,000
  reviews of ~1 KB (0.5 RCU per 4 KB of eventually consistent index reads, 1 RCU and ~5 ms per consistent GetItem):
  an erasure by author, CSV row or email reads ~125,000 RCU from the index (10,000 pages of 100) plus a GetItem per
  hit, a handful; the former Scan read the same ~125,000 RCU per million rows of the whole table; without the filter
  each review would cost a GetItem too (~1,000,000 RCU, ~83 min), so the filter saves GetItems and time, not index
  read units (CHANGELOG 7.96.00). Its role may Query those
  two indexes only on one workspace id's `#DATE#*` and `#FEEDBACK#*` partitions, for their keys and the filter's
  attributes, `SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES` required (`RetentionReadsPolicy`, nothing added to its inline policy), and read
  a `META`'s `created_at`. Consistency: the Scan read eventually consistent pages too (no `ConsistentRead`); the walk
  adds the index's replication lag, and each hit is decided on the row a strongly consistent read returns; a review
  written or indexed after the walk passed its day, or (by source id) written just before the Query but not yet in
  gsi4, or one whose matched attribute changed into the erased value within the index's lag (the index's filter
  still sees the old value), is the next erasure's, and a review with no `gsi1pk` or `gsi4pk` (none is written so) is on no partition the
  walk reads. No Scan except two fallbacks: a job handed over with a Scan cursor before 7.96.00 and a workspace whose
  `META` names no `created_at` or whose voc-workspaces is not configured (counted, `workspace_item_scans`). The
  deployment's erasures, the erasure by email that
  reaches every workspace included, Scan as before. A workspace's category reprocess
  does the same: it Queries `gsi1-by-date` one day partition `WS#{ws}#DATE#{day}` at a time (pages of 25, oldest
  first, the slice's source and category filters on each Query), from the UTC day before its `META`'s `created_at`
  (a day's margin for the clocks; the processor files a review under the day it processed it, never the review's
  own date, and writes only into an active workspace, so nothing of a workspace is filed earlier) to today, clipped
  to a processed-date window (`lambda/shared/reprocess_slices.py` `walk_days`, each day read by `read_day_page`); a
  review-date window reads every day and each review read is filtered by its own date (`reprocess_slices.py` `admits`,
  which `lambda/jobs/category_reprocess/handler.py` applies). It reads, and is billed for, its own reviews plus one Query and one checkpoint per day it walks
  (a workspace a year old: about 365 Queries), whatever the table's size; its role may Query only that index,
  where the partition is one workspace's day (`WS#{one id}#DATE#*`), and read a `META`'s `created_at` (held to it).
  Two cases still Scan the table through the workspace layer: a job checkpointed with a Scan cursor before this
  change (it finishes as it started) and a workspace whose `META` names no `created_at`. The deployment's own
  reprocess Scans the table as before.
  The cost rollup (slice B) is one invocation a day (no reserved concurrency, which a Workshop Studio account cannot
  spare; two runs at once write the same whole items): two days × ⌈groups / 50⌉ Insights
  queries (about 3 a day at 100 Lambdas), each scanning that day's Lambda logs (billed per GB scanned, so it grows
  with log volume, not with workspaces), then one PutItem per active workspace with calls that day, plus two; no hot
  key (each row its own partition and day, one writer). At 10× the workspaces it is 10× the puts, still one
  invocation; a day over Insights' 10,000 result rows (about 10,000 workspace × model × tier combinations) is not
  written and the run fails, which needs the query split by workspace first. `GET /platform/costs` is one Query per
  workspace on a page (at most 25, each at most 92 rows) plus two, no Scan; it is a platform admin's read.

## Export and delete

The owner accepted the proposal below (decision 17, [settled](#tenet-questions-settled-owner-2026-11-12)): the
controller's own request, with its stated basis and a tombstone per deleted item (TENETS.md, D5). Phase 4 builds it.

1. A workspace admin or a platform admin asks to delete the workspace; the web app offers **Export first** (on by
   default).
2. The export job writes JSONL per entity (feedback, projects and documents, personas, memory, agents and workflows
   (since 7.80.00), conversations (only counted since 7.24.00, below), settings, the Documents Library's items since 7.62.00,
   `library`: [documents-library.md](documents-library.md)) plus the raw objects into one zip under `ws/{ws}/exports/`, with a `manifest.json` that
   says what it holds and what it leaves out (a workspace's credentials are never exported). When it ends, the
   person who started it gets a job notice (when the job could write its outcome: one the timeout killed shows as
   failed an hour later); the export page then offers a download for 7 days (each link, asked for with
   `POST …/export/link` and audited, works 15 minutes; no email is sent). Export objects expire by object tag (10
   days), and an erasure that reaches the workspace deletes them at once (a derived copy, D5). The zip lives under
   the workspace's own prefix, so it goes with the workspace: download it before deleting.
   Since phase 4a (7.17.00, above) the job exports the feedback and the workspace's configuration, history and
   control-plane rows; what is not per workspace yet is listed in the manifest.
   Since 7.20.00 it also exports
   every current object under `ws/{ws}/` except its exports (`raw/`, `silver/`, and any other folder) as
   `objects/<key without ws/{ws}/>`, each its current version when the export's listing reached it (older versions
   are not exported); an object over 3 GiB, too slow to copy in one invocation, deleted meanwhile, or whose key
   cannot be a file name in a zip, is listed in the manifest as not included, with the reason. Already-compressed
   objects are STORED entries with a data descriptor, which some streaming readers reject (central-directory readers
   are fine), and keys that differ only in case or Unicode normalisation can overwrite each other when extracted on
   macOS or Windows (on Windows also keys that differ only by trailing dots or spaces). Since 7.22.00 it also
   exports the workspace's company memory: its company memories, their events and the import rows, each only the
   fields its allowlist names, without any person's hash (a company memory's `conflicts_with` can still hold a
   personal memory's id, and an event's `detail` is exported whole); personal memories and the extraction cursors
