   are their owners' only, so they are counted in the manifest, never exported. Since 7.24.00 the members'
   conversations are not included either: a person's assistant conversations are theirs, and the export goes to
   the workspace's admins. The manifest's `conversations` gives only how many people (current or former members)
   have any, no conversation total; two exports compared show how many people started or stopped keeping
   conversations in between, so whether a given member started using the assistant can be seen that way. A member
   gets no copy of their own conversations from it. Their owners are listed since 7.19.00 (`WS#{ws}#CONVERSANTS`,
   above), so the count reaches every member's partition, a former member's too, by Query on the workspace's own
   partitions, and the delete's conversations entry (step 4) finds them the same way. Since 7.39.00 it also exports
   the workspace's projects (found through its registry: a deleted project is skipped and counted), each with its
   documents, product documents' records, product context, personas, document versions and saved revisions,
   comment threads, jobs and links to participant lists, its company persona library, and its review links (each
   only while its project is live), every entity only the fields it lists or classifies as exported (top-level
   attributes classified; nested maps exported after a secret-name check); email addresses
   (a project's owner and members, a link's audience, a reviewer's comments) and members' ids are exported as the
   workspace's own collaboration data, a link's id (its secret) and a reviewer's email digest never. Since 7.43.00 an
   erasure by email that changes any of those addresses in a workspace's projects deletes that workspace's exports
   with them ([3g-b](#the-project-plan-phase-3), above). A deleted project's rows are
   not in it, but any object left under its `projects/{id}/` prefix still is, under `objects`, until its own delete
   removes it (3h). The workspace's participant lists and people are not in it yet. Since 7.80.00 (3g3-a) it also
   exports the workspace's agents and workflows (`agents_workflows`, found through its registry
   `WS#{ws}#AGENT_REGISTRY`): each agent's settings and pause history, each workflow's current row and every saved
   revision, each row kind only the fields it lists (never an index key, a run lock, a counter, the trigger state or a
   saver's id); no agent run is in a workspace yet.
   **The hard delete's gate, lifted (7.40.00, owner decision 2026-11-15):** from 7.28.00 to 7.37.00 a synth with
   `-c workspaceHardDelete=true` failed (`HARD_DELETE_REFUSED`) until a member could export their own conversations
   and personal memories before the delete. The owner decided the delete does not wait for that per-person export: a
   workspace admin's confirmed delete removes the workspace with everything in it, its members' assistant
   conversations and personal memories included, which the workspace's export does not hold. The web app
   (Workspace settings → Delete workspace, shown only with the flag: `features.workspaceDelete`) lists what is
   deleted, says that it cannot be undone and that members' conversations and personal memories are not in the
   export, offers the export first, and asks for the slug typed exactly and a reason (the `legal_basis`). The flag
   stays off by default. Known gap, unchanged: the cost rollup's day for a workspace being deleted (7.30.00) is
   rolled up twice, and a workspace whose status changes between the two runs has its calls counted both on its own
   `WS#{ws}#COST` row and in the deployment's `deleted_workspaces` for that day (a cost estimate; every model is
   unpriced for now, so only token counts can be doubled).
   Open (owner decision): whether a workspace's export should ever hold its members' conversations (each person's
   own, or all of them for a controller's request). Until the owner decides otherwise only the people are counted.
   Open (TENETS.md D5, older than workspaces): an erasure by email reaches neither the memory items nor the
   assistant conversations, the deployment's or a workspace's. The erasure job matches an email, and these rows are
   keyed by the person's Cognito `sub`, not their email: a conversation by its owner's, a personal memory by its
   owner's (a company memory by no one's); what either says may name anyone, and the job does not search it.
   Reaching them would take the person's `sub` in the erasure (looked up in the directory while the account exists,
   or recorded with the request), or their email on those rows, so the job could find the person's conversations
   (at `USER#{sub}`, and through each workspace's list of conversation owners) and personal memories; and a rule for
   a memory or a message that names the person in its text.
3. The admin confirms by typing the slug. Since 4d-2 (7.28.00) that is `POST /me/workspaces/current/delete`
   `{confirm_slug, legal_basis}` (the workspaces API; `voc-datalake/lambda/shared/workspace_deletes.py`), for the
   workspace's admins: a member admin, or a platform admin who entered (live-checked and audited), the role read from
   the table. The slug must be the workspace's exactly and the basis passes the erasure's rule (at most 300 characters,
   no email, phone number or similar: it is kept for good); 400 otherwise, before anything is written. While an export
   or an erasure of the workspace runs the answer is 409 (its export lock, or its erasure lock, or an erasure holding
   the export lock), and nothing changes. Otherwise the request takes both locks for `delete-{ws}`, then in ONE
   transaction moves `META` and the `WORKSPACES` listing row from `active` to `deleting` (with `deleting_at`), writes
   the job row `WSDELETE` / `{ws}` (conditional on no row or a cancelled one, so it is also the one delete per
   workspace) and two audit rows, `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` (kind `workspace_delete_requested`) and the platform's
   `AUDIT#workspace-deletes` (kind `requested`: who, the basis, the name and slug); then it invokes
   `voc-workspace-delete` with `{workspace_id}` and answers 202 `{delete: {workspace_id, status: 'waiting', phase:
   'fence', requested_at, deleting_at, fence_at, counts}}`. The transaction carries a `ClientRequestToken`, which the job row
   also stores (`request_token`); on any error of it the request reads the job row, and one that is its own (that
   token alone, since two tabs of one admin can share a `requested_at`: it committed, and only the answer was lost or a retry met its own rows) goes on to the invoke, while any other
   failure that still finds no job row on a second read, a second later, frees the locks it took (so a concurrent
   request whose transaction commits meanwhile keeps them; one committing later than that could still find them
   freed). The same transaction puts the workspace's drop counter at 0 (below). A repeat whose transaction meets another request's job answers that job; once the workspace reads `deleting`, every route, this one too, answers its one 404. A failed invoke leaves the
   job `failed` in its fence, which a platform admin cancels or resumes; the requester's 500 says so ("The workspace
   is marked for deletion, but its delete could not start. Ask a platform admin to cancel or resume it."). The workspace's status becomes
   `deleting`: every request is refused and its schedules stop. Since 4d-1 (7.28.00) every writer of a workspace's
   data checks that status before it writes
   (`voc-datalake/lambda/shared/ws_repo.py` `workspace_is_active`), so nothing new arrives while the delete runs:
   - **the scheduled fan-out** skips a workspace that is not active (`IngestFanOutSkipped`), and **an ingestor's run**
     in one is refused before it builds or reads anything (`IngestorWorkspaceNotActive`, `workspace_not_active`);
   - **the feedback processor** drops such a workspace's message before validation: counted
     (`WorkspaceMessagesDropped`), logged with the workspace id and no content, no row written, and the message
     acked, never failed. A failed message would reach the processing DLQ after its retries, and a redrive of that
     queue (the routine fix for a bad batch) would bring the workspace's feedback back after its delete had
     finished;
   - **the aggregator** skips its stream records (`AggregatorInactiveWorkspaceRecordsSkipped`), the delete's own
     `REMOVE`s included: the workspace's counters go with it, so nothing is decremented;
   - **a workspace's category reprocess** (7.28.00) asks at each invocation's start and before each page, and stops
     `cancelled` ("The workspace is being deleted: the reprocess stopped"), with no further rewrite and no
     data_version bump;
   - **the conversation writers** (7.25.00, above) check it before they list an owner or save a conversation: the
     chat routes resolve the workspace fresh on each write, and the stream Lambda reads the status fresh at a run's
     start and again, through a short cache, before each session write
     (`lambda/stream/src/assistant/session/workspace-status.ts`); a `deleting`
     or missing workspace gets the one 404 and nothing is written. So the last such write lands within one
     invocation of the mark (15 minutes for the stream Lambda, 30 s for the chat routes), and the delete's
     conversations entry (step 4) finds every conversation through the list of owners.

   So while a workspace is `deleting`, its messages in flight (queued before the change, or sent by a run that began
   within the 300 s a container may still hold `active`) are dropped once the processor reads `deleting`: acked and
   counted, never stored, so there is no item and no tombstone for them (TENETS.md, D5's gaps). A cancel during the
   fence does not bring them back (and a container that read `deleting` keeps dropping for up to 30 s after the
   cancel). With the hard delete built, the processor also adds each SQS batch's drops for a workspace to that
   workspace's drop counter, `WS#{ws}` / `WSDELETE_DROPS` (`dropped`; `lambda/processor/handler.py`
   `DroppedPerWorkspace`, `lambda/shared/workspace_deletes.py` `count_dropped`), a row apart from the job row: the
   request puts it at 0 in its transaction, a cancel deletes it in its own, and the delete sweeps it with the
   workspace's control rows. One UpdateItem per workspace per batch, `ADD dropped`, only while the counter exists
   (`attribute_exists(pk)`, so none is made for a workspace with no delete, or again after one), no read and nothing
   returned; a failed one is logged and counted (`WorkspaceDropsNotCounted`), never fails the batch. The count is a
   lower bound: counting is best effort, and a batch can land after a cancel or the delete has read the counter. The
   processor's role may UpdateItem `pk`, `sk` and `dropped` alone (`dynamodb:Attributes`) with `dynamodb:ReturnValues`
   NONE, on `WS#{one id}` rows; IAM cannot pin the sort key, so that reaches any row of a workspace's control
   partition, only ever to add a `dropped` number, and the code writes the counter alone; it has no access to the job
   rows (`WSDELETE`). Only in a build of the job. Load: at most one write per processor container per deleting
   workspace per batch, on that workspace's own counter (a busy deleting workspace with 20 containers busy: about 4
   writes a second at a 5-second batching window, far under an item's 1,000); nothing changes for an active workspace
   or a deployment-wide message.

   **Cancel** (4d-2): `POST /platform/workspaces/{ws}/delete/cancel`, a platform admin (live-checked), until the job
   begins deleting: while its row is still in the `fence` phase, which is normally its 21-minute fence, and later
   only for a job that never began (its start's invoke failed, or the flag was turned off; 7.28.00: the row's state
   decides, not `fence_at`, so such a workspace is never left `deleting` for good). One transaction moves the job row
   from `waiting` (or `failed`, still in the fence phase) to `cancelled`,
   `META` and the listing row back to `active`, and writes both audit rows (kinds `workspace_delete_cancelled`,
   `cancelled`); then both locks are freed. The job ends the fence by moving the same row `waiting` → `running`, so of
   a cancel and the fence's end exactly one wins; after it the cancel answers 409, and a repeat answers the cancelled
   job. The cancel's audit rows report at least how many messages were dropped meanwhile (`dropped_messages`: the
   drop counter as the cancel read it, which the same transaction deletes; what a container drops in the 30 s it
   may still read the workspace as not active is not in it, and `WorkspaceMessagesDropped` counts them
   deployment-wide; the delete's `completed` row reports the counter the same way), and the job
   row and the audit row mark the counters for a rebuild (`counters_rebuild`): the operator runs
   `voc-datalake/scripts/retention/rebuild_aggregates.py --workspace ws_… --apply` once the stream has settled (the
   script refuses a window with items processed in the last 24 hours, `--allow-recent` after checking the
   aggregator's IteratorAge). It is not run by the cancel: right after one, records still in the stream would be
   counted twice.

   Not active means any status but `active`, or no `META` at all. The check is one strongly consistent GetItem of
   `WS#{ws}` / `META`, projected to its status; each role is held to `pk`, `sk` and `status` of `WS#*` rows
   (`grantWorkspaceStatusRead`, `dynamodb:Attributes`). Each warm container keeps the answer (`WorkspaceStatusCache`,
   `voc-datalake/lambda/shared/ws_repo.py`): an `active` one for 300 s, a not-active one for 30 s, so a long active
   TTL only lengthens the delete's fence and a short not-active one keeps a cancel's loss window short; a failed read
   is not kept and fails the run or record, which is retried. So a writer stops within 300 s plus the invocation
   already running (at most 15 minutes): the delete job (4d-2) waits 21 minutes after `deleting` (5 + 15 + 1 of
   margin) before it deletes anything. A Run now or a scraper run refused this way ends the `running` row the API
   wrote (`error`, `workspace_not_active`), only if that row is still there; a status that cannot be read is not a
   refusal: the run raises, its row untouched, and Lambda's retry reads the status again. A deployment-wide run,
   message or record reads nothing new.

   **Load.** Each read is one strongly consistent GetItem (one read request unit, the `META` row is far under 4 KB).
   The total, at most: busy workspaces × warm containers ÷ 300 s, for the processor and the aggregator each (a
   container reads a workspace only when one of its records arrives, and then at most once per 300 s; a workspace
   being deleted at most once per 30 s; a cold cache has no single-flight, so the processor's worker threads, 5 at
   once in a batch of up to 10, can each read the same workspace once before the first answer is kept), plus the
   ingestors: one read per run in a workspace, and on each scheduled
   tick one read per enabled workspace, made one after another before the invokes.
   - Worked through for 1,000 busy workspaces, 20 processor containers (its concurrency cap) and, say, 4 aggregator
     containers (one per stream shard; no parallelization factor is set): (20 + 4) × 1,000 ÷ 300 = 80 reads a second
     at the worst, when every container sees every workspace every 300 s; about 207 million reads a month, about $26
     at the on-demand price of $0.125 per million reads. Real traffic is far below it: a processor batch is at most 10
     messages, so a container sees at most 10 workspaces per batch.
   - The tick: per plugin and tick, 1,000 sequential GetItems (a few seconds of the tick's run, before its
     deployment-wide run; a 5-minute schedule is about 290,000 reads a month per plugin, about $0.04), and each
     invoked run reads once more, in its own container.
   - Per partition: each workspace's `WS#{ws}` partition gets at most 24 ÷ 300, under one read every 10 s, from the
     processor and the aggregator, far under a partition's 3,000 a second.
   - 10×, 10,000 busy workspaces: 800 reads a second at the worst (about $260 a month), far under the table's
     default on-demand ceiling of 40,000 read units a second, no quota to raise; per partition unchanged; the tick
     spends tens of seconds reading 10,000 statuses one after another, the first ceiling to watch (the fix is
     batching them, BatchGetItem of 100, or reading only those the tick will invoke).
   - A deployment-wide message, record or run reads nothing new.

   **The watermark directory.** voc-watermarks is keyed on `source` alone, so a workspace's watermarks share no
   partition. Since 4d-1 an ingestor's `set_watermark` in a workspace first puts a directory row in voc-aggregates,
   `WS#{ws}#WATERMARK_KEYS` / `{plugin}#{key}`: the key alone, so an unconditional put each time the watermark is set
   is idempotent (a few writes a run), and it writes no watermark it could not list (counted,
   `WatermarkNotListed`); the delete finds every watermark with one Query. Watermarks written before 4d-1 have
   no row and are not backfilled: workspace runs are new, so this holds for fresh deployments only.

   The repository layer's checked deletes came with the job that calls them (4d-2, step 4): 4d-1 deleted nothing.
4. The delete job, `voc-workspace-delete` (4d-2, 7.28.00; `voc-datalake/lambda/jobs/workspace_delete/`,
   VocProcessingStack), self re-invoking and checkpointed like the export:
   - **Claim and fence.** Each invocation claims its generation on the job row (generation 0 only from `waiting`
     within the hour of its request, generation g only from g - 1 within the hour of its last progress), so a
     duplicate, stale or late delivery changes nothing. Nothing is deleted before the row's `fence_at`
     (`deleting_at` + 21 minutes: every writer's cached `active` has expired, 5 minutes, and the invocation each was
     running has ended, 15, plus a minute): generation 0 sleeps toward it and hands over before its timeout; at the
     fence it moves the row `waiting` → `running` (the race with a cancel, above). From then on each invocation first
     reads `META`: one that reads `active` again stops the job.
   - **The fence rule.** `FENCE`, 21 minutes (`voc-datalake/lambda/shared/workspace_deletes.py`), is at least the
     status cache's active TTL (300 s, `ACTIVE_STATUS_TTL_SECONDS` in `voc-datalake/lambda/shared/ws_repo.py`) plus
     the longer of the two conversation writers' timeouts, `CHAT_STREAM_TIMEOUT` (15 minutes,
     `voc-datalake/lib/stacks/api-assistant-lambdas.ts`) and `CHAT_API_TIMEOUT` (30 s,
     `voc-datalake/lib/stacks/api-engagement-lambdas.ts`), plus a minute for requests in flight and clock skew:
     300 + 900 + 60 = 1,260 s. Each writer's last write lands within its own timeout of the status read it acted
     on, and the writers run side by side, so the longer timeout bounds them (not their sum). Raising either timeout
     or the TTL raises the fence: `voc-datalake/lib/stacks/api-stack-assist-workers.test.ts` reads `FENCE` and the
     TTL from the Python source and fails until the fence, and this paragraph, move with them.
   - **The sweeps**, in order, each a named phase run a page at a time from its checkpoint (the registry `SWEEPS`,
     `sweeps.py`, the shape of the export's entities; `lib/utils/workspace-delete-grants.ts` names the same sweeps with
     their grants, and a test keeps the two equal): the credentials (`GetParametersByPath` of the workspace's
     credentials path with no decryption, then `DeleteParameters`); the feedback (the `gsi1-by-date` day partitions
     from the workspace's first known day to the day after, listing each source partition found, then a strongly
     consistent Query of each listed source partition and of each plugin's; each page lists the counter partitions its
     items touched in the inventory, writes one tombstone per item, `AUDIT#tombstone#<source>` / `{ws}#{id}`, a put of a
     new key paced near 800 a second, then batch-deletes the page); the counters (every listed partition, the fixed
     ones and those the `metric_type` index names); the run rows (`SOURCE_RUN#`, by source and plugin), the breaker's
     `FAILURES#` and `CIRCUIT#`, the `LOGS#` rows, `SCRAPER_RUN#` (by the scraper ids of the config lists and their
     history), the config lists and history, the forms (each form's id kept for its lookup row), the import runs (an
     owner list: the summary partition names each run's detail partition, deleted in full, the list last) and the
     erasure jobs, then the category reprocess jobs and their lock (`WS#{ws}#JOB#category_reprocess`, 7.13.00), and
     what the web_reviews runs learned of each review site (`WS#{ws}#WEB_REVIEWS#METHODS`, 7.44.00); the
     watermarks (through their directory rows); the memory (the company memories, the personal ones
     through the four `MEMSTATUS#personal#*` index partitions, the cursors and imports: `voc-datalake/lambda/shared/
     memory_partitions.py`, the partitions the export reads; one tombstone per memory, `AUDIT#tombstone#memory` /
     `{ws}#{memory id}`, then its `MEMEVT#` events and the memory); the exports (every export marked `erased`, its open
     uploads aborted, its job rows deleted); every version, delete marker and open upload under `ws/{ws}/` of the raw
     bucket, then the same under `ws/{ws}/` of the S3 import bucket (`import_objects`, 7.86.00: the workspace's
     staged files, its grant the raw bucket's three statements on that bucket; a workspace's upload links live 15
     minutes, but one minted just before the delete can still land a file after this sweep and the verify pass:
     the bucket's trigger refuses to ingest it, the workspace being no longer active, and the bucket's expiry removes
     it after `IMPORT_STAGED_EXPIRATION_DAYS`, 90 days); the
     workspace's Radar in voc-radar (`WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#radar`: config, brands and version; `WS#{ws}#RADARSCAN`; every
     partition its owner list `WS#{ws}#RADAR_OWNED` names, releases and brand states, then the list); the workspace's settings
     rows (`WS#{ws}#SETTINGS#{adoption, app_feedback, brand, categories, company_context, dimensions, resolved_problems, sources}`, after the
     counters sweep has listed the closed families from them into the inventory: a later round re-derives only the
     defaults and walks the inventory, which still lists round 1's partitions); each member's own objectives row,
     `WS#{ws}#USERCTX#{sub}`, a former member's too, from the list of who saved some (`WS#{ws}#USERCTX_SUBS`, put by
     the save before the row; the list deleted last); the review links, before the projects so every project's links go
     dark first (7.37.00: every id the workspace's registry `WS#{ws}#DOCSHARE_IDS` lists (registry rows carry no `ttl`), its link or route already expired or not: its
     deployment-wide routing row `DOCSHARE#{id}` released, `release_share_route`, only while it names this workspace,
     never read, a deployment's own link or another workspace's route kept and counted; then the link's own row
     `WS#{ws}#DOCSHARE#{id}`; then its registry row, last; a malformed id counted, `share_ids_malformed`, and its row
     deleted); the projects (7.28.00: every id the workspace's registry
     `WS#{ws}#PROJECT_IDS` lists, its project deleted or not, `iter_registered_project_ids`; for each id, first, since
     7.37.00, each series its partition lists (`COMMENT_SERIES#{series}`) and that series' comment partition
     `WS#{ws}#DCOMMENTS#{id}#{series}` in full, its threads and its review link's pointer; then every
     partition `project_partitions` names, each in its table, its rows and its document versions in voc-projects, its
     jobs in voc-jobs and its list links in voc-participants, deleted through `WorkspaceTable.batch_delete`; then its
     claim `PROJECT_ID#{id}` released, `release_project_claim`, only while it names this workspace, a claim another
     workspace holds kept and counted; then its registry row, last, so a delete stopped anywhere resumes from the
     registry; an id the registry does not list is never attempted, and a registry row whose id is not shaped like
     a project id is counted, `project_ids_malformed`, and deleted, nothing else touched; after the registry, since
     7.31.00, the workspace's company persona library, `WS#{ws}#PROJECT#company_personas`, the same partitions,
     which no registry lists and no claim names); the people and lists
     (7.31.00: an owner list over the workspace's index `WS#{ws}#PARTICIPANTS` in voc-participants, one row per list,
     person and address guard, put before a list's first row and in the same transaction as a person and its
     guard; each named
     `WS#{ws}#LIST#…`, `WS#{ws}#PERSON#…` or `WS#{ws}#EMAIL#…` partition deleted in full through
     `WorkspaceTable.batch_delete`, every read of keys alone, then the index last; a project's links to them went with
     its partition, above); the Documents Library (7.62.00: an owner list over `WS#{ws}#LIBRARY` in voc-projects, each
     `ITEM#` row naming its passages' `WS#{ws}#LIBRARY_CHUNKS#{item}`, deleted in full, every read of keys alone, then
     the library partition itself, its items, slug guards and count, last; its files are under `ws/{ws}/library/`,
     which the objects sweep takes; [documents-library.md](documents-library.md)); the agents and workflows (7.80.00:
     an owner list over the registry `WS#{ws}#AGENT_REGISTRY` in voc-agents, one row per agent and workflow put in the
     transaction of its first row, each naming `WS#{ws}#AGENT#…`, `WS#{ws}#WORKFLOW#…` or, for a run's journal,
     `WS#{ws}#RUN#…`, deleted in full, every read of keys alone, then the registry last); the members' conversations
     (7.28.00: an owner list over `WS#{ws}#CONVERSANTS` in voc-conversations, one row per person who ever kept a
     conversation in the workspace, a former member too; for each, the whole `WS#{ws}#USER#{sub}`, its `CONV#` and
     `RUNCANCEL#` rows, through `WorkspaceTable.batch_delete`, every read of keys alone; the list last, so a delete
     stopped anywhere resumes from it; a person's own `USER#{sub}` outside the workspace is never named. Only the
     partitions the list names are reached: conversations saved in a workspace before 7.19.00, when the list began,
     are found only if `scripts/conversations/backfill_conversants.py` ran (Upgrading, above); under decision 21
     (fresh deployments only) there are none. The verify pass checks the list is gone, and cannot see a partition
     the list never named); the
     control-plane rows (the rows of `WS#{ws}` but `META`, each member's `USER#` row, each form's `FORM#` lookup and the
     listing row only where they name this workspace, each plugin's `PLUGIN#` row, then `META`, only while it reads
     `deleting`; `SLUG#{slug}` is marked `deleted_at` and kept, so the slug is never reused). An inventory partition,
     `WS#{ws}#DELETE#PARTITIONS`, holds what a sweep found for a later one.
   - **Verify.** Every listed partition, the fixed ones, the owner lists (the conversations' included), the versions
     and uploads, the credentials path, the project and review link registries and `WS#{ws}` must hold nothing: anything found sends the job back to the first sweep once (every sweep is idempotent), then
     fails it, naming the families (never a key). Then the inventory goes, the platform's `completed` row is written
     (counts, what is kept; no content, no member's sub), and the job ends `completed`.
   - **Kept:** `AUDIT#WS#{ws}`, the tombstones, `AUDIT#workspace-deletes`, the job row, the slug guard; until they
     expire, the idempotency claims (no content, two days), backups and logs, and the counters of a category the
     deployment configured before 7.08.00 (when a workspace's items were enriched with the deployment's categories)
     whose items were all erased before the delete, until the counters' 90-day TTL (TENETS.md D5's gaps). Reserved: MCP
     tokens name no workspace until phase 3 (an empty entry says so); the share grants it gave or used come with sharing. A
     project's rows outside the partitions keyed by its id are not reached yet either: its comment threads
     (`DCOMMENTS#…`, deployment-only until 3e) and its review links (`DOCSHARE#…`). Copies other workspaces already imported stay theirs (TENETS.md, D8,
     settled above).
   - **Failure and resume.** Any failure ends the job `failed` with its error's type and its checkpoint on the row;
     `POST /platform/workspaces/{ws}/delete/resume` (a platform admin, audited `resumed`) invokes a failed job, or
     one that has not moved for an hour, again from its checkpoint at its next generation; a job still moving or
     finished answers 409. At most 600 generations, two invocations at once in the deployment (reserved concurrency),
     no async retry, events older than 40 minutes dropped.
   - **IAM** (`lib/stacks/workspace-delete-worker.ts`): every key held to one workspace id (`ws_` and 26 `?`):
     BatchWriteItem only on `WS#{id}#*` (voc-feedback's `WS#{id}#SOURCE#*`; IAM cannot tell a batch's deletes from its
     puts, and `dynamodb:Attributes` held to `pk` and `sk` would still pass a key-only put, so the code alone keeps
     the batches to deletes, but for the inventory's key-only puts in voc-aggregates), Query of its partitions and index
     partitions, PutItem only on `AUDIT#tombstone#*` (voc-aggregates) and `AUDIT#workspace-deletes` (voc-workspaces;
     the audit-row guard names it the writer of those two only), DeleteItem on voc-workspaces' `WS#{id}`, `USER#*`,
     `FORM#*`, `PLUGIN#*` and `WORKSPACES` (the code builds each key or deletes only a row naming the workspace),
     UpdateItem of `deleted_at` on `SLUG#*` and of its own job row; S3 `ListBucketVersions` with the `s3:prefix`
     `ws/{id}/*`, `DeleteObjectVersion` and `AbortMultipartUpload` under it, `ListBucketMultipartUploads` on the
     bucket (that action takes no `s3:prefix` condition, so the role could list the key and upload id of every open
     upload in the bucket, other workspaces' and the deployment's included, but no object's data; the code lists
     `ws/{ws}/` only; accepted); for the conversations (7.28.00), Query and BatchWriteItem on
     `WS#{id}#CONVERSANTS` and `WS#{id}#USER#*` in voc-conversations, each Query for the keys alone
     (`dynamodb:Attributes` `pk` and `sk`, which must be named, and `dynamodb:Select` `SPECIFIC_ATTRIBUTES`: no
     message, title or `first_at` is read); SSM `GetParametersByPath` and `DeleteParameters` on the workspace's credentials path, no decrypt; for
     the projects (7.28.00), Query and BatchWriteItem on `WS#{id}#PROJECT#*`, `WS#{id}#DOCUMENT_VERSIONS#PROJECT#*`,
     `WS#{id}#DCOMMENTS#*` (7.37.00) and `WS#{id}#PROJECT_IDS` in voc-projects and on `WS#{id}#PROJECT#*` in voc-jobs
     and voc-participants, and GetItem and DeleteItem on `PROJECT_ID#*` in voc-projects: a claim's key names no
     workspace, so IAM reaches every claim and cannot tie one to the workspace being deleted; the code does
     (`release_project_claim`'s condition that the claim names this workspace, and the ids come only from that
     workspace's own registry); for the review links (7.37.00), Query and BatchWriteItem on `WS#{id}#DOCSHARE_IDS`,
     BatchWriteItem alone on `WS#{id}#DOCSHARE#*` in voc-projects, and DeleteItem alone on `DOCSHARE#*`, which the deployment's own links
     share, so the code's condition (`release_share_route`: the row names this workspace) and the ids from the
     workspace's own registry keep it to that workspace's routes; for the people and
     lists (7.31.00), Query and BatchWriteItem on `WS#{id}#PARTICIPANTS`, `WS#{id}#LIST#*`, `WS#{id}#PERSON#*` and
     `WS#{id}#EMAIL#*` in voc-participants, each Query for the keys alone (as the conversations'); KMS
     for the tables; its own invoke. No Scan anywhere. The workspaces API gains the erasure lock's Put, Update and
     Query, an UpdateItem of `status` and `deleting_at` only on `WS#*` and `WORKSPACES`, Put and Update of `WSDELETE`,
     Put of `AUDIT#workspace-deletes`, and the job's invoke (`lib/stacks/api-workspace-delete.ts`).
   - **Load** (S1). A delete is rare and its own: one invocation at a time per workspace, two in the deployment. Its
     reads are Queries of the deleted workspace's own partitions, one per day of its feedback span and one per listed
     partition, plus a page of 1,000 object versions per DeleteObjects; its writes are batch deletes of 25 and one
     tombstone put per item and per memory, paced near 800 a second into `AUDIT#tombstone#<source>`, a partition
     voc-retention's tombstones share (under its 1,000 writes a second; a delete beside a large erasure of the same
     source may be throttled, and retried). About $22-44 per 5 million items on demand. No hot key of another
     workspace is touched; the control plane's `WORKSPACES` and `WSDELETE` partitions take a handful of writes per
     delete (and the processor's drop counts while it is deleting, step 3). 10×: ten workspaces deleting queue behind the two invocations, each waiting in Lambda's throttling
     retries (40 minutes), then resumed by a platform admin. The projects entry adds one Query of the registry per
     100 ids and, per project, one Query per partition page (four partitions), the batch deletes of its rows, one
     DeleteItem of its claim (a GetItem more when the claim is not released: none, or another workspace's) and one
     delete of its registry row; each claim is its own key, spread over every project id, and no other workspace's
     partition is read. The conversations entry adds one Query of the owner list per 100 owners and, per owner, one
     Query per 100 rows of their partition and its batch deletes, keys only; each owner's partition is their own. The
     people and lists entry is shaped the same: one Query of the index per 100 rows and, per list, person or guard,
     one Query per 100 rows of its partition (a list of 2,000 members: 20) and their batch deletes, keys only.
5. With `protectData` the tables stay protected; this is item-level deletion, so it still works.

## Trial workspaces

Since 7.71.00, at the end of a workshop a platform admin creates a **trial workspace** for the customer in one step
(Administration → Workspaces → **Create a trial**, or `voc-datalake/scripts/trial-pack.mjs` headless). It runs for 14,
30 or 60 days (30 by default) and then **pauses**. It never deletes itself: it stays paused until a platform admin
extends or converts it, its admins export it, or the data controller asks for its hard delete (the existing request,
behind `-c workspaceHardDelete=true`: [Export and delete](#export-and-delete)). Only platform admins create, extend and
convert trials.

**Create a trial** asks for a name, an address (slug) and a length, the people to add (each with a role and,
optionally, the role playbook to suggest to them), and a starter pack: forms from the workshop feedback and the in-app
feedback templates (created Off) and sample categories (`voc-datalake/schemas/trial-pack.json`). `POST
/platform/workspaces` with `trial: {days, pack}` makes the workspace in its usual transaction, with the same `trial`
map on `META` and the listing row and a `trial_created` audit row; nothing is scheduled. The starter
pack is then applied as ordinary creates through the existing routes, with the admin's own credentials, in the new
workspace (the SPA names it in `X-Workspace-Id`), each audited by its own route: `POST /me/workspaces/current/members`
per person (someone with no account gets one, and Cognito emails them a temporary password), `POST /feedback-forms`
per form and `PUT /settings/categories`. A step that fails is reported and the others still run. Then the trial's
import wizard (`/scrapers/import`, [import-wizard.md](import-wizard.md)) opens inside it, for its feedback and the
workshop's survey.

**The trial map** (`lambda/shared/trial_state.py`, `lambda/shared/trials.py`): `rev` (one up per write, each write
conditional on the one it read), `days`, `created_at`, `created_by`, `expires_at`, `pack` (what the starter pack asked
for), `extended` (`[{at, by, days, expires_at, resumed?}]`, at most 50), and once it happened `paused_at` and `paused`
(`{plugins, forms}`: what the pause turned off), or `converted_at` and `converted_by`. Every member reads its summary on
`GET /me/workspaces/current` (`trial`: when it ends, whether and since when it is paused, how many plugins and forms the
pause turned off, whether it was converted; never who did what), and the app shows it as a banner above every page.

**The end.** One EventBridge rule runs `voc-trial-expiry` (VocProcessingStack) every hour, so **a trial pauses within
the hour after its `expires_at`**. Each tick reads the platform listing once (one Query of the `WORKSPACES` partition,
whose rows carry each trial's map, written in the same transaction as `META`'s) and pauses every trial that is not
converted, whose `expires_at` has passed and that is not paused yet; a trial paused within the last day is visited
again, so a pause a failed attempt left half done is finished, and one trial that fails, whatever the error, is logged,
counted and left for the next tick without stopping the others. The pause reads the trial from `META` again (the stored `expires_at` decides): a trial
extended meanwhile, converted, being resumed, being deleted or no trial at all is left alone. A trial whose `expires_at` does not
parse is never paused: the tick skips it and logs a warning, and the pause refuses it too, until a platform admin
extends it. Creating, extending or converting a trial schedules nothing: the job's role may Query the listing
partition, and the workspaces API holds no scheduler or `iam:PassRole` action. A due trial is paused
(`lambda/shared/trial_pause.py`):

1. `paused_at` first: from then on a workspace admin cannot turn a plugin on (`PUT /me/workspaces/current/plugins/{id}`
   answers 409 `trial_paused`; its transaction's check of `META` needs `paused_at` absent, so a pause that lands
   meanwhile still wins) nor put a form live (`POST` or `PUT /feedback-forms…` with `enabled: true` answers 409
   `trial_paused`);
2. what is on now (the plugins the workspace enabled, its live forms), joined to anything an earlier attempt recorded,
   stored as `paused`;
3. a `trial_paused` audit row (actor `trial-expiry`, kind `schedule`, the lists);
4. each plugin turned off by the existing audited disable (its own `plugin_disabled` row, reason `trial_paused`) and
   each live form set `enabled: false` (its save counter one up, `updated_by` `trial-expiry`), each turn-off one
   transaction with a check of `META`: the workspace still active and the trial still at the `rev` step 2 saved
   (7.71.04, `lambda/shared/trial_state.py` `unchanged_check`).

No form is put live once step 1 committed (the publish's own transaction checks `paused_at`), so the live forms read in
step 2 are all there are (7.71.01 dropped a second read that could never find one).

**No new workspace status.** A paused trial is `active`, so every reader, writer, the export and the hard delete treat
it as any workspace: reads, edits, chat, imports and the export keep working; what stops is its scheduled plugins and
its live forms. **Nothing is deleted** by an expiry, an extension or a conversion: the job's only delete is the plugin
disable's own `PLUGIN#{plugin}` / `WS#{ws}` row (a present row means enabled; it holds no data), pinned by
`voc-datalake/lib/stacks/trial-expiry.test.ts` (no other delete action of any kind) and
`lambda/shared/test/test_trial_pause.py` (every row of the workspace but those stays).

**Prototype pins and the Validate card keep working.** A prototype's pins and its "Tell us how it went" card (7.68.00,
[project-workspace.md → Validate](project-workspace.md#validate)) are prototype review, not a live public form of the
workspace, so a pause leaves them alone. Projects are still the deployment's ([Phases](#phases), phase 3): a
prototype's pin form (`pf_…`) is in the deployment's `FEEDBACK_FORM` partition, never a trial's own
`WS#{ws}#FEEDBACK_FORM`, so the pause, which reads only the trial's forms, never turns a pin form or its card off and
never records one, and `PUT /projects/{id}/validation/form` is a deployment project's write, which no trial's pause
refuses (pinned by `lambda/shared/test/test_trial_pause.py`). When projects move into workspaces, a workspace
project's card is a live form of that workspace and joins the pause and its `trial_paused` refusal in the same change.

**Extend and convert** (`POST /platform/workspaces/{ws}/trial/extend` `{days}` and `…/convert`, platform admins only,
live-checked; the console's row of a trial): each writes its audit row first (`trial_extended`, `trial_converted`),
marks a paused trial `resuming_at` (so the hourly pause, which leaves a trial being resumed alone, cannot turn off
what the resume turns on before the route's own write), turns its recorded plugins and forms back on (each plugin the
deployment still offers, each form still there, still off and still publishable: a form edited while paused so that
its theme is unreadable or a text shows a placeholder as written stays off), then writes the map without the pause and
the mark. The answer's `trial.resumed` says what came back and names each form left off in `skipped` (`{form_id, name,
code}`, the publish rule's code; also kept in the extension's history), and the console's row shows them with why, for
their editors to fix and publish (7.71.01). An extension counts from the stored end, or from now once it ended; the
hourly tick reads the new end. A conversion ends the trial for good (the tick passes a converted trial by). A failure
after the audit row (a 409 `trial_changed`, the trial written meanwhile, or an error of the resume) leaves it marked,
(partly) resumed and still paused, and the same extension or conversion again finishes it (an extension counted from
the same stored end); the console's row says so ("Extend or Convert again to finish", `resume_incomplete` on the
listing's trial; a route's own answer never carries it, since it is sent once that route's write removed the mark,
7.71.03). The mark is the request's own time and holds the hourly pause off for 15 minutes only (an extension or a
conversion is one API request): a mark older than that is a resume that failed, so the trial is due again (its end has
passed: a failed extension never wrote its new one), even past the day a pause is revisited, and the tick turns off
what that resume turned on and removes the mark (7.71.02). The pause's own write of what it turns off is conditional on
the trial it read and comes before any turn-off, so an extension that marked the trial after that read makes the pause
fail (409, counted failed by the tick) and turn nothing off (7.71.02); and each turn-off checks in its own
transaction that the trial is still at the `rev` of that write, so an extension that marks it while the pause is
turning things off refuses the next turn-off and stops the pause there (409), and nothing the extension turned on is
undone (7.71.04; 7.71.03 read the `rev` again before each turn-off, one read each, and a mark that landed between that
read and the turn-off was not seen). A refused plugin's `plugin_disabled` audit row is written all the same, before its
transaction, as every plugin change's is.

**Fixed cost: about none** (tenet 7): 720 ticks a month, each one Query of the listing partition (a few rows per
workspace) and nothing more while no trial is due.

## Tenet questions, settled (owner, 2026-11-12)

The two points D5 left open are decided, and TENETS.md says so (since 7.05.00):

- **Workspace delete (phase 4): accepted as proposed.** The delete is the data controller's own request under its
  agreement with the deployment, a retention limit the deployment is bound to: it states its basis, is approved by
  typing the workspace's slug, writes an audit row, leaves a tombstone per deleted item like `voc-retention`'s, and
  offers the export first (TENETS.md, D5).
- **Imports and erasure (phase 5): no, an erasure in A does not reach B's copies.** A new data lake tenet, D8: each
  workspace is its own boundary. Sharing data with another workspace copies it there; from then on the copy belongs
  to the workspace that imported it, and nothing done in the source (an edit, an erasure, deleting the source
  workspace) reaches it. Each workspace's admins answer for the data it holds. The share step says so before
  anything is copied: "it will be copied to <workspace>, which then owns its copy". `imported_from` stays as lineage
  (D4), not as a path for the source's changes.

Also decided: per-workspace credentials are SSM SecureString parameters (decision 19, tenet 7; since 7.12.00),
workspace audit rows keep a partition key starting `AUDIT#` (decision 20, as built: `AUDIT#WS#{ws}`), and the
7.00.00 index re-key and decision 19's store are for fresh deployments only (decision 21, their Upgrade notes).

Also held to the tenets as built: the per-workspace secret of 6.09.00, a fixed cost per workspace, is gone with
decision 19 (Credentials above: one free standard-tier SSM parameter per plugin namespace), and the fixed-cost guard
now refuses any runtime create of a key or a secret (`runtimeFixedCostFindings`); and `voc-workspaces` holds primary data (members, audit) beside
invites, so 6.09.00, the first change that writes workspaces and members, pins that only `INVITE#` rows carry `ttl`
(no writer of the table names the attribute, `lambda/shared/workspace_members.py` included since 6.10.00:
`voc-datalake/lambda/shared/test/test_platform_workspaces.py`), with one exception (7.92.00; for the owner to
agree): `WS#{ws}` / `NOTICE#{sub}`, the member notice's 24-hour guard (`lambda/shared/member_notice.py`, the one other
module that names `ttl`, and only on its `NOTICE#` rows: the same test pins it), holds no address or name, only
`sent_at` and `ttl`, because a guard row without an end would keep a removed member's sub for good. The
audit rows are protected: they are keyed `AUDIT#WS#{ws}` / `{iso}#{random}`, and the audit-row guard
(`voc-datalake/lib/utils/audit-row-guard.ts`) covers `voc-workspaces` (since 6.07.00), so no role can change or
remove one and only its named writers can put one: `voc-workspaces-api`, and since 6.11.00 the users API, limited to
`AUDIT#WS#*` (a deleted user's membership removals); every other role that comes to write the table
(`voc-retention`, since 6.11.00) is denied putting an `AUDIT#` row. That role's PutItem is scoped (`dynamodb:LeadingKeys`) to the partitions a
creation, an add and an audit write (`AUDIT#WS#*`, `SLUG#*`, `USER#*`, `WORKSPACES`, `WS#*`), every put conditional
on a new key, and its UpdateItem (until decision 19 also `secret_status` of `META` and the listing row, now written
whole at creation) to `WS#*` and `USER#*` for `role` (a member's role change, since 6.10.00). Its one DeleteItem (since 6.10.00) is a
member's removal, scoped to `WS#*` and `USER#*`: membership rows are access configuration, not customer data (D5
covers feedback and raw items), each removal is audited first, and `voc-datalake/lib/customer-data-deletes.test.ts`
names the grant with that reason. IAM pins the partition key only, so the grant also reaches a workspace's `META`;
the code deletes `MEMBER#` and `USER#` keys only (its tests pin the keys). The users API holds the same DeleteItem
since 6.11.00, for a deleted user's memberships, named in the same test with its reason. A phase that adds another
audit writer names it in the guard.

## Phases

1. **Control plane**: the `voc-workspaces` table, the `platform-admins` group, `/me/workspaces`, the picker, `/workspace/{slug}`
   routing, the workspace middleware and repository layer, invites, the platform console (list and create
   workspaces, enter one, members; built in 6.13.00 with the members page); a workspace's credentials store (decision 19, 7.12.00).
2. **Data path**: ingestion → S3 → SQS → processor → feedback → aggregator → metrics and feedback read APIs, all keyed
   by workspace; the scheduled tick's fan-out; per-workspace credentials; the public routes (forms, webhooks, email,
   voting). Built so far (7.00.00 to 7.04.00): the repository layer, ingestion and the processor in a workspace,
   erasure by email and retention windows (7.03.00), the aggregator, the metrics and feedback read APIs and the
   operator routes (7.04.00), so inside a workspace the web app's feedback, dashboards and Sources are that
   workspace's; feedback forms and webhooks (7.06.00); and the configuration cut-over (7.08.00): the categories,
   dimensions and source profiles a workspace's feedback is processed, ingested and read with are the workspace's own,
   edited by its admins; an erasure by author, source id or CSV row in a named workspace (7.09.00) and a
   workspace's own category reprocess (7.13.00). **The data path is done (7.14.00)**: the ingestion hold is lifted,
   so a workspace's admins enable its plugins, the scheduled tick fans out to the workspaces that enabled each, Run
   now and a scraper's run run there (for an enabled plugin only), and a workspace form's submissions and a workspace
   webhook's deliveries are ingested into it; each item lands under `WS#{ws}#…`, its raw object under
   `ws/{ws}/raw/`, and is aggregated, read, erased and replayed in its workspace. **Still deployment-wide after
   7.14.00**, each a later phase's: of phase 2's entry points, email intake (the inbox, its dedupe state and caps)
   and voting sessions (`VOTE#{id}`, which score a Prioritization row), the S3 import API, its bucket and its trigger
   until 7.86.00 (since then a workspace's admins stage, list, delete and reprocess its own files under `ws/{ws}/` of
   the import bucket, and the bucket's trigger ingests an upload there in that workspace: [data-lake-structure.md](data-lake-structure.md#s3-import-bucket)),
   and the manual import until 7.67.00 (since then it imports into the
   workspace `X-Workspace-Id` names, its admins only, with its own source profiles, raw copies under `ws/{ws}/raw/`,
   messages stamped with its `workspace_id` and runs in `WS#{ws}#IMPORT_RUN`: [import-wizard.md](import-wizard.md));
   and phase 3 (below): Radar's brand claims in a workspace (a workspace's scrapers, connectors, uploads and source
   profiles may not name a Radar brand yet, and the processor refuses a workspace message that carries one; its routes
   and scans are per workspace since 7.73.00), projects and their
   Step Functions, the assistant and its stream Lambda, memory, agents, workflows and plans, the briefing and
   participants' asks (the read caching's `config_version` is per workspace since 7.78.00). The Data Explorer is per
   workspace since 7.90.00. Browser-saved state still not keyed by workspace (the three persisted stores are since 6.15.00),
   each with its reason: the Memory page's list of the imports this browser started (`voc-memory-imports`), which follows the
   memory API when phase 3 keys it; the onboarding cache (`voc-onboarding:{sub}`), a cache of a per-person
   setting the server holds; and, deployment-wide by design, the session (`voc-auth`), the language and theme
   (decision 7), the assistant launcher's position (`voc-assistant-bubble`, per person), the passkey offer, the
   public boards' and voting pages' own keys, and the workspace records themselves (`voc-last-workspace`,
   `voc-refused-workspaces`, `voc-platform-entries`). When phase 4's delete frees a slug, a browser that saved
   state under it would show it to a later workspace with that slug: phase 4 keeps deleted slugs reserved, or
   moves these keys to the workspace id.
3. **Everything else**: settings, categories, dimensions, sources, Radar, projects and their Step Functions, the
   assistant and its stream Lambda, memory, agents, workflows and plans, MCP tokens, the read-caching version
   counters ([Read Caching](caching.md)) per workspace, and the logs (per workspace since 7.75.00; the alerts and
   system diagnostics stay the deployment's: they watch its own Lambdas).
4. **Lifecycle and cost**: export, delete, cost metering and the platform cost page. Open since a workspace ingests
   (7.14.00): a workspace's own whole-source erasure (its items of one source, its counters, a lock of its own and
   `ws/{ws}/raw/<source>/`); until then a whole-source erasure is the deployment's request only.
5. **Later: the workspace import plugin** (below).

Guards added in phase 1 and kept green from then on: the raw-access static test, a cross-workspace test suite (a
member of workspace A is refused every id of workspace B exactly as an id that exists nowhere, route by route), and an
end-to-end check with two workspaces against the local mock. All three exist since 6.14.00 (the table above): the raw-access
guard (since 7.00.00 `lambda/shared/test/test_ws_raw_access.py`, which replaced 6.14.00's `audit: ws_raw_access`), the cross-workspace suite (since 6.18.00 `lambda/api/test/test_cross_workspace_routes.py`; phase 2
moves each data route out of its pending list) and `frontend/e2e/tests/cross-workspace.spec.ts`.

The cross-workspace suite (`voc-datalake/lambda/api/test/test_cross_workspace_routes.py`; its docstring has the
rules) names every route of the API, reads and writes, by file and route, in exactly one table: workspace-scoped (it
resolves `X-Workspace-Id` before it can return or write, found structurally, with a two-workspace case in
`cross_workspace_worlds.py`), outside a workspace by design (each with its reason), or pending
(`cross_workspace_pending.py`, by the phase that moves it, with `POST /mcp/global` too). For each workspace-scoped
route it generates the checks over two seeded workspaces: B's workspace in the header, and B's ids (path, body or
query) under A's header, are refused exactly as ones that exist nowhere, changing nothing, while the same request
works inside A. A new route, or a pending one that starts resolving the workspace, fails until it is classified, so
the pending lists only shrink. A platform admin entering B (decision 2, audited) is the one sanctioned way in
(`shared/test/test_workspace.py`). Beside the per-file detector, a scan of every module of `lambda/` follows
`resolve_workspace` through helpers in other modules, imports, aliases, module attributes and decorators: a route it
reaches must be workspace-scoped, so a helper in another module cannot hide a resolution from the tables. Naming B,
or a malformed id, answers the one 404 with no Cognito call at all, for A's viewer, a caller in no workspace and a
member of the deployment's `admins` only too.

## Workspace import plugin (later)

Workspaces stay fully separate; sharing is an import, like any other source.

- **Approval**: A's admin approves B for one of A's sources (`WS#{A}` / `SHARE#{source}#{B}`, audited). B's admin then
  adds a `workspace_import` connector pointing at it.
- **Modes**, per connector: `raw` copies A's raw objects into B's raw prefix and runs them through B's own pipeline
  (B's categories, models and settings; B pays the enrichment); `categorised` copies A's processed items as they are
  into B's partition, and nothing is re-run.
- **Fetch**: a pull with a watermark per connector over A's date index for that source, plus a backfill window on the
  first run. This plugin is the one sanctioned reader of another workspace's data, through a dedicated repository
  call that re-checks the approval on every run.
- **After the copy the data is B's**: stored as B's own items (`source = ws_<A-slug>_<source>`,
  `imported_from: {ws, source, id}`) and raw objects. Revoking the approval only stops future imports. A's edits,
  erasures, retention deletes and workspace deletion do not reach B's copies (TENETS.md, D8, decided by the owner:
  [settled](#tenet-questions-settled-owner-2026-11-12)); B's admins answer for what B holds, and the share step A's
  admin sees says "it will be copied to <workspace>, which then owns its copy". Copied items are read-only in B (no category or dimension edits); B can delete them and
  disconnect the connector.
